# Audit-trigger surfaces (binding scripture)

Moved VERBATIM from `CLAUDE.md` on 2026-08-05 (user-directed) so the
always-loaded operating doc stays small. Nothing in the table below was
rewritten in the move; `CLAUDE.md` keeps the binding RULE, the reviewer-agent
protocol, and a one-line index pointing here.

Any change to a surface below MUST spawn a focused adversarial soundness audit
before merge -- not as ceremony; each round has historically surfaced bugs the
test suite didn't catch (see `CLAUDE.md` "Audit-triggering changes" for the
rule, the reviewer agent, and the round protocol). `ARCHITECTURE.md` section
25.4 carries a parallel expansion of many rows; where a row below names the
ARCH row authoritative, it is. Unifying the two expansions is task #152 -- a
careful both-directions census, not a quick dedup.

The table is CUMULATIVE: a chunk that adds an audit-bearing surface appends its
full row HERE and a one-line entry to the `CLAUDE.md` index in the same PR.

| Surface | Representative files | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Exception entry + EL0-entry trampolines | `arch/arm64/start.S`, `arch/arm64/exception.c`, `arch/arm64/vectors.S`, `arch/arm64/userland.S` (`userland_enter`), `arch/arm64/context.S` (`thread_user_trampoline`) | Every syscall / IRQ / fault path. Privilege boundary. **#713 eret-window invariant**: any hand-rolled `eret`-to-EL0 path that sets `ELR_EL1` MUST mask DAIF (`msr daifset, #0xf`) across the ELR-set..`eret` window -- else an IRQ in the window overwrites `ELR_EL1` with the interrupted kernel PC and the `eret` lands EL0 at a kernel VA (rare, IRQ-timing-dependent, the #713 root cause). The `eret`'s `SPSR_EL1=0` re-enables IRQs at EL0 atomically. The kernel `thread_trampoline` (EL1, no `eret`) is exempt -- it legitimately unmasks. `KERNEL_EXIT` is exempt -- always reached IRQ-masked. See `docs/reference/08-exception.md` "eret-window IRQ race (P6 #713)". |
| Halls of Extinction crash dump | `arch/arm64/halls.c`, `arch/arm64/halls.h`, `arch/arm64/exception.c` (the four entry wrappers `exception_sync_curr_el` / `exception_irq_curr_el` / `exception_sync_lower_el` / `exception_unexpected` -> static `*_impl`, bracketed by `halls_enter_frame`/`halls_leave_frame`), `kernel/extinction.c` (`halls_dump((void*)0)` call sites) | HX-1 Tier-1 crash dump on the fatal path -- runs on a dying machine, so it must be SOUND under unknown state. **HX-I1**: a fault DURING the dump trips the per-CPU `g_halls_in_dump` guard (set BEFORE any faulting read) and bails to `_torpor` -- never loops/recurses. **HX-I2**: the fp-chain backtrace is depth-capped (32) + sanity-gated (`halls_fp_is_sane`: 16-aligned, strictly increasing, in `[lo,hi)`); a wild x29 cannot spin or read unboundedly. **HX-I3**: the `EXTINCTION: ` ABI line (TOOLING.md section 10) stays first + unchanged; the dump follows under `HALLS:`. The per-CPU live-frame slot is set/restored by the entry wrappers (handlers do not migrate CPUs mid-execution at v1.0); an extinction (noreturn) inside a handler keeps the slot pointed at the dying frame. Return addrs are PAC-stripped (`xpaci`) before KASLR link-translation. **HX-2 (Tier-2 symbolization)** adds `arch/arm64/halls_symtab.h` + `halls_symtab.stub.c`, the PER-BUILD-DIR generated `<build>/generated/halls_symtab.c` (`tools/gen-halls-symtab.py` + the two-pass `tools/regen-halls-symtab.sh`, wired via `kernel/CMakeLists.txt` configure-seed + `tools/build.sh` + inherited by `tools/test-fault.sh`), and `halls_symbolize`/`halls_symbolize_table` in `halls.c` feeding `func+0xN` into `halls_emit_code_addr`. **HX-2 dump-safety**: `halls_symbolize` reads ONLY the `.rodata` table (no faulting stack reads), is bounded by `log2(count)`, takes no locks + allocates nothing, so it composes with HX-I1/HX-I2; the offset subtraction cannot underflow (search invariant `tab[lo].off <= q`). **Reloc-free + KASLR-independent**: the table stores u32 link-relative offsets, NOT absolute VAs -> 0 `R_AARCH64_RELATIVE` relocs (an absolute VA would draw one per symbol that the boot stub then SLIDES; verified 0 relocs land in the table region) -> the stored values are never slid. Generated PER-BUILD-DIR (default 1727 vs UBSan 1760 symbols -> a source-tree copy would clobber across configs) + BEST-EFFORT (stub `count=0` -> `halls_symbolize` returns NULL -> graceful HX-1 raw-only fallback when nm/python3 absent; symbolization is ergonomics, never a build gate). **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 broadening -- prose validation in `docs/reference/101-halls.md` (Tier-2 section) + `docs/HALLS-OF-EXTINCTION.md` + this row + the audit + the 8 unit tests (`halls.*` incl. `symbolize_table`) + the `tools/test-fault.sh` E2E. **THE CONSOLE CLAIM (2026-08-16)**: HX-I3 said the ABI line "stays first + unchanged" and carried nothing that would produce it under CONCURRENCY. The re-entrancy guard is per-CPU by design, so two CPUs extincting at once both proceed and each emits the banner as four unlocked `uart_puts` calls -- interleaved, the prefix can stop starting a line, and every consumer anchors the match (`^EXTINCTION:`), so a torn banner is a real extinction the harness CANNOT SEE (fail-open on the trusted channel). Closed by `extinction_claim_console` -- ONE `__atomic_exchange_n`, deliberately a raw atomic and not a kernel spinlock (a lock carrying order-assertions or debug bookkeeping could itself fault on a dying machine and turn a diagnosable crash into a silent one), TRY-once and never spin (the winner never releases, since every path ends in `_torpor`), losers park emitting NOTHING (a torn line can be read as a clean boot; a missing one leaves the guest visibly hung -- take the loud failure). A loser counts itself; the winner reports "peer-cpus-also-died: at least N" AFTER `halls_dump`, worded to contain neither `EXTINCTION` nor `EXTINCTION:` and to start no line at column 0, because some consumers match the bare token and a peer note shaped like a banner would be counted as a SECOND extinction by the very tooling this exists to keep honest. Prosecute on any change: the claim must stay lock-free + try-once; the recursive banner must stay gated on `g_extinction_owner[cpu]` (so "only the owner prints" is structural, not an argument about what can fault); nothing may export a way to claim the LIVE console (a test that took it would silence every later extinction in the boot -- the same fail-open from the other side, guarded by `extinction.console_unclaimed_on_clean_boot`). **SCOPE -- one of three tearing sources, and the names are confusingly close**: this closes extinction-vs-extinction. STILL OPEN: extinction-vs-a-peer's-normal-console-write (the vault's `seam-extinction-line-unserialized` -- the crash emitter holds no console writer role and its pre-emit ring flush is a bounded try-lock that SKIPS when a peer holds the ring, so a peer mid-write is exactly the case it declines to handle; the prescribed lift is a TRY-acquire of the writer role, never a park), and `IPI_HALT` (a commented-out reservation) which would subsume both. Tests `extinction.{claim_word_exactly_one_winner, console_unclaimed_on_clean_boot}`, both sabotage-verified (1367/1367 -> 1365/1367 under sabotage); as-built `docs/reference/04-extinction.md`. **THE RING CLAIM -- tearing source 2 of 3, closed 2026-08-18 (the vault's `seam-extinction-line-unserialized`)**: the winner now takes the console TX ring lock (`cons_tx_claim_for_dump`, kernel/cons.c) and NEVER releases it, so no peer's NORMAL console write can land inside the banner. Every steady-state producer pushes its unit under `g_cons_tx.lock` and every ring->FIFO drain pops under it, so holding that lock owns the wire. Deliberately NOT the writer role the seam prescribed -- the DRAIN does not consult the role (main#144), so bytes a peer already pushed would still reach the FIFO from cpu0's TX IRQ or a peer's kick; **a prescribed remedy is a hypothesis, and this one was checked against the drain path and refined.** Shape, and note EVERY property is the OPPOSITE of the console word's one file over, on purpose: **a BOUNDED spin, not try-once** (the word's holder is a dying peer that never releases, so a spin could only burn its bound; the ring's holder is a HEALTHY peer that WILL release in microseconds, so a try-once fails in exactly the case it exists for) -- 20 ms wall clock + a `1<<20` iteration backstop for a frozen/pre-init timer; **RAW, never counted** (`spin_trylock_raw`, NEW in spinlock.h -- the second legitimate raw holder after `sched()`'s cross-thread handoff, and the comment there now enumerates both); **IRQs masked FIRST and never restored** (with the ring lock held on this CPU, its own TX IRQ arm `cons_tx_drain_from_irq` -> `spin_lock_irqsave` would SELF-DEADLOCK -- a silent hang in place of the dump; the caller parks in `_torpor`, so nothing is owed back); **held forever on success** (peers reaching for the ring afterwards spin IRQ-masked on a dead machine -- the intended poor-man's `IPI_HALT`); **the flush under it is the FULL bounded ring** (the healthy `cons_tx_flush` loop shape through the non-spinning `uart_tx_try_putc`, no TXIM re-eval, no nested lock), because with the lock held forever whatever is still queued when the flush stops is LOST, where the predecessor's one-FIFO flush let the rest trickle out after the dump; **the owner re-entering is answered "held" at once** (a per-CPU owner record -- the classic seed `extinction` -> `halls_dump` faults -> ... -> `el1_sync_runaway` on the SAME cpu must not burn the bound on a lock it already holds and then report a miss it did not have). **THE OTHER EMITTER OF THE ABI LINE, which neither the 2026-08-16 console-word fix nor the vault's `abi-boot-banner` mirror set had enrolled**: `arch/arm64/exception.c::el1_sync_runaway` (the #214 EL1h-sync recursion banner) prints `EXTINCTION: el1-sync recursion ...` WITHOUT going through `extinction()`; `quaestor owner` flags it as matching the literal outside the mirror set, and the COMPILE is what found it (it called the deleted `cons_tx_flush_for_dump`). It now takes BOTH serializers: `extinction_console_claim_or_own()` (NEW in extinction.c -- claim the word, or confirm THIS cpu already owns it, since the runaway is reached from a chain that may have claimed it at depth 1; a PEER holding it means a peer is dumping, so it parks silent like any loser, counted) then `cons_tx_claim_for_dump()`, whose miss it reports AFTER its own banner on `extinction()`'s exact terms. **That path is exercised by NO test and this chunk added three calls to it (main#246)**: in a healthy kernel the #806 guard extincts at the SECOND kernel fault so `g_el1_sync_depth` never reaches 3 -- reaching the runaway requires the extinction/Halls path ITSELF to fault, which is exactly what the base tree was doing and what this fix removed. So the fix deleted the only thing reaching it, and everything on it is static-audited only; "no current path drives it" is the latent-P1 trap, not a safety argument. A variant injecting a fault INSIDE `halls_dump` would drive it (do it with main#245). **A PRE-EXISTING DEFECT THIS FIXES, MEASURED ON THE BASE TREE (main#244)**: `tools/test-fault.sh recursive_kernel_fault` produced **NO OUTPUT AT ALL** on `f525cea3` -- that variant installs a wild `TPIDR_EL1` on purpose, the pre-emit flush's COUNTED `spin_trylock` -> `spin_preempt_inc` -> `current_thread()->magic` FAULTED inside the extinction path, the nested EL1-sync faults reached `el1_sync_runaway` which called the same flush and faulted again, and the `depth > MAX` arm parked SILENTLY. Three runs isolate the one variable (base TIMEOUT / raw PASS / counted-restored TIMEOUT byte-identical to base). Broken ~2026-07-20 (#75 P1-F meets #360's counted spinlocks) and unobserved for a month because **`test-fault.sh` is wired into NO gate** -- grep-proven over the Makefile, `ci-smp-gate.sh`, `test.sh`, `test-interactive.sh`, `.github`; wiring it is main#245. **The rule that generalizes: a dying-machine path may not call a primitive that reads state the crash may have destroyed** -- #360 retrofitted that deref under every existing `spin_trylock` caller, including one on the extinction path, without anyone re-asking whether that caller could survive it. **Prosecute on any change**: the claim stays RAW (a counted variant reintroduces main#244 exactly); the IRQ mask stays BEFORE the acquire and is never restored; the spin stays BOUNDED with the miss reported (`console-ring: NOT held ...` after the dump, worded per the peer-report rules -- no `EXTINCTION` token, nothing at column 0); nothing may export a claim of the LIVE ring (`cons_tx_claim_core` takes a caller-supplied lock; `cons.ring_unclaimed_on_clean_boot` guards it, and a test that took the live ring would silence the console for every test after it); both ABI-line emitters stay enrolled in BOTH serializers. **RESIDUAL, NAMED (main#243)**: the ring lock reaches only writers that go THROUGH the ring -- steady-state kernel diagnostics that still write the UART directly (`sched.c`'s runnable-dump, `syscall.c`'s vivarium unserved/`viv-trace`, `exec.c`'s exec-failure, `9p_client.c`'s ownerless-frame) sit outside it and are also the #126 20-ms-per-byte class; cons.h's contract already says to use `cons_diag_line`. Tearing source 3 (`IPI_HALT`, a commented-out reservation in smp.h) would subsume all of it. Tests `cons.{ring_claim_core_returns_holding, ring_claim_core_bounded_when_held, ring_unclaimed_on_clean_boot}`, both sabotage arms in one run (1427/1427 -> 1424/1427, each failure naming its own assertion) + `test-fault.sh` 7/7. |

Lock order `vma_lock -> buddy zone->lock`. `exec_setup`/`vma_drain` exempt (single-threaded by construction). |

verified torpor.c:188 is the tree's ONLY production uaccess-under-spinlock that can hit a FILE page) + **SA-F1** `file_demand_page_slow` single-read corrupts an interior dev9p text page on a partial Rread (FIXED: loop the read; regression `demand_page.file_short_read_fills_page`); **F2** [P3, unreachable at v1.0] stale `freq->slot` reuse -> closed-with-justification. v1.x seams: anon-COW data, per-page I-32 charge, the blob-path unification, the Image pressure-reclaim pass (which must re-establish the torpor non-blocking-uaccess-under-lock property). **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 broadening -- prose validation in `docs/EXEC-LOAD-DESIGN.md` + ARCH §6.5/§28 I-36 + `docs/reference/126-revenant.md` + this row + the focused audit (`memory/audit_revenant_closed_list.md`) + the kernel tests + the boot proof + default 986/986 + the SMP gate. **Read-ahead cluster addendum (go-arc)**: `file_demand_page_slow` becomes a thin entry -> `file_demand_page_cluster` (ONE batched `dev->read` of up to `REVENANT_READAHEAD_PAGES`=64 pages via a KP_ZERO staging buffer -> per-slot order-0 pages, word-copied + I-cache-synced BEFORE any PTE can back them -> per-slot install-once under `vma_lock`+`v->lock` [`file_install_cluster_locked`: sibling-won slots keep the resident page, ours freed outside `v->lock`; The per-Burrow-single-prot property is ENFORCED by the `exec` component of the Image key (#45 audit F1: a crafted ELF's aliased R+X/R-only PT_LOADs get distinct Burrows -> no dual-prot Burrow -> the #317 stale-I-cache hazard closed); the gate also requires `PF_R` (#45 audit F2). Prosecute: the gate keeps `PF_W` eager; the `exec` key component stays in both search passes + the install; the I-36 segment-generality (EXEC-LOAD-DESIGN section 4.6 walk). **Unaligned-`PT_LOAD` addendum (#149, 2026-08-03)**: a segment maps from the page-aligned FLOOR of `p_vaddr` with its bytes at Burrow offset `lead` (ELF requires only `p_vaddr == p_offset (mod p_align)`, and aarch64's 64 KiB `p_align` means every real linker emits an unaligned data segment -- 20 of 20 stock Alpine ELFs; the blanket reject held only because everything OUR toolchain emits is aligned, #136's shape). Prosecute: the leading slack stays ZERO (never file-filled -- it is outside `[p_vaddr, p_vaddr+p_memsz)`, so filling it maps content no segment claims); the file-backed arm keeps requiring `lead == 0` via `exec_load_into`'s `file_shareable` gate, which is what keeps section-4.6's `burrow-offset-0 == seg->vaddr` identity true BY CONSTRUCTION (an unaligned non-writable segment degrades to the private eager arm; `map_file_backed` re-checks + fails closed); two `PT_LOAD`s sharing a page stay REFUSED (the W+X union I-12 forbids); no `file_offset` alignment gate on the eager arms. **Any new refusal on this path must report itself** -- the reject ran in the child thunk after the pid was returned, so a refused load was indistinguishable from a program that ran and exited 1. **Environment-on-the-frame addendum (#140, L-6c):** the builder used to write a lone NULL for `envp` in BOTH frame shapes, so no Thylacine process had ever had a POSIX environment; the shapes now collapse to ONE (each had written its own envp NULL, so the defect had two homes) and the environment arrives as DATA -- `exec_stage_env` projects a Proc's own `/env` for the spawn paths and the native `SYS_EXECVE` (whose ABI has no envp and therefore means PRESERVE), while a phenotyped `execve` packs its own; bounded independently at `EXEC_ENV_MAX` / `EXEC_ENV_DATA_MAX` and refused with `T_E_2BIG` rather than truncated. The authoritative prosecution copy is the ARCH section 25.4 row. |
| Boot-time LSE alternatives-patcher (Lazarus W1.5) | `arch/arm64/alternatives.{c,h}` (the `ALTERNATIVE()` macro emitting `.altinstructions` + `.altinstr_replacement`; `apply_alternatives()` the boot pass + the `g_alt_total`/`g_alt_applied` counters), `arch/arm64/atomic_lse.h` (the LL/SC-default patchable RMW primitives `t_atomic_xchg_acq_u32` / `t_atomic_fetch_{add,sub}_*` via the `T_ATOMIC_XCHG` / `T_ATOMIC_FETCH_OP` macros + the `fetch_sub == fetch_add(-v)` C wrappers), `arch/arm64/mmu.c::mmu_patch_text` + static `patch_kva_to_pa` (`at s1e1r`+PAR_EL1) / `patch_map` / `patch_unmap` / `patch_sync_icache`, `arch/arm64/kernel.ld` (`.altinstructions` + `.altinstr_replacement` in `.rodata` with `_alt_instructions_start/_end`), `kernel/main.c` (`apply_alternatives()` after `hw_features_detect` + allocator, strictly before `smp_init`), the routed sites (`kernel/include/thylacine/spinlock.h` `spin_lock`/`spin_trylock` xchg, `kernel/sched.c` `g_try_steal_rotate`, `kernel/spoor.c` + `kernel/srvconn.c` int refcounts) | Lazarus W1.5 (PORTABILITY.md section 4.5; user-voted 2026-06-06 over outline-atomics). **Self-modifies `.text` -- the central invariant is I-12 (W^X).** Rewrites the LL/SC atomic baseline (the v8.0 floor from W1) to single-instruction LSE on FEAT_LSE cores at boot, with zero steady-state branch -- the Linux arm64 `apply_alternatives` model. **I-12 holds because the write goes through a TRANSIENT RW-not-X alias** (a scratch VA mapped `PTE_KERN_RW` = RW+PXN+UXN); the canonical `.text` stays RO+X and the P3-Bb direct-map alias RO+XN throughout -- no page is ever writable AND executable at PTE granularity, not even momentarily. Runs **single-CPU, full-DAIF-masked (`msr daifset, #0xf`), strictly before `smp_init`** -- no peer executes a site mid-patch; secondaries start later with cold I-caches and fetch the patched bytes. (Masking F/A as well as I also closes the `at s1e1r`->`PAR_EL1` window in `patch_kva_to_pa`; W1.5-audit F1.) I-cache maintenance per ARM ARM B2.4 (`dc cvau` on the scratch alias / `ic ivau` on the canonical VA / `dsb ish` / `isb`; CTR_EL0 line stride; per-page loop for cross-page spans). The `.altinstructions` table is **reloc-free + KASLR-independent** (PC-relative `.word` offsets slide with their targets, like the Halls symtab). LL/SC is the always-correct fallback: an unpatched site (A72, or any skipped entry) is correct by construction, so a patcher bug fails **safe** (slower, never wrong). `fetch_sub == fetch_add(-v)` preserves the signed-PRE value the Spoor/SrvConn `pre <= 0` UAF check reads. **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 broadening -- prose validation in PORTABILITY.md section 4.5 + this row + the ARCH section 25.4 row + the focused audit (`memory/audit_w15_closed_list.md`) + the `alternatives.*` unit tests (`patch_applied`: `g_alt_applied == g_alt_total` -- every FEAT_LSE site -- on `-cpu max`; `atomics_correct`: the patched LSE forms compute correctly) + default 716/716 + the SMP gate (default+UBSan x smp4/smp8) on `-cpu max` (LSE active). |

the **borrow-guard UAF** (the kthread borrows the client from a live op's pin, NEVER owning the client lifetime -- the Loom-4 F1 class); the **lock order** (`g_dev9p_poll_lock -> c->lock` at submit; `dev9p_poll_complete` is `c->lock` -> atomics-only -> no cycle; the **on_complete seam contract** (under `c->lock`: no sleep, no poll-state lock, no `p9_client_*` re-entry, atomics only); the **QTPOLL gate soundness** (a regular dev9p file -- Stratum/corvus -- is POSIX always-ready, NEVER probed; FAIL-SAFE: an unmarked file / a netd->dev9p plumbing slip degrades to always-ready, never an unsound probe; a native `SYS_POLL` on a regular dev9p file is reachable, so the gate is load-bearing); the **mask-union widening** (a broader poller abandons + resubmits the union via #845 Tflush so a `poll(POLLOUT)` cannot hang behind a concurrent `poll(POLLIN)`); the **stranded-op GC** (a timed-out poll on a never-ready socket abandons the op when its poll-list empties, else the pin leaks the Spoor); the **deadline-capability guard** (the kthread's frame-boundary recv deadline REQUIRES a deadline-capable transport -- netd's srvconn `/net` mount is; FIXED: `dev9p_poll_collect_clients` pumps EVERY distinct non-terminal-op client per cycle (bounded `DEV9P_POLL_MAX_PUMP=16`, dedup-by-client, the borrow-guard N-fold; k<=16 = every realistic config COMPLETE). F2 [P3] OOM-no-covering-op unwakeable-park (+ R2-F2 the narrower-live-op-under-sustained-OOM no-progress) -> degrade to always-ready; F5 [P3] the lockless `p->poll` fast-path read -> `__atomic` ACQUIRE/RELEASE pairing. F3 (idle-pump 50Hz wake while parked, #221) + F4 (pouch lazy ready-fd ABA on slot reuse, #222) + R2-F1 (the >16-client case STARVES the tail via LIFO, #223) + #220 (netd listener-poll) = documented v1.x seams. The borrow-guard (N-fold pin/clunk balance), the `dev9p_close` extinction-invariant (a live `ps->op` always holds its own pin -> the collect-ref is never sole), the lock order, and the `npump==0`-vs-`count!=0` park race (sleep() re-checks the cond before registering) ALL traced sound by both prosecutors. Spec gate RE-VERIFIED (clean GREEN + `BUGGY_LOST_READY` counterexample + liveness). **#294 cancel-at-close addendum (the readiness-op TEARDOWN lifetime; spec-first `specs/net_poll_teardown.tla` model-first @bb72098):** the original design pinned the netd `ready` Spoor (`op->pinned = spoor_ref`), so `dev9p_close` -- which sends the slot-freeing `ready`-fd Tclunk -- could only run AFTER the poll-pump kthread GC'd the stranded op + dropped the pin; a real SMP race left stranded ops un-GC'd -> the Tclunk never delivered -> a PERMANENT per-connection netd slot LEAK on the poll-timeout/abandon path (NOT a /net session death -- the network stays UP; bounded at netd `MAX_SLOTS=16`; the prior "shared reader-pump latches c->dead -> session death" diagnosis was OVERTURNED by ground truth). The Heisenbug -- heavy in-guest instrumentation HIDES the leak (prints shift the GC timing window) -- makes in-guest boots unreliable for verifying a concurrency fix here, so **the model is the reliable design verifier** (`net_poll_teardown.tla` Fix=TRUE clean + the Fix=FALSE `buggy_leak` counterexample, TLC-green). **The fix (cancel-at-close):** the readiness op no longer pins the Spoor -- it holds a refcounted poll-state ref (`dev9p_poll_state.refs`: the priv holds 1 [`cand->refs = 1` at lazy-alloc], each outstanding op holds 1; freed when both drop -> no UAF, the model's `NoUseAfterFreePs`) + a session ref (`p9_attached_ref` on the priv's `attached_owner`; NULL only on the kernel-test path where the client is externally owned). So `dev9p_close` runs AT the user's fd-close (the Spoor's LAST ref), GRABS the still-outstanding op from `g_dev9p_poll_ops` under `g_dev9p_poll_lock` (whoever removes it owns the teardown -- the kthread may have collected it first; ps->op is consistent with the registry under g_lock), CANCELS it (`p9_client_abandon_async` = clear `c->inflight[tag]` + Tflush #845), `dev9p_poll_op_free`s it (drop the session + ps refs), then delivers the `ready`-fd Tclunk DETERMINISTICALLY -- the slot frees at fd-close, not hinged on the kthread GC. The borrow-guard (`dev9p_poll_collect_clients`) switches from `spoor_ref(op->pinned)` to `p9_attached_ref(op->attached_owner)`. **A SECOND load-bearing fix the kernel TEST surfaced (BELOW the model's abstraction): `kernel/9p_session.c::any_outstanding_on_fid` counted `awaiting_flush` entries**, so the abandon's Tflush (which leaves the readiness oldtag `awaiting_flush` targeting the fid) made the immediate SendClunk-precondition REFUSE the Tclunk -> the slot would leak anyway (and this would bite production identically). Fix: `any_outstanding_on_fid` EXCLUDES flushed entries -- a cancelled op does not block a fid op (Tflush-then-Tclunk is the standard cancel-then-close; the tag stays reserved until its Rflush, the I-10 reuse guard, which is orthogonal to the LIVE-op precondition). Sound for all 4 callers (walk-new_fid / clunk / lopen / lcreate); the late flushed reply is discarded ownerless by tag, never bound to a reused fid (I-11). Prove: `dev9p.poll_cancel_at_close` (a walked QTPOLL Spoor over a loopback whose readiness Tread DEFERS -> op outstanding at close -> assert no extinct + the op torn down [registry baseline] + the op cancelled [Tflush] + the `ready`-fd Tclunk delivered [the leak fix] + the fid unbound). Files: `kernel/dev9p_poll.c` (the lifecycle), `kernel/dev9p.c` (`dev9p_close` ordering: cancel BEFORE Tclunk BEFORE attached_owner unref), `kernel/9p_session.c` (`any_outstanding_on_fid`), `kernel/test/test_dev9p.c`. No new section-28 invariant (composes I-9 + I-7-analog refcount + I-10/I-11). `memory/bug_294_net_session_death.md` + `memory/audit_net6_closed_list.md` (the #294 close). |

These JOIN this row. **net-4d CLOSED (Opus-4.8-max prosecutor + concurrent self-audit, CONVERGED on the deferred-overwrite root; 0 P0 / 0 P1 / 1 P2 / 3 P3, NOT dirty; + a precautionary focused round-2 on the F1 deferred-reply fix, CLEAN -- converged over 2 rounds)**: **F1[P2]** a held deferred cs/dns read tag could be LOST (I-9) -- `Query.deferred` is a single `(tag,cap)` slot, so a SECOND concurrent read on one fid (legal for a multi-thread Proc -- the kernel dev9p client multiplexes by TAG, the #841 model) OVERWROTE it, and a RE-WRITE while a read is deferred (`query_begin`->`query_drop`) dropped it; the first held `Rread` was never delivered (no crash, self-inflicted, death-recoverable -> P2 not P1, I-5 not engaged). FIXED with two MINIMAL guards (NOT a wait/wake restructure): `query_read` returns an empty `Rread` for a 2nd read when one is already deferred (the first keeps its real answer); `h_write` rejects (`E_PROTO`) a re-write while `fid_has_deferred(fid)` (fid-specific; bounded by the ~10s DNS timeout, never a permanent wedge). The bare-clunk-while-deferred facet is mitigated on the trusted mount (Tflush->`cancel_dns_flush`, #845) + a client protocol violation otherwise (dispositioned per the net-3 listen-clunk close). **F2[P3]** `Content` buf 128->256 (the net-4c claim of 256 was wrong; the ~120 B status render was safe -- `push` min-clamps, no OOB -- but tight). **F3[P3]** documented the shared dns socket's `queries` Vec high-water (bounded <= MAX_CONNS*MAX_FIDS, REUSED -- not a leak). **SA-3[P3]** clarified the DHCP-renewal comment (iface + ifc both pinned at bring-up -> coherent; renewal re-application is the v1.x seam). The single-completion `get_query_result` discipline (verified against the REAL smoltcp 0.12 source -- `slot.as_mut().unwrap()` panics on a freed slot -> a whole-network DoS), the no-query-slot-leak, the parser fail-closed bounds, the iface<->snapshot coherence, the single-threadedness, and the isolation (I-1) all HELD. Three NEW deterministic in-guest proofs (the `loopback_e2e`/`ipifc_e2e` pattern): `proto_selftest` (the cs/dns/ndb/mask parser battery -- the OWED-since-net-2d host-test coverage delivered IN-GUEST, since netd is a no_std+aarch64 BIN crate that can't `cargo test` without a feature-gated refactor that would risk the green device build -- the netdev-lib `cargo test --no-default-features` path does not apply to a bin; carried as a NAMED seam), `dns_defer_guard_selftest` (the F1 regression -- fails on pre-fix code by construction), `dns_loopback_e2e` (the OWED net-4b deferred-read E2E -- a mock DNS responder bound to 127.0.0.1:53 on an ISOLATED stack answers a fixed A query via `build_dns_response`, so the shared `dns::Socket` resolves a name in-guest via the real `dns_query_start`->poll->`dns_poll` methods; the net-3d loopback analog). **THE net-4 ARC IS COMPLETE.** prose in `docs/reference/121-netd.md` (the net-4d section) + NET-DESIGN section 20 + this row + boot OK (`net-4d proto selftest PASS` + `net-4d dns defer-guard PASS` + `net-4d dns loopback E2E PASS` + 930/930 + 0 EXT) + the SMP gate (default+UBSan x smp4/smp8 N=10 = 40/40 PASS, 0 corruption, 0 timing, the cleanest possible). `memory/audit_net4_closed_list.md`. **net-6a-1 LANDED** the BLOCKING `data` read (NET-DESIGN §12; pure userspace, kernel byte-unchanged) -- closes the net-5 audit F2 recv-returns-0 seam: NEW `data_recv_outcome` (`RecvOutcome::{Data,WouldBlock,Eof}`, smoltcp-`recv_error_check`-grounded: TCP `Ok(0)`=WouldBlock / `Err(Finished)`=Eof / `Err(InvalidState)`+is_active=connecting-WouldBlock; UDP/ICMP `Err(Exhausted)`=WouldBlock, never Eof) replaces the EOF-vs-no-data-collapsing `data_recv` (kept as a thin wrapper for the net-3 demos); `h_read(FK_DATA)` parks a `PendingRead{fid,slot_n,tag,cap}` on WouldBlock (`Disp::Deferred`, the held tag) + the serve loop `poll_data` delivers the held Rread on data/EOF -- the net-3a/4b deferred-reply pattern applied to the data stream, with the SAME clunk/teardown/Tversion/Tflush cancel discipline + a MAX_FIDS bound (#65). A pouch recv()->read() blocks AUTOMATICALLY (no shim change). **SELF-AUDIT found+fixed SA-1**: a `count==0` read parked forever (cap=0 -> `recv_slice(&mut[])`=Ok(0) even on a readable socket -> WouldBlock -> park; POSIX says return 0 at once) -> `if cap==0 { return Rread(empty) }` guard. The FLAT-Vec PendingReads (distinct tags, FIFO over the slot) AVOID the net-4d single-deferred-slot clobber; I-9 holds by netd's single-threadedness (`net.poll` runs before `poll_data`). Boot proof: `net-6a recv-blocking E2E PASS` (`server::recv_blocking_e2e` -- an isolated 127.0.0.1 loopback connection driven through the REAL `data_recv_outcome`: empty->WouldBlock, sent->Data, peer-close->Eof; LESSON: it FAILED first at stage `connect` from an OMITTED `iface.update_ip_addrs` 127.0.0.1/8 -- the stage diagnostic pinned the establish bug instantly, ground-truth over theory [[feedback-no-host-load]]). net-6a-1 is autonomy-covered userspace on this surface; the FORMAL net-6 focused audit is **net-6b** (the `dev9p.poll` bridge -- SPEC-FIRST `net_poll.tla`, the one ABI surface, user-voted 2026-06-18). `memory/audit_net6_closed_list.md`. The redesign that retires the per-Proc-permanent-ASID model (which extincted on the 256th concurrent Proc -- RW-1 B-F1, P1) for the Linux arm64 rolling-ASID cache: ASIDs are recycled across a generation counter with TLB-flush-on-rollover -> unbounded concurrent Procs. **SMP-race-bearing -- prosecute hard.** The central hazard is a generation rollover concurrent with another CPU's context switch reassigning an `active`/`reserved` ASID -> two address spaces alias one ASID -> the TLB returns a wrong translation -> silent cross-Proc corruption (the famous rolling-ASID race). **Model-first**: `specs/asid.tla` (clean + the `rollover_steals_active` buggy cfg) is written + TLC-green BEFORE the impl -- **spec-first RE-ENABLED for this surface** (the SMP-race precedent from the deep-smp-review, ARCH §8.4). | Tickless idle (NO_HZ_IDLE; the TI arc) | `arch/arm64/timer.c` (`timer_arm_oneshot_cnt` + the pure `timer_oneshot_tval` -- the one-shot primitive, clamped to `[TIMER_MIN_RELOAD, TIMER_MAX_RELOAD]`; TI-1), `kernel/sched.c` (`timerwait_earliest_deadline` + the pure `tickless_target_cnt` + the shared `sched_idle_park(bool tickless)` body [arm one-shot -> WFI -> `timer_arm_this_cpu()` restore-periodic + `timerwait_tick()` service-deadline] + `bootcpu_idle_main`; TI-2), `kernel/smp.c` (`per_cpu_main` calls `sched_idle_park(timer_armed)`), `kernel/include/thylacine/sched.h` (`TICKLESS_IDLE_BACKSTOP_NS`). The #299 measurement: HVF idle **332%** (the never-stopped tick -> per-tick VTIMER exit + emulated-GICv2 MMIO vmexits + a WFI that never parks at a 1 ms period) vs TCG 21.5%; 250 Hz -> 5.2%; bug_110's "interactive chardev kick / nothing to do" closure CORRECTED (sample-proven: the idle QEMU main thread is parked in `mach_msg`, the vCPU threads churn `hvf_vcpu_exec`/`hv_vcpu_run`/`hvf_wfi`). **NO new §28 invariant** -- composes **I-9** (no wakeup lost across the idle one-shot arm: register-then-observe, `idle_in_wfi=true` BEFORE the arm + WFI under the IRQ-masked idle region, §8.5) + **I-8** (a deadlined / work-arrival wake still reaches a tickless idle CPU; the `IPI_RESCHED` work-arrival path is tick-independent + unchanged). Prosecute: the **I-9 arm-race** (no deadline / work-arrival lost in the compute-nearest-deadline -> WFI window); the **wake-to-running restore** (TI-2: `timer_arm_this_cpu()` re-arms periodic BEFORE placed work runs -- else an IPI-woken CPU runs un-ticked to the backstop, an I-17 window; the re-arm DEASSERTS the one-shot IRQ, so `timerwait_tick()` runs explicitly under the same masked region or a deadlined sleeper never wakes -- a busy-spin); the **dropped-IPI backstop** (the 100 ms cap self-heals a hypothetical dropped IPI -> a latency hiccup, never a hang -- it replaces the smp.c always-armed-timer backstop, #868 having made the `IPI_RESCHED` reliable); **lock order** (`g_timerwait.lock` read-then-release before WFI; `timerwait_tick` nested under the idle loop's `spin_lock_irqsave(NULL)` IRQ-mask -- no new cycle, g_timerwait.lock a leaf); **timekeeping unaffected** (`CNTVCT`, not the tick; `g_ticks` freezes at cpu0-idle but is busy-wait / diagnostic-only + busy-wait runs in a running context); the **redundant multi-idle-CPU deadline coverage** (N idle CPUs arming the same deadline is safe, bounded by ncpus); the **secondary gating** (`sched_idle_park(false)` pre-preempt = byte-identical WFI-on-IPI, quiescent during the test phase). **Spec-first RE-ENABLED** (the scheduler surface, ARCH §8.4/§8.10): the focused sibling `specs/sched_tickless.tla` LANDED model-first (clean cfg TLC-green: `NoLostWake` + `ParkedImpliesRegistered` + `RunningNotParked` + the `EventuallyRuns` liveness; + `sched_tickless_buggy.cfg` = `BUGGY_PARK`, the park-before-register lost-wake counterexample at depth 4) -- a sibling, not a `scheduler.tla` extension (which already proves the tickless wake-correctness; the arm-race would entangle its 11 cfgs -- the `sched_oncpu` precedent). Prose validation in `docs/TICKLESS-IDLE.md` + ARCH §8.6 / §8.10 + this row + the focused audit (TI-3) + the kernel tests (the pure `tickless_target_cnt` arm-decision; the nearest-deadline scan; the one-shot clamp) + the HVF idle re-measure (~0%, the #299 method) + the SMP gate. **TI-4 ADDENDUM (the work-conservation push side; #304/#307/#309): the busy-tick overload kick (TI-4c -- `kernel/sched.c::sched_tick` + `cpu_has_surplus_for_kick`, reusing `sched_notify_idle_peer`; modeled by `specs/sched_rebalance.tla::Overload`), the always-true `thread_may_run_on` affinity-ready seam (consulted at `select_target_cpu` + `try_steal` -- the priority/affinity pluggable point), the wake-source telemetry (`sched_wc_stats.tickless_{ipi,oneshot}_wakes`), and the `TICKLESS_IDLE_BACKSTOP_NS` retune 100 ms -> 4 ms (TI-4e). NEW wake path on this lineage -- prosecuted: the kick's I-9 register-then-observe (the IPI lifts a registered-idle peer's park) + the IRQ-context lock-free surplus read (relaxed head load, never deref, benign racy) + the affinity-gate inertness (always-true -> the placement/steal gates collapse to their originals) + the 4 ms backstop's tick-coupled-test safety (the test phase runs PERIODIC idle, go_tickless=false, so the backstop does not apply during tests). **TI-4e root-caused the residual tickless boot slowdown to HVF deep-park vCPU resume latency (99.85% IPI wakes -- NOT a guest bug; #299/#890): the 4 ms re-poll is an HVF dev-loop warmth knob (HVF boot ~7 s vs ~17-35 s at 100 ms, ~5% HVF idle), NOT a scheduler fix -- on bare metal deep-park already gives fast boot + ~0% idle. Bare-metal confirmation owed at Lazarus/RPi.** Focused Opus-4.8-max audit CLEAN 0/0/0/2 (both P3 = pre-existing test-fragility shared with the sibling `sched.*` tests; `memory/audit_ti4_closed_list.md`). **#363 park-guard ADDENDUM (the #33-audit F1, 2026-07-05): `sched_idle_park` MUST NOT park over its own queue.** sched() picks BEFORE it requeues prev, so a slice-expiry preempt (or yield) of a thread on an otherwise-empty queue dispatches the pinned idle with the preempted thread landing in run_tree[NORMAL] AFTER the pick; the dispatched idle resumes INSIDE sched_idle_park past its own sched() and pre-#363 proceeded straight to the one-shot arm + WFI -- parking up to the 4 ms backstop over its own RUNNABLE thread (no IPI exists for a local self-requeue; the multi-ms TI-4d starved-park records were THIS, misattributed to peer backlog; the `scale` bench is structurally blind -- a self-ratio). FIX: after sched() returns, loop `while (cpu_has_surplus_for_kick(cs)) sched();` before the arm+WFI (two relaxed head loads; the deferred park is a stutter on sched_tickless.tla's park action -- NoLostWake/ParkedImpliesRegistered untouched; a peer's concurrent insert still rides the idle_in_wfi register-then-observe IPI, I-9). Witness: the boot-wc tickless starved_ms collapse (pre-fix ~9.6 s/boot). Prosecute on any change: the loop must stay deref-free + must not consume need_resched + terminates (each iteration dispatches real work). Spec `sched_rebalance.tla` clean + `buggy_nokick` + `buggy_nolift` GREEN. Prose: `docs/reference/15-scheduler.md` "Work-conservation under tickless idle (TI-4)" + ARCH §8.6 + `docs/TICKLESS-IDLE.md` §9.8 + the `scheduler.cpu_surplus_for_kick` test + the SMP gate. |

| Handle table | `kernel/handle.c` | Rights monotonicity (I-2, I-6), transfer-via-9P (I-4), hardware-handle non-transferability (I-5). **#844 handle-lifetime pass** (`c1c2948` + the F1/F2 audit close): the per-Proc `HandleTable.lock` (the Plan 9 Fgrp lock) serializes alloc / close / get / dup; `handle_get` returns a BY-VALUE snapshot with the obj refcount HELD (`handle_acquire_obj` under the lock) paired with `handle_put` that drops it OUTSIDE the lock (release may sleep -- spoor_clunk's Dev close hook, SrvConn teardown) -- closing the multi-thread-Proc TOCTOU where a sibling `handle_close` freed a slot's obj + zeroed the slot under a live `struct Handle *` (widest across the blocking dev->read/write / accept / 9P handshake). The KOBJ_SPOOR-only helpers (`sys_lookup_spoor` / `sys_lookup_rw_handle` / `sys_resolve_dir_wr`) TRANSFER the ref (the caller `spoor_clunk`s); direct sites snapshot + `handle_put` on every exit. Lock order: table lock -> `v->lock` (a `burrow_ref` in get/dup); never the reverse. Audit R1 (Opus) closed **F1[P1]** (`handle_alloc` -- the primary fd-creating path -- was left UNLOCKED while get/close/dup were locked -> peer-thread slot collision -> double-owned fd / leak / UAF; fixed by routing it through the same locked `handle_install_locked`) + **F2[P2]** (`sys_srv_peer` read the raw `cn = sp->aux` AFTER `spoor_clunk(sp)` -> UAF window; fixed by hoisting the value-captured reads before the clunk); **F3[P3]** the pivot-vs-walk `root_spoor` race tracked (#848, dormant). **No new spec** -- prose in this row + `memory/audit_844_closed_list.md` + the matrix (smp4 + smp8 + UBSan 704/704 + login E2E + 0 EXTINCTION). **#151 close-on-exec addendum (LINEAGE L-6c; the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution copy is the ARCH section 25.4 Handle table row):** `struct HandleTable` gained a `cloexec` BITMAP parallel to the slot array (+ `handle_dup_posix` / `handle_set_cloexec` / `handle_get_cloexec` / `handle_close_on_exec`, and a sweep in `sys_execve_core` after the commit point). Parallel rather than a field in `struct Handle` because POSIX close-on-exec belongs to the DESCRIPTOR, not the open file description behind it -- `dup(fd)` yields a second descriptor onto the same description with the flag CLEAR, so a bit in `struct Handle` would be shared by exactly the two things POSIX requires to differ (Linux keeps `close_on_exec` beside `fd[]` in `struct fdtable` for the same reason); and because `struct Handle` has no slack, so a u32 there takes the table across the 2-page boundary into 3 for one bit per slot. Prosecute on any change: the bit is ESTABLISHED at `handle_install_locked` (the single fd-creating chokepoint) and NEVER inherited, which is what stops a reused index carrying the previous occupant's flag; the four lifecycle sites are a SET and a missing one is silent (install establishes, close clears, `handle_table_copy_into` COPIES -- fork preserves, per POSIX and because a shell sets it once and forks per command -- `handle_replace` LEAVES ALONE, since the fd number persists so the fd's flag must); the sweep must stay AFTER the commit point (the closes SLEEP via a Spoor's Dev close hook, and a FAILED exec must leave the process unchanged) and BEFORE the trapframe rewrite; `handle_dup_posix` reads the parent's rights under the SAME lock hold as the install, and its `min_idx` is load-bearing (a shell's `savefd()` does `F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC(fd, 10)` to move a bookkeeping fd out of the low range). NATIVE BEHAVIOUR IS BYTE-UNCHANGED and that falls out rather than being arranged -- nothing outside the phenotype can set the flag, so a native Proc's bitmap is empty. No new section-28 invariant (the flag is not a right and confers nothing). Tests `handles.cloexec_lifecycle` / `cloexec_exec_sweep` / `cloexec_fork_preserves` / `dup_posix`. **#198 fid-ceiling addendum (`PROC_HANDLE_MAX` 64 -> 256 -> 1024, user-approved 2026-08-13):** a warp GL client holds ONE handle per live BO mapping, so a Quake-class texture set needs 300-500; tapestryd holds a `dma_fd` per BUILT BO on the same constant (the #214 ~237 dma-create wall). The table is now 8 + a 128-B cloexec bitmap + 24*1024 ~= 24.7 KiB, an order-4 `alloc_pages` (it was order-3 at 256). **Prosecute any lift of this constant along TWO axes that are easy to miss.** (1) **Consumers that SIZE something off it.** `sys_poll_for_proc` stack-allocates `waiters[POLL_MAX_NFDS]` + `held[POLL_MAX_NFDS]`; had those been sized off `PROC_HANDLE_MAX` the frame would now be ~56 KiB against a 32-KiB kstack -- a guaranteed overflow. They are correctly decoupled (`poll.h` says so at the constant), but `docs/reference/72-poll.md` NAMED `PROC_HANDLE_MAX` as the bound, which was harmless only while both were 64; the lift is exactly when a wrong-constant citation stops being cosmetic. Re-grep every `PROC_HANDLE_MAX` mention on any change and ask of each whether it is the REAL bound or a coincidence. (2) **TRANSITIVE bounds that quietly rested on the table being small.** The fid-lift audit F1 found one: Loom SQPOLL kthreads were left uncounted by the thread cap precisely because "one per live SQPOLL Loom, and Looms are bounded by a 64-entry handle table" was a sufficient bound -- at 1024 that is 32 MiB of unswappable kstack, so the kthread now takes a real `proc_sqpoll_charge` against `PROC_THREAD_MAX`. A constant that is an argument in someone else's safety proof cannot be lifted without re-running that proof. Also re-derived at the lift: `LOOM_MAX_REG_HANDLES` (64) was justified as "Matches PROC_HANDLE_MAX" -- a rationale the lift VOIDED; the value stands on the per-RING cost argument instead (a Proc may hold many rings, each entry pins a Spoor ref). |

The page backing lives in `kernel/burrow.c` itself; `mm/` holds only the allocators**) | Refcount, mapping lifecycle (I-7). **#847** (`8acdcce`, the #844 precursor): the dual refcount (`handle_count` + `mapping_count`) AND the `handle_count==0 && mapping_count==0` free decision -- duplicated in `burrow_unref` + `burrow_release_mapping` -- now run under a per-Burrow `spin_lock` (was a plain non-atomic `++/--` + a lockless dual-check -> a latent SMP UAF/double-free reachable from a multi-thread Proc: torn count, or two paths racing the free decision). `burrow_free_internal` runs OUTSIDE `v->lock` (leaf discipline; it takes the buddy / mmio / dma locks). As-built `docs/reference/125-weft.md` "The data drive" (the RX arm).** **Weft-6c-1 (the Loom data drive) ADDS the native-async zero-copy path over Loom -- NO new ABI (composes existing Loom+Weft, NET-THROUGHPUT 6): `kernel/loom.c::loom_submit_payload` detects a weft-bound `/net` data fid whose registered buffer is the flow's WHOLE shared ring (`buf == wb->burrow && buf_reg_len == wb->ring_size`; `dev9p_priv->weft` read `__atomic`-acquire) and routes the async LOOM_OP_READ/WRITE to `loom_build_weftio` (Tweftio off/len/dir) instead of `loom_build_read/write`; the slice validates via the REUSED `weft_binding_validate_rw(wb->guest_va + buf_off)` (whole-ring registration makes buf_off ring-base-relative; the guest_va cancels in the validator); `loom_payload_result`'s `op->weft` branch copies NOTHING (result = the Rweftio count -- the bytes moved in place). A non-ring buffer falls to the byte path (the section-4.8 hybrid); an in-ring OOB slice is `-EINVAL`. **The honest F_NOTIF realization**: a weft WRITE is a zero-copy send, but at v1.0 netd's h_weftio COPIES, so it is the io_uring SEND_ZC COPIED path -- a SINGLE terminal CQE (no LOOM_CQE_MORE), NO `loom_async_op.notif` field, no deferral; the deferred two-CQE (MORE then LOOM_CQE_F_NOTIF on the last {netd,NIC,ACK} holder clear) is the v1.x true-zero-copy seam (needs a netd-holds-the-page TX path + a netd->kernel holder-clear channel, neither at v1.0; the consumer reads LOOM_CQE_MORE to decide whether to wait -> forward-compatible). Prosecute (Weft-7/6c-3): the weft-binding read lifetime (`sp` pinned via spoor_ref -> the binding cannot be freed under the routing read [`dev9p_close`->`weft_binding_release` runs only at the last Spoor ref]; the `__atomic`-acquire matches the sync `dev9p_weft_try_rw` path); the whole-ring contract soundness (a whole-ring-size registration MUST start at Burrow offset 0 to fit one VMA, so buf_off is Burrow-relative even across a second mapping); I-30 (the offset is computed from the SQE snapshot + the kernel-private `wb->view`, never re-read from the shared ring); the buffer pin (#847, dropped at reap). `weft.tla` + `loom.tla` UNCHANGED (the copied case = `weft_notif_arm_copied` -> not-in-flight; the READ/WRITE drive = the symmetric `Consume`; all buggy cfgs re-ran green). Tests: `9p_client.loom_weft_{read,write,hybrid_fallback,oob_rejected}_e2e`. 965/965 + boot OK + 0 EXT at the 6c-1 landing; the native `libthyla_rs::net` API + the in-guest E2E are 6c-2, the focused buffer-lifetime audit + SMP gate are Weft-7 (6c-3). As-built `docs/reference/125-weft.md` "The Loom data drive".** **Weft-6c-2 (the native push/pop/wait API + the readiness edge) ADDS -- PURE USERSPACE, the kernel BYTE-UNCHANGED (a weft flow is just a Loom ring whose registered buffer is a `SYS_WEFT_MAP`'d region, which the 6c-1 kernel already detects): `usr/lib/libthyla-rs/src/net.rs::WeftFlow` (the Demikernel-shaped `push`/`pop`/`wait` over Loom -- `open(&TcpStream)` does `SYS_WEFT_MAP` + `read_ring_geom` + `Ring::setup` + register `(data fd, whole ring)`; `push(len)`/`pop(max)` stage a `LOOM_OP_WRITE`/`READ` SQE [`buf_off = payload_off`], `wait` does the io_uring submit-and-wait + parks on the **Loom CQ wait** [NET-THROUGHPUT 6: the park IS the CQ wait, no separate weft Rendez]; `tx_buf`/`rx_buf` for true zero-copy fill/read, `send`/`recv` convenience) + `usr/lib/libthyla-rs/src/weft.rs` (`read_ring_geom` [the geometry-mirror inverse: `ring_size = payload_off+payload_size`, the whole-Burrow size the routing requires] + the readiness-drive `ready_signal`/`ready_observe` -- the userspace mirror of the kernel `weft_ready_signal`/`weft_ready_observe`, the `weft_readiness.tla` store-buffer register-then-observe reproduced for the cross-Proc seq-cst story) + `usr/netd/src/server.rs::weft_recv_into_ring` (the `WEFT_READY_RX` edge on each real recv-into-ring -- the section-6 syscall-free busy-poll; netd ignores the wake-needed return since the client parks on the Loom CQE, not a weft Rendez -- the direct-park leg stays validated-not-wired, v1.x). **Single-in-flight** (the one payload region -> push XOR pop at a time; a 2nd before its `wait` -> `WouldBlock`); `WeftFlow` is `!Send`+`!Sync` (the raw `ring_va`) so it cannot race across threads; the borrow checker keeps `tx_buf`/`rx_buf` from outliving a submit. NO new ABI, NO new invariant, NO new spec module (composes Loom+Weft; the kernel byte-unchanged). Prosecute (Weft-7/6c-3): the single-in-flight buffer-lifetime contract (no in-flight ring mutation), the readiness cross-Proc seq-cst (the mirror reproduces the modeled orderings), the `register_buffers` whole-ring contract (`buf_reg_len == wb->ring_size`). v1.x seam: a `wait`-enter failure leaves `inflight` set (drop the flow); full-duplex + multi-op pipelining (payload-region partitioning). Proven in-guest by `net-echo`'s `weft_async_e2e` (`net-echo: weft-6c async E2E PASS` -- a bidirectional zero-copy round-trip over the resident `lo`: push verified server-side, pop verified out of `rx_buf`, the readiness seq advancing, single-in-flight enforced). 965/965 + boot OK + 0 EXT + spec gate (weft + weft_readiness + loom clean). The SOUND set (the cross-Proc #847 dual-refcount ledger, share_id consume-once + unforgeability, I-30 submit-time pin / ring-TOCTOU, I-29 count clamp, I-9 RX-defer + readiness seq-cst mirror, W^X, kernel-owned geometry) all traced + survived; spec gate GREEN (`weft.tla` 1412 + liveness + the 4 buggy cfgs; `weft_readiness.tla` + buggy_lost_wake; `loom.tla`). **The throughput bench (NET-THROUGHPUT s7/s8; #269 M6) -- CORRECTED 2026-06-21 (#290; an earlier claim here that "weft is ~10x slower ... pays per-op Loom+Tweftio overhead" was WRONG):** `netperf` MW mode (the M2 twin over `WeftFlow`), run HEAD-TO-HEAD with M2 at the SAME 256 KiB, instrumented to split data-move us from POLLOUT-readiness-stall us. Ground truth: (1) weft is NOT slower -- the aggregate is a dead heat (MW 2394 vs M2 2382 KiB/s); the earlier "~4.3x slower" was a pure SIZE artifact (M2 had been at 32 KiB, too small to ever fill the 4 KiB window + never stalled, vs MW at 256 KiB which did). (2) Weft's DATA-MOVE is ~2x faster (it moves the bytes in ~half the ops -- each push absorbs more than the window since the ring >> the window; per-op cost is EQUAL, so there is NO Loom/Tweftio penalty). (3) ~95% of BOTH aggregates is the POLLOUT readiness-stall (M2 100620 us, MW 101519 us -- identical, transport-INDEPENDENT): a bulk sender fills the 4 KiB window then waits for the writable edge via the dev9p.poll bridge -- the registered seam #221 (prompt-readiness-wake) / #288 (grow the window), NOT a weft cost. The bench output + comments were rewritten to the honest data-move-vs-readiness-stall breakdown (`weft_breakdown` for both M2+MW). 966/966 (+`weft.share_cap_gate`) + boot OK + 0 EXT + the SMP gate (default+UBSan x smp4/smp8 N>=10, 0 corruption). **THE WEFT ARC (#266) IS COMPLETE** -> next is REVENANT (#231, ASK first). Prosecute: I-45's guest-exposure half across the NEW cross-context authority path (a client must never reach the compositor's context, its probe pair, its screen, or a peer's imported resource — the import is compositor-side and bounded by hosting; the seam's raw device-global ids + the C-2c witness's guest-pixel seed window at `alloc_weave` before any client mapping); the in-flight clause (nothing may name a slot/BO resource past a response — the sync slot is what makes detach-before-unref sufficient; any fenced/pipelined blit reintroduces the drain obligation); the latch (a wrong box, a foreign format, a resource not held by the compositor context latches vrend for the process lifetime — every blit source must carry `comp_attached`/`comp_imported`, every request must come from `blit_request` under a CONFIRMED class convention, and the health verify must remain per-tick-after-blit with a CPU fallback in the same dispatch); the two-path invariant (the CPU path stays byte-identical wherever the GPU one does not apply — `screen_push` rects, chrome uploads on both kinds, `Held::Composed { cpu, gpu }` release semantics); `probe-screen`'s DoS posture (test-mode only, per-tick cap, no authority) and the sync request region widening (`REQ_REGION_LEN` 0x100 → 0x500 with the layout assert). Gates: `tools/warp-host.sh composed` (nine terms, both legs) + the S1/S2 sabotages recorded in GPU-DESIGN §4.5.11; `quake`; `decomp gl` (the BO composed arm's only driver). **C-4 (2026-08-17, GPU-DESIGN §4.5.12; same C-5 round, closed):** the health verify moved to a BUFFER pair (`warp_hprobe_build`, `comp_hprobe`; the texture pair `comp_probe` stays for the import witnesses and is the fallback), ISSUED once per `HEALTH_PERIOD` (4 ticks) and READ a period later (`comp_health_tick`: read-then-issue, never issue over a pending copy), so no step enqueues GPU work; a present-path cost census (`Cost`, `cost <kind> …` ctl lines, timers around each sync step and each dispatch). Prosecute: the verdict window (≤ 2 periods; the discovering present still CPU-composes in the same dispatch; `comp_gpu_latch` sticky; a synchronous `comp_ctx_health` at import cancels the pending deferred copy — it overwrites the pair — and must not leave a stale pending flag that reads a copy it never issued); the buffer pair's reachability (device-global ids: same containment argument as the texture pair — the per-verify repaint bounds a forged mark to one probe, and the buffer copy width is 4 bytes, never the client's); the issue step's own positive control (the poison is read back and must equal the token BEFORE the copy is asked for — without it a token upload that never reached the host makes the verdict vacuous; a failed control is UNKNOWN and latches); fail-closed on every errored step; the census must stay OFF every decision path (a timing cell is read only by the ctl; `cost_arm` is set inside `present` and consumed at its one call site); the `dbus-gl` lane is measurement-only (no gate may claim pixels under it). **C-5 close (2026-08-17):** `WarpBo.composable` (flags-0 `PIPE_TEXTURE_2D` or `PIPE_TEXTURE_RECT` [OSMesa's framebuffer target] `B8G8R8A8` one layer/level unsampled, recorded at create3d) gates BOTH the C-2c BO import (`comp_import_bo` SKIPs anything else — a witness copy from another shape is a command the renderer may refuse, and a refusal latches the SHARED compositor context: the client-reachable all-client degradation F1 closed) AND the `bo_gpu` blit arm (`GlAdopt.composable`; a `Y_0_TOP` BO would compose mirrored); `reconcile`'s structural arm drops every held region (a `Held::Composed` release after a layout change must never upload chrome bytes over a pane the new layout put under it); the GPU slot arm's failed `submit_blits` return sets `res_stale[slot] = true`. The Fable round's fixes (this close): **F1** every client ctx's probe is a BUFFER pair (`warp_hprobe_build` first, `warp_probe_build` the counted fallback -- `probe-texture` on the global ctl), the transfer form + copy width keyed on `CtxProbe.buffer` through ONE helper set shared with the compositor's health pair (`probe_upload`/`probe_readback`/`probe_copy_region`); **F5** one import witness per ctx per compositor tick (`WarpCtx.import_tick`/`import_pending`, replayed by `comp_replay_deferred_imports` in `frame_tick`); **F6** `C0-REJECT DONE` iff every C0 arm passed, else `INCOMPLETE(<arm>)`, hard-failed by `warp-reject.exp`; **F3** recorded on #171 (comment at `warp_probe_res_kind`). **F2 [P1] STAYS OPEN as Warp-C C-6**: the composed READBACK fallback (`transfer_from_3d_sync(g.dev_ctx, ...)` in the composed-GL present's `!done` arm) waits for the client's own queue on the console's dispatch thread and `fence_poisoned` structurally cannot guard it. Prosecute on any change: (a) the verify's every step must key on the pair's kind -- a texture-form transfer on a buffer resource (or the reverse) is a silent wrong-width op the response never reports; (b) the `verify` gate bounds only the CALLER's queue and the docs must say so -- any claim that it bounds a cross-client wait is false on the texture fallback; (c) the import rate limit must DEFER, never DROP (a dropped consent leaves a BO un-imported for its life); (d) the C0 completion token is a verdict -- a new C0 arm must feed `incomplete` or the scenario passes around it; (e) any readback on the sync slot under a CLIENT ctx inherits F2's exposure until C-6 lands. **Warp-C C-6 addendum (2026-08-18; GPU-DESIGN 4.5.13 + its AS-BUILT paragraph; the spec `tapestry_present.tla` `ComposeReadbackIssue/Complete` + `NoTornReadback` + `buggy_readback_free` landed FIRST at C-6a; the follow-up Fable round on the C-0d fixes + C-6 is OWED -- the C-0d Fable close was DIRTY):** F2 CLOSED as the fenced DEFERRED readback: `Comp::rb_request`/`rb_issue` (the composed-GL `!done` arm) issue `Gpu::transfer_from_3d_comp` on the fenced lane's ONE reserved slot (`COMP_FSLOT` = FENCED_SLOTS - 1; `alloc_fenced_slot`/`lane_exhausted`/`test_fenced_free` read the CLIENT pool `0..COMP_FSLOT`) tagged `FenceTag { comp, readback, ctx_pub: <the CLIENT's> }` and record `Comp.comp_rb`; `warp_service_fences` routes `tag.comp` to `comp_readback_retired` BEFORE `warp_pump_retires` (re-validate: same gen, scanout Composed, `gl_adoption` resolving to the same ctx/BO/res/va/w/h; else `rb_dropped`), then `comp_rb_pump` issues the next wanted incarnation off the gen-pinned `rb_wanted` FIFO (latest wins, one in flight compositor-wide); the readback is counted in the client ctx's `fences_in_flight` (retire safety) AND `comp_rb_in_flight` (subtracted in `warp_fenced_admit`; never `fence_signaled`); abandonment poisons the CLIENT's ctx via the tag; `Controlq::submit_and_wait`'s stale-wake deadline is `FENCE_ABANDON_MS` while `readback_in_flight()` (the `readback` bit on ANY tag -- a client's `transfer_3d(to_host=false)` or ours), sticky per wait; `Cost::ReadbackWait`; the global ctl `comp-rb` census. Prosecute on any change: (f) the tag's ctx_pub MUST stay the client's -- 0 is `warp_ctx_vindicate`'s no-slot sentinel and would let an abandoned-then-retired compositor readback `ctx_destroy` an unrelated live context, and the client's own vindication must wait for OUR poisoned slot (round-4 F1); (g) `comp_readback_retired` must run BEFORE the retire pump in the pass and must re-validate through `gl_adoption` (which refuses a retiring BO/ctx) -- reading `va` after a free is the I-40 device-write-into-freed-pages twin one step later; (h) `fences_in_flight` accounting must stay symmetric with `comp_rb_in_flight` (issue +1/+1; the pump -1/-1 on the tag) -- a miss strands the ctx's retire or shortens the client's share; (i) the reserved slot must never be borrowed from the client pool nor a client chain placed on it (`fenced_begin` vs `fenced_begin_comp`); (j) the deadline widening must key on the `readback` bit set at `transfer_3d(to_host=false)` AND on our own tag, and must stay sticky for the wait (a readback that retires mid-stall is exactly the wait's cause); (k) `comp-rb`'s keys stay distinct from the test-mode `abandoned` key (`parse_field` returns the first hit); (l) the gate's DEEP arm (positive control that the queue was deep) must stay -- LIVE alone is satisfied by a light queue; (m) `verify` E_AGAIN while our readback is in flight is documented behaviour, not a regression. Gates: `tools/warp-host.sh readback` (`warp-prove readback` ARM/DEEP/LIVE/DEADLINE + the F2B measurement) + `decomp`/`quake` unchanged (the blit arm untouched: `composed gpu >= 1`, `readback` n = 0 there) + `reject`/`prove`. **THE OWED ROUND RAN 2026-08-18 (OPUS 5 fallback -- Fable was out of credits and the spawn DIED mid-run; family diversity is INVERTED not forfeited, since C-6b was Fable-authored): 1 P0 / 3 P1 / 3 P2 / 3 P3, DIRTY on both triggers. Do-not-re-report = `memory/audit_c6b_opus_closed_list.md` (+ the five preambles it honoured). A FOLLOW-UP ROUND IS OWED on these fixes.** All ten fixed here (F7 as a recorded measurement debt, see below). **F1 [P0], PRE-EXISTING from the Warp-4 sync arm and in NONE of the five preambles**: a client-declared BO backing had no LOWER bound against its declared geometry (`wbo_create` bounds `size` only from above -- its comment states the one-directionality outright -- and `gl_adoption` checked only `w`/`h` equality), while `compose_cpu` reads `sw * sh_full * 4` from the BO's `va` with the dims taken from the SURFACE. So `create3d ... 512 512 ... size=4096` (page-aligned, under both caps, `Y_0_TOP` so it takes the readback arm) made the compositor read 1 MiB out of a 4 KiB mapping -- `weave_va_next` is a bump allocator, so the overrun is a neighbouring allocation (another client's pixels, painted onto the attacker's own pane) or unmapped VA (a FAULT in the process that IS the console). Fixed at BOTH ends: the exact bound at the read gate (`gl_adoption` refuses `b.size < b.w * b.h * 4` -- exact, because adoption already pins `b.w == s.w && b.h == s.h`) and a door check in `wbo_create` keyed on `VIRGL_FORMAT_B8G8R8A8_UNORM` alone (a general floor would refuse legitimate COMPRESSED textures at under a byte per texel; and it must NOT be keyed on `composable`, since the attack shape is B8G8R8A8 + `Y_0_TOP` = precisely NOT composable, which is how it reaches the readback arm). New `WDIAG_UNDERSIZED` = 17. **F2 [P1]**: C-6b dropped the readback's response check -- the pre-C-6b arm gated its compose on `transfer_from_3d_sync(...).is_ok()`, and the fenced form logged a non-OK `resp_type` and pushed the tag anyway, so the arm composed on an ERROR retire (painting whatever the backing held -- zeros on a fresh BO = the pane BLANKS) and counted it `rb-landed`. Fixed: `FenceTag.ok`, set in `drain` from `rt == VIRTIO_GPU_RESP_OK_NODATA` and mirrored in the test-mode hold-release replay (so the lever cannot prove the wrong thing); `comp_readback_retired` counts `rb-dropped`. **F3 [P1] (= the author's own SA-1, converged independently)**: a VINDICATED compositor readback bumped the CLIENT's dense `fence_signaled` -- the completion arm guards on `!tag.comp`, but a vindication is produced after abandonment TOOK the tag and `FenceVindication` carried only `ctx_pub` (the CLIENT's, per AS-BUILT 1). `warp_fence_wait` returns on `signaled >= seq`, so one ahead = every wait returns ONE FENCE EARLY for the ctx's life (the client may reuse a buffer the GPU is still writing; the winsys #210 clamp disables the throttle but does not fix the wait). Fixed: `FenceVindication.comp`, sourced structurally from `slot == COMP_FSLOT`. **F4 [P1]**: the shipped `warp-hold`/`warp-abandon` levers reached the RESERVED slot (both match on the tag's ctx = the client's), and `drain` cleared `fslot_since` ONE LINE BEFORE the hold check, so `reap_abandoned`'s `(Some(_), Some(t0))` test could never fire on a held slot -- the pin was INDEFINITE. Compositor-wide: every other client's readback frozen on stale frames, `readback_in_flight()` permanently true (the 500 ms sync deadline disabled process-wide), `warp_fences_pending()` permanently true (a ~1 kHz spin in the console for the life of the box). Refutes #178's "the worst a client can do is wedge its own ctx". Fixed: `COMP_FSLOT` exempt from the hold branch AND from a SCOPED `abandon_matching` (the UNSCOPED callers -- the deadline, a dead engine -- still reach it: a wedge that real is genuinely global), and `fslot_since` cleared AFTER the hold branch. **F5 [P2]**: the C-0d F5 import rate limit sat BELOW the C-5 F1 `!composable` skip, so `present-to` on a `Y_0_TOP` BO was an un-rate-limited `say!` + counter storm at 9P-write rate (`comp_imported` is never set for such a BO, so the `already` short-circuit can never damp it) -- the limit moved ABOVE the skip. **F6 [P2]**: `rb_wanted` was UNBOUNDED while its comment claimed "bounded by MAX_SURFACES" -- the dedup key was `(slot, gen)` and `gen` comes from a monotonic `gen_seq`, so a create/present/destroy loop appended one entry per cycle, and `contains()` is a linear scan (quadratic per-present cost while the reserved slot is held or poisoned, since the pump only runs `while comp_rb.is_none()`). Now keyed on the SLOT with the latest gen overwriting in place -- which is what "latest wins" already meant. **F7 [P2] -- NOT closed by code; recorded as a measurement debt (main#253)**: the gate cannot DISCRIMINATE a sabotage that removes the deadline widening (the certifying run measured `F2B max 267 ms` against `SUBMIT_DEADLINE_MS` = 500, so a build without the widening passes identically), and the deadline is evaluated ONLY AT A STALE WAKE while the stall it exists for raises no interrupts -- so whether the widening is load-bearing at all depends on unmeasured INTx sharing. GPU-DESIGN 4.5.13 now says so instead of "correct by construction". **F8/F9/F10 [P3]**: DEEP asserted a SUM over an unknown retire count -> now requires exactly one retire in the round; `cost readback-wait` charged ABANDONED readbacks against its own "per COMPLETED" contract -> the `cost_add` moved below the abandoned early return; the census line's placement vs the `w210` custody mirror -> the line was SHORTENED instead (the round's own second option), because `w210` and the unbounded tail are BOTH test-mode-only and moving down would drop the census out of a PRODUCTION tapestryd entirely. **main#247 (self-found)**: the census comment claimed the keys were "prefixed/distinct on purpose" while FOUR OF SIX shipped BARE -- `parse_field` takes the first whole-token hit, so a bare `issued` elsewhere would feed the gate's verdict arms the wrong counter WITHOUT erroring. Every key is now `rb-`-prefixed (`rb-issued rb-landed rb-dropped rb-coalesced rb-abandoned rb-slot`); `rb_census` + 149-warp.md updated. **Prosecute on any change**: (n) the adoption size guard is the LOAD-BEARING half of F1 -- `comp_readback_retired` is the only `Some(va)` caller of `blit_composed_pixels`, so any NEW reader of a BO backing with foreign geometry needs its own bound; (o) `FenceTag.ok` must be set on EVERY construction path incl. the test-mode replay, and `!ok` must DROP rather than compose; (p) `FenceVindication.comp` must stay sourced from the SLOT INDEX, the only thing that survives abandonment; (q) any new client-drivable lever that matches on a tag's ctx must exempt `COMP_FSLOT`, and nothing may clear `fslot_since` before a swallow. **The lesson: A DEVIATION IS SOUND FOR THE REASON IT WAS TAKEN AND DANGEROUS EVERYWHERE ELSE THAT READS THE SAME FIELD** -- AS-BUILT 1 was recorded and argued and its COST (every mechanism keyed on a tag's ctx now reaches the reserved slot) was never enumerated; prosecuting the documented deviation as a design change is what found F4. |
| MMU user-PTE clear + TLBI | `arch/arm64/mmu.c::mmu_uninstall_user_pte / mmu_uninstall_user_range`, `kernel/burrow.c::burrow_unmap` (call site) | P6 hardening #2 / audit F1 fix: symmetric counterpart to `mmu_install_user_pte`. Walks L0..L3, clears the leaf PTE, broadcasts `tlbi vaae1is + dsb ish + isb` for the inner-shareable domain. Called from `burrow_unmap` BEFORE the underlying pages are freed back to the buddy. Without it, stale PTEs + TLB entries persist after `SYS_BURROW_DETACH` -> content-sensitive silent corruption when buddy LIFO returns a different PA on re-attach (suspected AEGIS-256 / mallocng root cause). Invariants: idempotent on already-cleared PTEs and on never-faulted-in pages; `pgtable_root == 0` rejected with -1 (early-boot helper paths); VA bounds match `mmu_install_user_pte`'s (`vaddr >> 47 != 0` reject). Per-page cost dominated by `dsb ish` wait (microseconds); batched-TLBI optimization deferred. **No new spec** — prose validation in this row + the audit (see `memory/audit_p6_memory_model_hazards.md` F1) + the runtime test suite (599/599 PASS at landing). |
| Errno ABI surface + `snare:*` fault-note family | `kernel/include/thylacine/errno.h` (T_E_* registry; ABI-pinned by `_Static_assert`s to POSIX errno values), `kernel/include/thylacine/notes.h` (NOTE_NAME_SNARE_* family), `docs/ERRORS.md` (binding design + rollout) | P6 hardening #3a scripture (user-authorized 2026-05-26). Two coordinated registries: (a) Thylacine-wide errno header pins POSIX-aligned values (`T_E_INVAL == 22 == EINVAL`, etc.) so the pouch boundary-line patch's `[-4095,-2]->errno` passthrough works without translation; (b) `snare:*` thematic note-name family (`snare:segv`/`snare:bus`/`snare:align`/`snare:bti`/`snare:brk`/`snare:ill`/`snare:fpe`) for kernel-synthetic posts on EL0 unhandled fault, replacing the v1.0 FAULT_UNHANDLED_USER kernel extinction with per-Proc termination via `proc_fault_terminate`. **ABI commitments**: errno values are append-only; `snare:` prefix is reserved for kernel-synthetic posters (userspace SYS_POSTNOTE with a `snare:`-prefixed name MUST be rejected at `notes_post`). Adding/removing/renumbering any errno value or `snare:*` name is audit-bearing -- re-validate every syscall surface that emits the changed code + every fault-classification site. **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 spec-to-code broadening -- prose validation in `docs/ERRORS.md` + per-header `_Static_assert`s + the audit + the runtime test suite. Rollout staged: scripture LANDED first (no code); #3a impl uses the new constants; v1.x extends to the structured 64-bit exit_status + per-touch syscall errno upgrade. **APPEND (Menagerie build-arc step 4, 2026-06-15): `T_E_NODEV = 19` (POSIX ENODEV; the MENAGERIE §10 `T_E_DEVGONE`-class)** -- the Loom device-gone terminal CQE, executing the user-ratified MENAGERIE §10 scripture (the `T_E_CANCELED = 125` Loom-5 precedent: a Loom sub-chunk appends a POSIX-fixed errno under the adopted Loom scripture). Emitted ONLY by the async (Loom) 9P-completion path (`kernel/9p_client.c::client_mark_dead_locked(c, devgone)` -> `-P9_E_NODEV` CQE; the sync front-end + boot path keep `-P9_E_IO`). Validated by `specs/loom_devgone.tla` + I-29 (the device-gone extension) + the `9p_client.async_{peer_gone,mark_devgone}_posts_nodev_cqe` tests. |
| Memory-model defense-in-depth (F3 + F4 + F5) | `mm/phys.c::phys_init` (RAM cap), `mm/phys.c::alloc_pages` (KP_ZERO barrier), `arch/arm64/mmu.c::clear_page_then_free` + `l3_walk_and_free` / `l2_walk_and_free` / `l1_walk_and_free` / `proc_pgtable_destroy`, `kernel/proc.c::proc_free` (asid_free / pgtable_destroy ordering) | P6 hardening #2 follow-up: closes the P2/P3 findings the Opus auditor surfaced alongside the F1 P1 fix. **F3** (P2) caps the buddy zone at `mem_base + 8 GiB` so `alloc_pages` never hands out a PA the kernel direct map (`l1_directmap[1..8]`) cannot reach -- otherwise the KP_ZERO loop dereferences `pa_to_kva(pa)` past the direct-map cliff and takes an unhandled EL1 translation fault. A uart diagnostic fires only when DTB-reported RAM exceeds the cap (QEMU `-m 2048` default is dormant). To raise the cap, extend `l1_directmap` first. **F4** (P2) zeroes every Proc-pgtable page (L0/L1/L2/L3) before returning it to the buddy free-list, removing the speculative-prefetch info-leak vector of L3-leaf PA-of-user-page bit-patterns surviving into recycled buddy pages; and reverses `proc_free`'s ordering so `asid_free` (inner-shareable TLB flush) runs before `proc_pgtable_destroy`, narrowing the window in which a sibling-CPU speculative walker (carrying stale ASID TLB hints) could reach a recently-recycled sub-table page. Both orderings are correct -- the F4 order is the defense-in-depth choice. **F5** (P3) adds `dsb ish` after the KP_ZERO clearing loop so a second CPU mapping the same PA via a different VA sees zeroes (single-CPU v1.0 dormant; Phase 5+ SMP load-bearing). **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 spec-to-code broadening -- prose validation in this row + the audit (`memory/audit_p6_memory_model_hazards.md` F3 + F4 + F5) + the runtime test suite (599/599 PASS at landing, default + UBSan). |
| 9P client (pipeline restoration, #841) | `kernel/9p_client.c`, `kernel/9p_session.c`, `kernel/9p_transport.c`, `kernel/9p_attach.c`, SrvConn transport boundary (`kernel/srvconn.c` client send/recv + `kernel/9p_srvconn_transport.c`) | Wire protocol, fid lifecycle (I-11), tag uniqueness (I-10), no-lost-wakeup (I-9 per-rpc rendez), flow control, out-of-order completion. **#841 restores committed ARCH §21/§21.10 pipelining (elected-reader, Plan 9 `mountio`) from the R15-c F230 serial regression** -- the single per-client spinlock held ACROSS the blocking `recv` + the 30s per-op deadline that desynced the shared 9P byte stream (root cause of the stalk-3c-d "UBSan flake": corvus's post-pivot X-search `Tgetattr` over the joey-shared Stratum client busy-spins a CPU stratumd needs -> 30s `TSLEEP_TIMEDOUT` -> stream desync + transport ERROR-latch -> corvus exits -> kproc `wait_pid` wrong-pid extinction). As-built: lock NEVER held across `recv`; multi-in-flight tag-demux; per-rpc reply buffer; no per-op timeout (block until reply or ring-EOF; death-interruptible via #811). Public `p9_client_*` API unchanged (dev9p + consumers untouched). **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 broadening -- but `specs/9p_client.tla` (clean + the 4 buggy cfgs: tag_collision / ooo_match / fid_after_clunk / unbounded) is the pre-commit gate; prose validation in ARCH §21.10 + this row + the audit + the multi-in-flight runtime tests + the #841 UBSan/`forkstorm`/`capbare` repro GREEN across N boots under host load. Preempts the stalk-3c-d close. **As-built close**: the boot-hang root cause was a SEPARATE latent bug the removed 30s deadline had masked -- `kernel/devsrv.c::devsrv_close` honored `kernel_attached` on the SERVER endpoint, suppressing the ring EOF that wakes the no-timeout client (corvus's post-BadFormat Q11 teardown -> joey's Tclunk hung forever); FIXED by skipping teardown ONLY for the kernel-attached CLIENT endpoint (`(c->flag & CSRVCLIENT) && kernel_attached`) -- the SERVER endpoint always tears down (regression `devsrv.kernel_attached_server_close_eofs`, non-vacuous). Focused audit closed 1P1+1P2+3P3: F1[P1] reply-buffer UAF (read/readdir/readlink alias the per-op buffer `client_run` freed before the caller copies out) FIXED via `c->done_reply_buf` deferred-free; F2[P2] DIED-leaked outstanding slot **CLOSED by #845 (Tflush-on-abandon, see the addendum at the end of this row)**; F3/F5 reconciled in §21.10 (send is all-or-nothing-fail not block-on-room; demux-malformation also deaths the session); F4 accepted. Verified: default 704/704 + UBSan 3/3 + smp8 2/2 + capbare 12/12 under load + spec gate (clean + 4 buggy cfgs). **DIRTY-CLOSE RECURSION (the restructure changed a wait/wake protocol): round-2 prosecuted the death/hand-off SMP path round-1 only reasoned about + found F6[P1] (reader-role-loss strands a survivor when a hand-off target's Proc dies before assuming the role -> re-hand-off on the DIED path, be_reader-gated) + F7[P2] (be_reader never cleared on election-race-loss -> busy-spin -> clear before sleeping) + F8[P3] (destroy free now under c->lock), all FIXED; round-3 prosecuted the round-2 fixes + hand-off completeness -> CLEAN 0/0/0/0. CONVERGED clean over 3 rounds. Closed list: `memory/audit_841_closed_list.md`. Owed: a deterministic multi-in-flight/cross-Proc-death unit test (the SMP window the synchronous harness can't produce) -- lands with the A-5b multi-user workload. **#845 Tflush-on-abandon addendum** (`c31e7fd` + close): closes the F2 DIED-leaked slot via 9P Tflush -- the protocol's request-cancellation (Plan 9 mount driver + Linux v9fs). On CLIENT_WAIT_DIED the client sends `Tflush(oldtag=T)` under `c->lock`; T stays reserved (`outstanding[].awaiting_flush`) and is freed ONLY by its `Rflush`, NEVER by a late original reply (the I-10 reuse-race guard -- per 9P oldtag is not reusable until Rflush, so no stray/duplicate reply is mis-attributed to a reused tag). The flush is ownerless (no `inflight[F]`); a survivor's elected reader drains the Rflush via `demux_frame_locked`'s ownerless dispatch (`p9_session_dispatch_rmsg` routes the late-original = consume-without-clear, AND the Rflush = TFLUSH branch frees oldtag + the tail frees the flush tag). Fallback to the pre-#845 path if the flush can't be built/sent (no regression); a failed transport write latches the session dead. New wire `p9_build_tflush`/`p9_parse_rflush`; `struct p9_outstanding += awaiting_flush + flush_oldtag`. stratumd already Rflushes (lp9 + p9 servers) -> Thylacine-only. Audit (Opus R1) **0 P0 + 0 P1 + 1 P2 + 3 P3**: F1[P2] dup-Rflush-on-a-REUSED-flush-tag = the generic "server sends <=1 reply per tag" wire trust assumption (no per-tag wire generation in 9P; v1.0-UNREACHABLE with trusted stratumd/dev9p; the existing `awaiting_flush` guard is already maximal) -> CLOSED-with-justification + v1.x untrusted-server seam; F2[P3] test-strengthen (end-to-end reclaim-then-reuse) FIXED; F3[P3] late-Rwalk-skips-fid_bind divergence DOC; F4[P3] dead `out->kind` FIXED.
