Closed list: `memory/audit_845_closed_list.md`. **#349 send-side flow control addendum** (the on-device `go build` blocker's masking layer): a transiently-FULL c2s ring is BACK-PRESSURE, not session death. Pre-#349 `srvconn_client_send_frame`'s 0 ("ring full but ALIVE") collapsed to -1 and `client_run` marked the WHOLE shared dev9p session dead -- killing every in-flight op incl. a PEER's in-flight REVENANT text page-in (Twrite back-pressure -> `snare:bus` + an `-EIO` cascade across the joey-shared Stratum client). FIX: a `P9_TRANSPORT_EAGAIN` (-11) sentinel threaded `srvconn_transport_send` (n==0) -> `do_send` (propagated ONLY at sent==0; mid-frame EAGAIN stays fatal) -> `client_send_flow` (replaces the raw send in `client_run`): on EAGAIN, self-pump one s2c frame when no reader (drain -> server frees c2s; the deadlock-breaker) else PARK until a reader bumps `send_progress` / the session dies, then RETRY. The op's own tag is NOT on the wire (all-or-nothing send), so a self-pump only demuxes OTHER ops' replies. **MULTI-WAITER (R2-F1, the audit's headline)**: the client is shared across every Proc whose territory resolves through its mount, so N senders can be back-pressured at once -- a single `Rendez` is single-waiter (extincts on the 2nd sleeper, an unprivileged SMP-reachable PANIC on exactly the parallel-`go build` workload), so senders park on a `poll_waiter_list send_waiters_list` (each on its OWN stack `Rendez` via a `poll_waiter`; `client_send_progress_signal`/SA-1 wake them ALL; lock order c->lock -> list -> rendez; the audited poll.tla register-then-observe). SA-1: `client_mark_dead_locked` (the SOLE c->dead setter) wakes the list so no parked sender strands on a death. The 2 Tflush sends + the async `submit_async` keep their existing fail-close (#845 flush-fallback / the pre-existing #350 seam); `client_run` is the only EAGAIN-aware caller. **No new spec** (`9p_client.tla` models tag/fid/ooo, not flow control -- unchanged + re-gated). Tests: `9p_client.send_backpressure_self_pump` (PROVEN non-vacuous: pre-fix 1005/1006 FAIL, post-fix PASS) + `9p_client.send_backpressure_multi_waiter` (the F1 structural guard: 2 waiters coexist + both wake, no extinction). The `eagain_budget` knob added to `kernel/9p_transport_mq.c`. **Audit CONVERGED CLEAN over 2 rounds (Opus-4.8-max + concurrent self-audit each; MODEL(end)=claude-opus-4-8 both)**: R1 0 P0 / **1 P1** / 1 P2 / 1 P3 -> DIRTY (the self-audit MISSED F1, the formal round caught it -- the exact value of running both): F1[P1] the single-waiter `send_rendez` extinction (FIXED -> multi-waiter `poll_waiter_list`); F2[P2] the test covered only the self-pump branch (FIXED -> the multi_waiter test); F3[P3] self-pump recv-error latches-dead (sound, doc'd). R2 on the multi-waiter restructure CONVERGED CLEAN 0/0/0/1 (the P3 = the multi_waiter test proves the mechanism but not the live park `sleep()` loop -- the owed deterministic two-thread-same-client harness, shared with #841/#845/Loom). Closed list: `memory/audit_349_closed_list.md`. v1.x SEAM: #350 (the async `submit_async` EAGAIN-as-fatal -- same class, pre-existing, the clean fix needs the async on_complete-double-fire design). **#375 spill addendum (the task-#50 root fix, 2026-07-13): `client_send_flow` spills the frame OUT of the shared `out_buf` at the FIRST back-pressure.** The pump/park drops `c->lock`, so a peer could legally rebuild `out_buf` and the retry then pushed the PEER's bytes: an equal-length peer frame (two msize-clamped F1 flush Twrites -- the dominant concurrent cold-build shape) went out as a clean DUPLICATE with the parked frame LOST; the duplicate's second reply landed on the freed-then-REUSED tag as a WRONG reply (an Rlerror parses cleanly for ANY op -- 9P has no per-tag wire generation; the #845-F1 "server sends <=1 reply per tag" trust assumption was VOIDED by the client itself being the duplicate source), so a stray Rlerror(ENOENT) poisoned a live Twalkgetattr for an EXISTING component into a persistent negative dentry -> the task-#50 S3 failure cluster (spurious mkdir/create ENOENTs across $WORK+GOCACHE + a stray write EIO, ~10% of cold builds once F1's 128 KiB flushes made concurrent equal-length back-pressured sends common); an unequal-length peer frame failed `p9_transport_send`'s size==len validation -> session death. The spill (kmalloc+`client_copy` at the first EAGAIN, retry from the private copy, freed at return) means `out_buf` is NEVER re-read after the lock has dropped -- which also makes the DIED-path Tflush + async-clunk `out_buf` reuse safe against a parked sender by construction; the all-or-nothing EAGAIN contract (zero bytes pushed) is what makes retry-from-spill exact. Prosecute on any change: no path may re-read `out_buf` after `client_pump_or_park_locked` has run; the spill must be taken BEFORE the first park; spill-OOM fails closed. Regression `9p_client.send_backpressure_spill_survives_outbuf_reuse` (the mq scribble knob models the peer build inside the dropped-lock window -- deterministic, single-threaded; PROVEN non-vacuous: pre-fix 1103/1104 FAIL, post-fix 1104/1104). Spec gate re-run GREEN (clean + 5 buggy cfgs). **CLOSED CLEAN (Fable-5-max, MODEL start==end; `memory/audit_375_spill_closed_list.md`): 0 P0 / 0 P1 / 2 P2 / 2 P3, NOT dirty.** The fix is SOUND+COMPLETE (the sole post-drop out_buf re-read anywhere is the NOTAG handshake -- private-client by construction, #360) and the #50 root chain is PROVEN by the code at every mechanical link (Rlerror passes for ANY outstanding kind with only the tag echo validated -- `9p_session.c:955`; negative install only on a clean -T_E_NOENT -- `dev9p.c:765`); confirmed empirically by the N=10 post-fix S3 loop (10/10 clean, 0 clusters). Both P2s are PRE-EXISTING (NOT #375 regressions), tracked: **F1 -> task #52** (never-sent op leaks outstanding[tag] on a live shared session; 3 members) + **F2 -> task #53** (the DIED-path Tflush/abandon_async treat EAGAIN[-11<0] as a broken stream -> whole-shared-session death under congestion, the #349 collapse on a different send path, reachability grown by F1+#50). Both P3 (the field-contract comment + the 47-9p-client.md over-broad claim) FIXED. **#52/#53 addendum (the non-client_run send-path error dispositions, 2026-07-13): LANDED.** #52 (never-sent tag-slot leak, 3 members): `client_send_flow` now returns a distinct `CLIENT_SEND_NEVER` for the zero-bytes-pushed exits (self-dying / dead-observed / spill-OOM -- the all-or-nothing transport contract is what makes them reclaimable) and the callers + the reply_buf-OOM path reclaim the tag via the new `p9_session_abort_unsent` (never-sent => the server never saw the tag => I-10-safe immediate reuse; fail-soft on inactive/awaiting_flush); the spill-OOM no longer latches the shared session; `clunk_async` captures its tag BEFORE the send (the #375 out_buf discipline). #53 (DIED-path Tflush + abandon_async EAGAIN-as-fatal): both flush sends now treat `P9_TRANSPORT_EAGAIN` as back-pressure -- `p9_session_flush_rollback` frees the never-sent flush tag + clears the victim's awaiting_flush, restoring the pre-#845 ownerless reclaim (a dying thread must not park; an abandoner is in a teardown; latching the shared session was the #349 collapse on a different send path); only a genuine break latches. Prosecute on any change: the never-sent classification must remain exactly the zero-bytes-pushed set (a misclassified partial-push reclaim breaks the stream + I-10); abort_unsent/flush_rollback stay fail-soft; the clunk tag capture stays pre-send. Regressions: `9p_session.abort_unsent_reclaims_tag` + `9p_session.flush_rollback_restores_victim` (incl. the F1 fid-9 clunk-succeeds + the R2-F1 fid-11 flush-less legs, both revert-probed) + `9p_client.abandon_async_eagain_keeps_session_alive` (revert-probed: 1106/1107 FAIL pre-fix). **CLOSED CONVERGED over 2 Fable rounds (MODEL start==end both): R1 0 P0/0 P1/1 P2/1 P3 -> F1 [P2] the rollback's cleared awaiting_flush made the victim count LIVE in any_outstanding_on_fid and REFUSED the #294 cancel-then-close Tclunk (netd slot leak reopened + tag accumulation) -> FIXED via the `abandoned` bit (the #294 exclusion extended: owner gone, will not act on the fid; UNLIKE awaiting_flush the late original reply frees the tag -- no Tflush is in flight so no Rflush to wait for); F2 [P3] fail-closed pre-send exits. R2 on the fixes 0/0/0/2 P3, NOT dirty: R2-F1 the flush-BUILD-failure sibling (pool-full at abandon) -> `p9_session_mark_abandoned` at both flen<=0 fallbacks; R2-F2 the any_outstanding_on_fid caller list refreshed to SEVEN (walkgetattr/setattr/rename joined post-#294 -- keep it current). RESIDUAL tracked: netd drops a cancelled parked read's tag without replying (task #56). The exclusion's soundness: the only reachable late-reply fid mutation is a walk-family fid_bind on a FRESH monotonic (never-reissued) new_fid.** 1107/1107 + spec gate (clean + 5 buggy cfgs) + SMP gate 40/40 0-corruption (after the [[feedback-mv-restore-mtime-trap]] stale-object false-fail was diagnosed) + the reduced amplifier gate on the R2 close. `memory/audit_5253_closed_list.md`. **#89/8c-3 addendum (the debug-stop reader-role release; the AUTHORITATIVE home is the debug-fs audit-trigger row + ARCH §25.4 8c-3 addendum)**: the elected reader held `reader_active` across the 8c-2 debug-stop detour-park, freezing survivor Procs sharing the SYSTEM Stratum client for the stop duration. FIX in `client_wait` + `client_handoff_reader_locked` (+ the `sleep()`/`tsleep()` detour + `Thread.stop_unwinds` + `p9_rpc.owner`): the reader's recv is FRAME-ATOMIC (the 8c-3 holotype F1 [P1] correction): `reader_recv_frame` (a wrapper) holds `Thread.stop_no_park` for the whole recv + sets `stop_unwinds = (got==0)` per-chunk, so a stop UNWINDS only at a frame boundary [`SLEEP_INTR` -> `client_wait` releases + hands off the role, skipping debug-stopped owners so it lands on a survivor, then parks role-free + re-elects] and BLOCKS THROUGH mid-frame [the `sleep`/`tsleep` detour falls through to register+sched when `stop_no_park` is set + `stop_unwinds` clear]. A mid-frame unwind would discard the consumed partial frame -> a survivor reads the tail as a header -> shared-session desync (delivery is CHUNKED; `srvconn_client_recv` tsleeps on an empty ring, reachable mid-frame under pipelining depth >= 2 + ring pressure -- the abandoned simple-unwind's F1 bug). **F2**: ALL FOUR `reader_active` sites handle the stop-return -- the self-pump + `p9_client_reader_pump_once` skip `client_mark_dead_locked` on a `client_stop_pending` unwind; `client_send_flow` (spill `out_buf` FIRST, #375) + `client_drain_until_free_tag` PARK a stopped sender at their loop top (else a stop-unwound self-pump drains nothing -> the send EAGAINs forever -> the debugger's stop HANGS); only `p9_client_reader_pump_once_deadline` is unguarded (kproc-only: dev9p_poll + loom SQPOLL, `t->proc == NULL`, detour-immune). The IDENTICAL death-path mid-frame unwind (the `#811` die-check below the detour is UNCHANGED) is a PRE-EXISTING #841/#811 latent -> **task #90** (its fix narrows #811 -> a §28 refinement, ARCH §8.8.1.1). **#90 DESIGN (block-through, mirror 8c-3; signed off + design-voted 2026-07-19):** the die-check (`thread_die_pending`, sleep()/tsleep()) becomes frame-atomic for the elected reader -- unwinds a dying reader ONLY at got==0, BLOCKS THROUGH mid-frame (finishes the frame, bounded by the trusted server per CF-3 B), REUSING the existing `stop_no_park`+`stop_unwinds` (the guard widens to `!stop_no_park \|\| stop_unwinds`; NO new Thread field -- both are already `= (got==0)`) so a mid-frame reader death never desyncs the survivor's shared stream. Spec-first RE-ENABLED: `specs/reader_frame.tla` model-first (NoDesync + EventuallyUnwinds + a mid-frame-unwind buggy cfg). v1.x LIVENESS SEAM: an untrusted/hung server sending a partial frame then stalling hangs a dying reader mid-frame. The AUTHORITATIVE prosecution copy is the ARCH §25.4 row. Prosecute on any change: the handoff must skip an `owner->debug_stop_req != 0` op (else a stopped sibling strands a survivor); a stop must unwind ONLY at `got==0` (mid-frame blocks through); both flags cleared before the role-free park; DeathWinsOverStop (the die-check precedes the stop check AND still unwinds a mid-frame block-through). NO new spec (below `9p_client.tla` + `debug_stop.tla`, both re-verified GREEN; the block-through is kproc-immune, so it has no deterministic kproc regression -- the SMP gate + reasoning are its durable coverage). Regression `9p_client.handoff_skips_debug_stopped_owner` (revert-probed). The AUTHORITATIVE prosecution copy is the debug-fs audit-trigger row + ARCH §25.4. Closed list `memory/audit_8c3_closed_list.md`. |
| Pipe wait/wake | `kernel/pipe.c` | Missed-wakeup-freedom (I-9 specialized to pipe two-direction state machine); per-pipe lock + rendez discipline. Modeled in `specs/pipe.tla`. **#96 addendum (CL-5 storm; audit-light):** `devpipe_stat_native` (POSIX `fstat` on a pipe -> `T_S_IFIFO \| 0600`, size 0) + a monotonic `qid.path` stamped into BOTH ends at `pipe_create` (one pipe, one inode). Read-only + creation-time only -- the ring, the locks, and the rendez discipline are BYTE-UNCHANGED, so `pipe.tla` is unperturbed. Prosecute on any change: `.seekable` must stay false (the RW-4 R2-F2 decoupling -- adding `stat_native` must NOT re-enable lseek/pread on a pipe; `sys_prw.pipe_not_seekable` pins it); `size` must stay 0 (a buffered-count report races the peer by construction); the qid stamp must stay inert outside `stat_native` (nothing else keys on a pipe's `qid.path` -- the Larder is dev9p-only, the cons/pts qid flags are dc-gated). Why it existed: no pouch program had a pipe on fd 0/1/2 at startup until `make -jN` (`get_bad_stdin`) put one there, and clang treats a non-EBADF fstat failure on a standard fd as fatal -- the CL-4 layer-3 shape, third door. Regression `pipe.fstat_reports_fifo` (revert-probed). The remaining `stat_native`-less Devs were swept: only `devnotes` is also EL0-reachable (task #97); `null`/`zero`/`full`/`random` are served by `devdev`, and `devcap`/`devnone` are mounted nowhere. |
| poll | `kernel/poll.c` | Missed-wakeup-freedom across N fds (I-9); register-then-observe + poll-hook list lifetime. Modeled in `specs/poll.tla`. |
| Notes / signals | `kernel/notes.c`, `kernel/devnotes.c`, `kernel/include/thylacine/notes.h`, `kernel/proc.c` (synthetic `child_exit` post in `exits`), `kernel/pipe.c` (synthetic `pipe` post on write-to-closed), `arch/arm64/exception.c` (EL0-return-tail delivery) | Delivery ordering (I-19); async-safety (delivery only at zero-lock EL0-return tail); N-2 consumed-exactly-once across handler + fd-read paths; N-3 in_handler re-entrancy guard; N-4 `kill` non-catchable (a cross-Proc `kill` cascades via `proc_group_terminate` WHATEVER the thread count -- SYS_EXIT_GROUP, ARCH §7.9.1 / I-24, tasks #809 + #241; it was multi-thread-only until #241). **No `specs/notes.tla`** per the 2026-05-23 spec-to-code broadening — prose validation in `notes.h` + the audit + the runtime test suite are the rigor. See ARCH §7.6.1-§7.6.8 for the design + invariants. **#97 addendum (the #96 sibling; audit-light):** `devnotes_stat_native` (POSIX `fstat` on a notes fd -> `T_S_IFCHR \| 0666`, SYSTEM-owned, size 0, `blksize = sizeof(struct note_record)`). Read-only; the queue, the wait/wake discipline, and the delivery path are BYTE-UNCHANGED, so I-19 / N-2..N-4 are unperturbed. Why it existed: `devnotes` had a 9P `.stat` (returning -1) but no `.stat_native`, so `SYS_FSTAT` fell through the NULL-slot arm -- the same shape #96 hit on a pipe, where it killed every concurrent `make -j4` job silently (clang treats a non-EBADF fstat failure on fds 0/1/2 as fatal). Prosecute on any change: `.seekable` must stay false (the RW-4 R2-F2 decoupling -- adding `stat_native` must NOT re-enable lseek/pread on a stream); `size` must stay 0 (a pending-note count races the reader by construction); `blksize` must stay `sizeof(struct note_record)` (NOT advisory -- `devnotes_read` REJECTS a smaller buffer); and **`qid_path` must keep bits 40 and 41 CLEAR** -- pouch decodes is-a-pts as `S_ISCHR`+bit 40 and is-a-cons as `S_ISCHR`+bit 41, so under the new `S_IFCHR` posture those clear bits are the only thing keeping a notes fd from reading as a TERMINAL (the same reason devdev withholds the cons flag from `consctl`). The qid is deliberately NOT stamped per-open (unlike a pipe's): the Spoor is a stateless marker whose queue resolves from the running Thread, so there is no per-instance object to name and the opener's identity would be the wrong thing to record -- the `/dev/tty` precedent, one identity with per-process content. Regression `notes.fstat_reports_chr` (drives `spoor_stat_native`, poisons the struct first, and asserts both flag bits clear). **#15/#236 addendum (the per-note default action; ABI-SEMANTICS, prosecute hard):** `SYS_NOTED(NDFLT)` was unconditionally `exits(name)` for every note; it now dispatches on `notes_default_action(name)`, a pure lookup of the new `dfl` column of `g_known_notes` -- TERMINATE (`interrupt`/`kill`/`pipe`/`tty:quit`/`tty:hup`), STOP (`tty:susp`), IGNORE (`child_exit`/`tty:winch`/`tty:cont`). Files: `kernel/notes.c` (the column, `notes_default_action`, the rewritten `notes_noted_default` -- **no longer `noreturn`**), `kernel/include/thylacine/notes.h` (`enum note_default`), `kernel/syscall.c` (`sys_noted_handler`'s NDFLT arm), `kernel/proc.c` + `proc.h` (NEW `proc_job_stop_self`), `usr/lib/pouch/patches/0021-pouch-pty.patch` (SIG_DFL `SIGTSTP` -> NDFLT). **Prosecute on any change:** (1) the STOP arm restores BEFORE arming, so a failed restore leaves no stop armed, and the resume lands on the interrupted PC rather than in a returned-from handler frame; (2) DEATH STILL WINS -- `el0_return_die_check` precedes `el0_return_stop_check` and the park loop re-checks `group_exit_msg`, which is the ONLY enforcement of `pty_stop.tla`'s `StopJob` `~gflag` guard (the set site does not check it -- inherited PTY-1f posture, deliberate, do not "fix" without moving the spec); (3) `proc_job_stop_self` KEEPS the POSIX orphan rule (an orphaned group's stop is discarded -- applying it hangs the Proc forever, since the `proc_become_zombie_locked` hup+cont rescue only fires for a group orphaned LATER) and DROPS the catchability gate (`proc_tty_susp_would_stop_locked` already ran and is what routed the note to the handler -- re-asking it would refuse the stop because a handler exists, which IS the bug); (4) the note is already consumed at delivery, so no disposition re-queues it (a stale susp surviving a cont would re-suspend a resumed program); (5) `notes_name_terminate_latch` now derives its class from the SAME column -- behaviour-identical on all nine rows, and `pipe`'s missing latch is a KNOWN OPEN disagreement with two doc rows (task #237), preserved deliberately, NOT to be laundered as designed. Regressions `notes.default_action_table` (every row + both readers) and `notes.ndflt_dispatch` (STOP arms with a handler installed; child_exit and tty:winch do NOT stop; the orphan discard) -- all four legs sabotage-verified to go red. **#241 addendum (N-4 vs the job stop; DEATH PATH, prosecute hard):** `postnote_walk_cb` (`kernel/syscall.c` -- now IN SCOPE for this row) used to route `kill` through `proc_group_terminate` only when `live_threads > 1` and let a SINGLE-thread target fall through to `notes_post`, trusting the EL0-return tail's non-catchable-kill branch to pick it up. `kill` arms no terminate latch (`notes_name_terminate_latch` returns 0 for `NOTE_BIT_KILL`), so the queued note woke NOTHING -- and a job-stopped target parked in `el0_return_stop_check` leaves only via `group_exit_msg` / `!proc_stop_requested` / `thread_die_pending`, none of which a latchless queued kill satisfies. `SYS_POSTNOTE` returned SUCCESS and the Proc lived forever: **the job stop caught the uncatchable note.** PRE-EXISTING (reachable via a native coreutil ^Z'd in a pts fg group, or `/proc/<pid>/ctl suspend`); #15 widened the population to every ^Z'd pouch program. Fixed by dropping the thread-count gate. **NOT a semantics change, and the argument is load-bearing:** `thread_exit_self`'s become_zombie arm derives status from `group_exit_msg` with the same `"ok" -> 0 / else -> 1` collapse `exits()` uses, so the outcome is exit_msg "killed" / status 1 either way -- and `/proc/<pid>/ctl`'s `kill` verb has ALWAYS dispatched via `proc_group_terminate` uniformly (`devproc.c`), so this makes the two surfaces agree rather than inventing a third behaviour. **Prosecute on any change:** (1) the SELF-post fast path in `sys_postnote_handler` KEEPS its `live_threads > 1` gate deliberately -- the posting thread is by definition RUNNING and the tail delivers notes BEFORE `el0_return_stop_check`, so a self-kill cannot be swallowed; do not "unify" it without a defect to point at; (2) `proc_group_terminate` runs under `g_proc_table_lock` here (held by `proc_for_each`) and that is REQUIRED by its #811 death-wake contract, not incidental; (3) the `live_peers != 0` re-enqueue arm in `notes_deliver_at_el0_return` stays as defense-in-depth for kernel-internal posters + the R3-F3 `SYS_THREAD_SPAWN` TOCTOU -- no `SYS_POSTNOTE` kill reaches the queue any more. Regression `notes.kill_terminates_single_thread`: asserts `group_exit_msg` SET (positive -- fails closed on any fixture defect) AND no kill queued (negative), with a same-shape `interrupt` CONTROL differing in exactly one variable (the note name) proving the queue counter can actually see a queued note, so the zero is a measurement rather than a broken reader. Sabotage-verified: restoring the `live_threads > 1` gate reddens it on its own assert. **#240 addendum (the freshness premise on #15's DEFERRED stop; prosecute hard):** #15 split "decide to stop" from "apply the stop" -- the decision is taken at POST (`job_stop_cb`'s CAUGHT arm) and applied at the target's own `SYS_NOTED(NDFLT)`, a full EL0 handler execution later, interruptible by preemption, page faults and syscalls. `proc_job_stop_self` revalidated exactly ONE premise (orphanhood). A cont landing inside that gap reached `proc_job_resume_one_locked`, found `job_stop_req` still 0, and returned having done NOTHING -- after which the NDFLT applied the very stop the cont was cancelling. Concretely: `pts_free` destroys the registry entry (`pts_clear_locked`) and THEN runs `pts_teardown_fan`'s hup-then-cont, which the PTY-1 close named as THE carrier-loss guarantee; with the entry already gone the shell's later `fg` gets `-T_E_NOENT`, so the job is stopped, the rescue ran and did nothing, and nothing can ever resume it. Fixed by a SECOND premise: `Proc.susp_stop_armed` (new; the 4-byte pad between `name[]` and `exe_path`, NO struct growth -- the `exe_path == 352` assert is the control), SET by `notes_arm_susp_stop_locked` on the commit of any STOP-disposition note inside `notes_post` (both the enqueue and the coalesce paths), CLEARED at the TOP of `proc_job_resume_one_locked`, READ in `proc_job_stop_self` under `g_proc_table_lock`. **Prosecute on any change:** (1) THE PLACEMENT IS THE FIX -- the clear must precede that function's `job_stop_req == 0` early return; moving it after leaves the defect intact while the code reads as correct, and the fix becomes vacuous; (2) the arm is anchored at POST, not at delivery, which is what covers the cont-BEFORE-delivery window (a target blocked in a syscall is posted a susp, the carrier drops, and only then does the note reach the handler) -- an at-delivery snapshot closes only the documented cont-after-delivery half while looking complete; (3) it lives in `notes_post` rather than at the single susp poster so coverage is structural, and because the failure direction of a MISSED arm is a stop applied against a cont -- i.e. it reads green; (4) a flag, not a queued-`tty:cont` predicate: that alternative was checked and WOULD cover the teardown case, but a notes-fd reader can consume the cont, a mask can defer it and a coalescing change could drop it, silently re-opening the window; (5) it must NOT become a one-way latch -- a susp posted after a cont re-arms, or a resumed job could never be suspended again. Regression `notes.ndflt_stop_discarded_after_cont`: a POSITIVE CONTROL first (no cont -> the stop DOES apply, differing from leg A in exactly the one step), then leg A (cont after delivery), leg B (cont before delivery), leg C (susp after cont re-arms). THREE sabotages, each reddening a DIFFERENT named assert: clear-after-early-return -> leg A's disarm assert; gate removed -> leg A's discard assert; arm site removed -> the positive control AND `notes.ndflt_dispatch`'s new post-armed assert. That last one also required clearing the flag before the fan in `ndflt_dispatch`, since leg (1) left it set and the assertion would otherwise have been satisfied by the stale value. **#240 also re-armed a leg it would otherwise have hollowed out:** `ndflt_dispatch`'s orphan leg asserts `job_stop_req == 0`, a NEGATIVE -- a fresh Proc has `susp_stop_armed == 0`, so the new gate discards that stop too and the leg would have stayed green with the orphan rule deleted outright. It now arms the orphan explicitly so the orphan rule is the only thing that can discard. **#247 addendum (exec drops the in-handler LATCH; NEW FILE-SCOPE: `kernel/proc.c`'s exec reset):** the exec reset block cleared `handler_va`, dropped the sigtab (by `kfree` then; reset in place since #254) and zeroed `note_mask` -- POSIX's "a successful exec resets the caught-signal dispositions" -- but NOT `self->in_handler`. `notes_deliver_at_el0_return` returns early on that flag, and the gate sits ABOVE both the V-6c phenotype branch and the `handler_va` branch, so a thread that execs from INSIDE a note handler carries the latch into the new image and that image receives no further note. `kill` is the sole exception (the kill peek precedes the gate -- the R2-F7 fix), which is the only reason this was a deafness bug and not an unkillable-process bug. A `SYS_NOTED` from the new image would additionally pass the `in_handler` gate and restore the OLD image's saved registers into the exception frame -> eret to a stale PC/SP in a fresh address space (contained: faults -> snare:segv -> per-Proc termination, not an extinction). REACHABLE by an ordinary shape -- execve is async-signal-safe and catch-SIGHUP-then-re-exec is the standard supervisor idiom. PRE-EXISTING; found by the #240 self-audit, not by a failing test. Fixed by extracting `proc_exec_drop_image_state(p, self)` (the note-side reset, now ONE place) and adding `self->in_handler = false`. **Prosecute on any change:** (1) keep the reset in the single helper -- splitting it across the helper and the call site is precisely how the field was missed; (2) the helper carries NO single-thread guarantee of its own, it inherits `proc_exec_alone` re-checked under the lock at the swap, so it must not be called from anywhere that lacks it; (3) clearing the one flag IS the whole fix -- `note_handling_name` and the `note_saved_*` block are unreachable while `in_handler` is false, the invariant `notes_noted_restore` states when it deliberately leaves the name buffer intact, so do not add a scrub and do not weaken that invariant; (4) the doc claim in `83-pouch-signals.md` that `in_handler` has exactly N writers is load-bearing for the FP save/restore pairing argument -- it now reads FOUR, and a fifth writer must be reconciled with that argument rather than just added. Regression `proc.exec_drops_image_note_state`: arms handler_va + note_mask + in_handler, ASSERTS each is armed first (the precondition is what makes the four "and now it is zero" post-conditions measurements rather than starting values -- it earned its keep immediately, catching that `NOTE_BIT_*` are bit INDICES not masks, so the bare constant set the mask to ZERO and the post-condition would have passed vacuously), then drives the helper. Sabotage-verified: removing the in_handler clear reddens THAT assertion and only that one. **KNOWN COVERAGE GAP, tracked:** nothing tests the N-3 suppression itself -- that `in_handler == true` actually blocks delivery -- so this fix's severity argument rests on reading the gate, not on a test. See the task queue. **#251 addendum (the tty:susp catchability gate is PHENOTYPE-BLIND; found by the 2026-08-14 round-2 re-audit):** `proc_tty_susp_would_stop_locked` asked the disposition question with one load, `handler_va != 0`. That is right for a native Proc and wrong for a phenotyped one: a Linux guest never calls `SYS_NOTIFY`, so its `handler_va` is 0 whatever it asked for, and its SIGTSTP disposition lives in the per-Proc `sigtab`. Both a phenotype **handler** (the guest's handler never runs; it is stopped instead) and an explicit phenotype **SIG_IGN** (stopped despite ignoring the signal) were read as uncaught. `notes_post`'s V-6b SIG_IGN discard cannot save the latter, because the uncaught arm stops the Proc WITHOUT ever calling `notes_post` -- a decider that runs INSTEAD of the post has no upstream drop to rely on. Fixed by NEW `notes_proc_default_applies(p, name)` (`kernel/notes.c`, header-declared), which answers for both phenotypes and which every disposition decider now routes through. **VERIFIED, not assumed:** SIGTSTP maps to `VIV_SIGNOTE_TTY_SUSP` and owns that note EXCLUSIVELY (`viv_signal_owns_note_exclusively`), and the note is deliverable -- so `vivarium_sigaction_decide` admits BOTH a handler (with `SA_RESTORER`, which musl always supplies) and SIG_IGN (which needs no restorer at all, the same trivially-reachable shape as the `signal(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN)` idiom the code names). **The V-8 F2 shape, recurred:** `notes.c` already documents that this exact test is wrong for a phenotyped Proc -- that finding fixed the SIBLING site (`notes_arm_intr_terminate_locked`) and left this one, the standing "the fix that exists on site N stops you asking about site N+1" failure. **SEVERITY, and the criterion was initially posed wrong:** filed P1 on the reasoning that it is P0 iff a shipped Linux binary sets a SIGTSTP disposition. Both shipped Alpine busybox binaries DO carry ash job-control code (measured: the `can't access tty; job control turned off` string is `setjobctl`'s), but that is the wrong discriminator -- a disposition is inert unless a `tty:susp` can REACH it, and `proc_job_stop_pgrp` has exactly ONE production caller (`kernel/pts.c`, `SYS_TTY_SIGNAL`). So the chain needs an interactive Linux guest in a pts foreground group, and every DISTRO gate runs `sh -c` (non-interactive -- ash does not call `setjobctl`), with the interactive container path still blocked by other missing rows (getdents64, O_CREAT). Wrong today, unreached today, P0 the moment the interactive container shell works. **Prosecute on any change:** (1) the predicate must answer HANDLER-or-IGNORED, not just handler -- `notes_proc_has_live_handler` deliberately covers only the handler because its caller (the terminate latch) runs BELOW the SIG_IGN drop; a caller that runs INSTEAD of `notes_post` needs both, and conflating the two predicates re-opens the SIG_IGN leg; (2) routing a phenotype SIG_IGN down the CAUGHT arm is CORRECT and is NOT the F2 leak -- `notes_post` drops it at generation and returns success, so nothing is queued (the round-2 finding's own suggested fix said otherwise; it was checked against the code and is wrong); (3) the phenotype is the gate, not the presence of a sigtab -- a native Proc with a table hung off it must still take the default. Regression `notes.susp_gate_reads_phenotype_sigtab`: a POSITIVE CONTROL first (phenotype + SIG_DFL still stops -- one sigtab row from leg A, and without it all three negatives are satisfied by a fixture that cannot stop anything), then leg A (sigtab handler), leg B (SIG_IGN), leg C (native with the same table). Every leg asserts its sigtab row reads back as armed BEFORE asserting the outcome. TWO sabotages, each reddening a DIFFERENT leg: reverting the whole predicate to the `handler_va` load -> leg A; dropping ONLY the ignore leg -> leg B (and leg A then passes, proving the two are independently covered). **#252 addendum (a queued uncaught tty:susp had no reader; same round):** when every thread masks `NOTE_BIT_TTY`, gate 1's last line returns false and `job_stop_cb` POSTS instead of stopping -- POSIX's "a blocked stop signal becomes pending". Nothing could then consume it: the EL0 tail's `handler_va == 0` branch handled only the terminate class, and `tty:susp` is STOP-class, so `notes_terminate_note_name_locked` missed it and the note was left queued for "the fd-read path" -- which does not exist for a non-self-managing native Proc and CANNOT exist for a phenotyped one (a Linux guest has no notes fd). The `^Z` was silently lost. Fixed by NEW `notes_stop_note_name_locked` (the STOP-class twin of the terminate scanner, reading the `dfl` column) consulted in that branch after the terminate check misses; on a hit the tail dequeues, drops `q->lock`, and applies the stop via **`proc_job_stop_self`** -- the same primitive `SYS_NOTED(NDFLT)` uses, so the orphan rule and #240's `susp_stop_armed` freshness both apply without restatement, and a cont that landed while the note sat masked correctly evaporates the deferred stop. **THE FINDING'S STATED CONSEQUENCE WAS OVERSTATED and the correction matters:** it claimed the ring fills to `NOTE_QUEUE_DEPTH` (16) and the Proc becomes un-Ctrl-C-able. It cannot -- the pts fan posts every susp with `sender == NULL`, so all repeats share the `(name, sender_pid=0)` coalesce key and accumulation STOPS at `NOTE_COALESCE_THRESHOLD` (12), leaving four slots and the `interrupt` name reachable. Twelve permanently-dead slots plus a queue permanently in coalesce mode is a real leak; a lockout it is not. The LOST STOP is the defect. **Prosecute on any change:** (1) the stop MUST be applied with `q->lock` dropped -- `proc_job_stop_self` takes `g_proc_table_lock` and the established order is proc_table_lock -> q->lock (the kill branch drops it for the same reason); (2) consume BEFORE stopping, or the note survives the stop and a later cont+delivery re-suspends a resumed program (the same hazard #15's "already consumed at delivery" clause names); (3) the self-managing exemption is load-bearing -- that Proc has an fd reader and consuming here would STEAL its note; (4) do not reimplement the stop -- reusing `proc_job_stop_self` is what keeps the three spellings of "apply the default stop" from drifting. Regression `notes.masked_susp_stops_at_delivery`: positive control (unmasked stops at POST), leg A (masked -> posted, pending, NOT yet deliverable), leg B (unmask -> deliverable, dequeues, stop applies), leg C (a cont while masked cancels it -- the #240 composition), leg D (self-managing keeps its note). Sabotage-verified: removing the STOP-class arm reddens leg B while the #251 test stays green. **KNOWN COVERAGE BOUNDARY, stated in the test:** the tail's three lines of WIRING (that it calls the decider, dequeues on non-NULL, and drops the lock before stopping) are NOT covered -- deleting that block leaves every assertion green. A unit test cannot install a `current_thread()`, which is exactly why both this decider and the terminate twin are exposed at all. **The closer named here was WRONG and is corrected in the maskstop addendum below: no `^Z` E2E can reach this arm, including the one that later landed (`pty-susp-pouch`, aux#238) -- `proc_job_stop_pgrp` takes the stop at POST time, so the note is never left queued-and-deliverable. The arm's precondition must be CONSTRUCTED (all-masked, then unmasked).** **#254 addendum (exec's sigtab `kfree` was a cross-Proc USE-AFTER-FREE; found by the #251/#252 self-audit, fixed 2026-08-15):** `proc_exec_drop_image_state` freed `p->sigtab` and NULLed it while holding NO lock -- `g_proc_table_lock` is released ~40 lines earlier in `proc_exec_replace` and never re-acquired. The free's comment justified it by naming ONE reader ("`notes_deliver_at_el0_return`, which runs on a thread of THIS Proc") and concluding from `proc_exec_alone` that there was nobody else. **The single-THREAD half was true; the single-READER half was false**, and had been since before the free existed. The readers are a SET, each taking an ARBITRARY `Proc` and dereferencing `p->sigtab` lock-free from another CPU: (1) `notes_post`'s V-6b SIG_IGN hook (`notes.c`, above the `q->lock` acquire, returning before it -- cross-Proc on EVERY caller: `child_exit` to the parent, `pipe` to the writer, `SYS_POSTNOTE`, the pgrp fan, the console `interrupt`, `TTY_HUP`); (2) `notes_proc_has_live_handler` via `notes_arm_intr_terminate_locked`; (3) `notes_proc_default_applies` via `proc_tty_susp_would_stop_locked` inside `job_stop_cb`, where the Proc is an arbitrary PGRP MEMBER -- **reader 3 was added by the #251 fix two commits before this defect was filed**, which is the point: the free was CORRECT WHEN WRITTEN and became wrong as the reader set grew, the failure mode no single-commit review catches. Excluded by the same enclosing-function test, NOT by assumption: the two `viv_sigtab_set` WRITES (`notes.c`'s SA_RESETHAND arm and the V-6c delivery path) run on a thread OF THE TARGET, which `proc_exec_alone` genuinely excludes -- so this is a UAF READ, not a UAF write, and an escalation to heap corruption was checked and WITHDRAWN. Fixed by NEW `viv_sigtab_reset(tab)` (`kernel/vivarium.c`, declared in `vivarium.h`): exec resets the table **in place** and the object survives; the only `kfree` is now `proc_free`. **Zeroing is EXACT POSIX, not an approximation** -- `VIV_SIG_DFL == 0` and `VIV_SIG_IGN == 1` are not addresses (the rule `viv_sigtab_note_handler` already discriminates on), and `viv_sigtab_of` allocates with `kzalloc`, so a zeroed table is BYTE-IDENTICAL to a fresh one. **Prosecute on any change:** (1) **do not reintroduce a free here, and do not "fix" this with a lock or a refcount** -- the deciding argument is evidential: a lock only protects readers who remember to take it, and reader 3 proves a future reader will not (its author was holding this very finding and still wrote a bare `__atomic_load_n`); an immortal-per-Proc table is safe as the reader set GROWS; (2) the residual is a BENIGN unordered read -- a cross-Proc post racing the reset may observe the old or the new disposition, which is the same latitude POSIX gives a `sigaction` racing a signal already in flight (`proc.h`'s sigtab paragraph states it as the standing rule); do not claim the fix imposes an ordering, it does not; (3) `proc.c` must keep `struct viv_sigtab` INCOMPLETE -- the forward function declaration is deliberate layering, so the reset stays one call to the owning module rather than a field walk that drifts; (4) `proc_free`'s safety argument must stay stated over the reader SET, and rests on the free being a field of a Proc that is ITSELF being freed (a cross-Proc reader must hold `p` to reach `p->sigtab`, so anyone who could observe it already has a dangling Proc -- a louder, different bug); (5) `proc.h`'s "LOCK-FREE ENTRY ACCESS" paragraph now answers BOTH axes (intra-Proc excluded by the clone-argument-domain; cross-Proc NOT excluded and sound for the pointer-stability reason) -- do not narrow it back to a claim about threads, which is what it said through three previous rewrites. **THERE IS NO BEHAVIOURAL TEST FOR THIS AND ONE MUST NOT BE ATTEMPTED:** `viv_sigtab_note_ignored`/`_handler` are NULL-safe and answer "nothing ignored, no handler" for a NULL table -- EXACTLY what they answer for an all-default one -- so freed-and-NULLed and reset-in-place are indistinguishable at every observable surface, including the `^Z` fan. Regression is the memory-safety property directly, in `proc.exec_drops_image_note_state`: capture the pointer, arm TWO rows (one per encoding -- SIG_IGN and a real handler address, since a reset clearing only one would pass a single-row check), assert both read back ARMED, drive the helper, then assert `p->sigtab == tab_before` plus both rows cleared. **Sabotage-verified with the discriminating result recorded:** restoring `kfree`+NULL reddens EXACTLY ONE assertion -- the object-identity one -- while BOTH POSIX assertions stay GREEN, i.e. the executed proof that a behavioural test passes under the bug. **aux#253 addendum (the round-2 F4 fix's owed regression, plus the anti-drift refactor that made it writable):** F4 landed the fix -- the SELF `kill` arm cascades unconditionally instead of only when `live_threads > 1` -- WITHOUT a test, because `sys_postnote_handler` resolves its target from `current_thread()`, which in the harness is the harness's own thread. Closed by taking the honest option of the three the task recorded: **factor the decision instead of duplicating it.** NEW `postnote_kill_cascade_locked(target, name)` is now the ONE spelling of N-4 (`kill` terminates, whatever the thread count and whatever the ring state); BOTH arms call it, the cross arm already holding `g_proc_table_lock` via `proc_for_each` and the self arm taking it around the call. The self arm itself is extracted to `postnote_self(p, name)` with a `sys_postnote_self_for_test` hook on the `sys_postnote_cross_for_test` pattern -- the hook drives the REAL arm, not a second copy of the decision (the "delegation hides a check" risk the task named: testing an extracted predicate proves the predicate, never that the caller reaches it). **Prosecute on any change:** (1) do not re-inline the kill decision into either arm -- two arms agreeing by inspection produced BOTH #241's defect and F4's, and a third spelling still lives in `devproc.c`'s `/proc/<pid>/ctl` kill verb (fold it in if that file is touched for this reason); (2) the self arm is NOT under `g_proc_table_lock` on entry and the cross arm IS -- the predicate documents the requirement and neither caller may quietly stop meeting it; (3) `postnote_self` must stay the function the syscall CALLS, not a copy the test calls, or the hook silently stops covering production. Regression `notes.self_kill_through_full_ring`: fills the ring to `NOTE_QUEUE_DEPTH` with `synthetic = false` posts (which NEVER coalesce -- the coalesce block is gated on `synthetic`, so the count rises past `NOTE_COALESCE_THRESHOLD` instead of stalling there, which is what the task's trap note warned about), proves fullness TWO independent ways (the counter, and a live post now REFUSED), then drives the real arm. **ASSERTION ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING AND WAS INITIALLY WRONG:** `TEST_ASSERT` `return`s on failure, so only the FIRST failing assertion is ever observed. Written with the return-value check first, the sabotage reddened on the ABI symptom and the `group_exit_msg` INVARIANT assertion never executed -- a claim of "red pre-fix" sitting untested beside a test that looked thorough. Reordered so the invariant is first and the symptom second. TWO sabotages, each reddening a DIFFERENT named assertion and NOTHING else: restoring `proc_count_live_peers_locked(p, NULL) > 1` on the self arm reddens the `group_exit_msg` assertion (and leaves `notes.kill_terminates_single_thread` GREEN, proving the two arms are independently covered); removing the ring-fill loop reddens the fullness CONTROL, proving the control measures the precondition rather than decorating it. **maskstop addendum (the deferred-`^Z` stop arm was reached by NOTHING, and reading it found a P1; 2026-08-16):** #252's coverage boundary above named an "in-guest `^Z` E2E" as the closer. That was wrong for **any** `^Z` E2E: the STOP class has a POST-time decider (`proc_job_stop_pgrp` -> `proc_tty_susp_would_stop_locked`) that consumes the signal before the note is ever left queued, so the tail's arm is unreachable from an ordinary `^Z`. Its precondition is a state that must be **constructed** -- every thread masks `NOTE_BIT_TTY`, the fan POSTS instead of stopping, and the mask then lifts. Deleting the arm left every existing green untouched (three in-guest E2Es plus the unit test written for the fix): those tests were not wrong, they were **irrelevant**, which from a green is indistinguishable. **This is a general class, not a one-off** -- ask of any late-path arm "which test makes its precondition true", and note that its terminate TWIN three lines away IS covered (an uncaught `interrupt` has no post-time decider, so the tail is its only consumer): **sibling symmetry is not coverage symmetry.** Closed by `/susp-mask-child` + `jc-probe`'s `maskstop` rung (boot-fatal via joey, so it rides every boot of every gate; it is also the only leg that exercises `q->lock` -> `g_proc_table_lock` under real contention). **[P1] What executing the arm found:** it DECIDED with the class-filtered peek (first deliverable STOP-class note, at ANY index) and CONSUMED with the general `notes_dequeue_locked`, which is class-BLIND (first mask-permitted entry in FIFO order, i.e. the head). With `[child_exit, tty:susp]` queued the stop applied correctly while the `child_exit` was popped into a stack local that is never read -- **destroyed, not delivered; a wait notification silently gone** -- leaving the `tty:susp` queued to re-fire at the next EL0 return. `pipe` behaves identically. Reachable by any native Proc holding a child (`child_exit`'s #15 default is IGNORE, so it is RETAINED in the ring) that then takes a deferred `^Z`. **Why it survived:** on a ONE-NOTE queue every selection rule looks identical, and every queue any prior test built held exactly one note; and both sibling paths are consistent by construction, which is what stopped anyone asking here -- the handler-delivery path was explicitly fixed at round-2 M1 to "pop under the SAME `q->lock` as the peek so the popped note IS the candidate", and the terminate arm never pops at all (`exits()`). The standing "the fix that exists on site N stops you asking about site N+1" failure, third recurrence on this row. Fixed by NEW `notes_stop_pending_idx_locked` (index-returning, shared by peek and pop) + NEW `notes_stop_dequeue_locked`; the dispatcher now makes ONE call. **Prosecute on any change:** (1) **never reach for the general `notes_dequeue_locked` from a path that selected its note by class** -- that is the whole defect, and it reads correct; (2) the dispatcher must keep calling the CONSUMER, not peek-then-pop -- two calls means two gate lists and two scans that must agree, and "must agree" is a property a reader has to check, whereas one call cannot disagree with itself; (3) `notes_stop_note_name_locked` is now **TEST-ONLY** and its gate list must stay verbatim-identical to the consumer's, or the tests driving it verify a disposition production does not apply (the header says so); (4) the consumer does NO terminate-latch bookkeeping after its pop, BY CONSTRUCTION -- it pops only names whose `dfl` is STOP, for which `notes_name_terminate_latch` is 0 (it answers 0 unless `dfl` is TERMINATE), so `notes_drain_intr_locked` would return at its first line for any note this path can ever pop, present or future; the call it used to make was dead and its "kept for a future STOP-class addition" comment described a case the code cannot reach (the c8ab2744 round, F3) -- do not re-add the call on that reasoning, and if a STOP-class name ever ALSO carried a latch the table row would be self-contradictory before this site mattered; (5) both peek and pop still require `q->lock`, and the stop still applies with it DROPPED (the #252 lock-order obligation is unchanged). Regression `notes.stop_dequeue_picks_its_own_note`: leg A **EXECUTES** the class-blind pop on a two-note queue and asserts it takes the `child_exit` -- the defect, executed, so the leg cannot decay into a tautology if the two rules ever converge again -- then leg B drives the real consumer and asserts BOTH halves (the susp went AND the `child_exit` survived; a count-only check would pass if the consumer had eaten the wrong one). Legs B and D of `notes.masked_susp_stops_at_delivery` were re-pointed at the consumer the tail actually calls -- they had been driving the general dequeue and the test-only predicate respectively, the same "verified on a function production does not reach" hole one layer up. **The formal round RAN 2026-08-17 (Fable 5, MODEL start==end): 0 P0 + 1 P1 + 1 P2 + 2 P3, the audited change clean, both real findings PRE-EXISTING in the `handler_va == 0` branch three lines above the audited arm -- and CLOSED the same day.** **c8ab2744-round addendum (the class scans were PHENOTYPE-BLIND and a SIG_DFL `pipe` was consumed by NO arm; the audit close):** **[F1, P1]** `notes_terminate_pending_name_locked` gated on `handler_va` (always 0 for a Linux guest) and returned the first latch-class name at ANY index with no per-Proc disposition -- so on PHENO_LINUX, when the candidate was a `SIG_DFL` fall-through (a masked-then-unmasked `tty:susp`, or a retained `pipe`), a CAUGHT `tty:hup`/`interrupt` queued behind it was returned and the guest was `exits()`ed with its SIGHUP/SIGINT handler installed. #251's `notes_proc_default_applies` reached the post-time decider and the stop predicates and NOT this scan -- the fourth "site N+1" recurrence on this row. Fixed by gating every hit of BOTH class scans (the terminate scan and the STOP index scan) on `notes_proc_default_applies(p, name)`, both now taking `p`; the fixed-name `notes_proc_default_applies(p, NOTE_NAME_TTY_SUSP)` gate outside the stop scan is retired (per-note subsumes fixed-name; the peek's and the consumer's gate lists are again identical -- self-managing, then the gated scan). Native Procs byte-identical. **[F2, P2]** a `SIG_DFL` `pipe` on PHENO_LINUX reached no arm: `pipe` has no native terminate latch (#237), so the phenotype fall-through's terminate scan skipped it, the stop consumer skipped it, and "leave it queued for the fd reader" stranded it at the head of a queue a Linux guest has no fd to read -- the permanent dispatcher candidate: every later caught signal stranded behind it, every later default-ignore note never dropped, and (F1) every later caught terminate-class note KILLED the guest. Fixed PHENOTYPE-SCOPED (native `pipe`/#237 untouched): NEW `viv_signote_default_is_terminate` (PIPE / INTERRUPT / TTY_HUP / TTY_QUIT; `kernel/vivarium.{c,h}`) and, in the phenotype branch after the `have_handler` arm, `spin_unlock(&q->lock); exits(notes_canonical_name_ptr(candidate.name))` -- the phenotype `wait4` folds a note-death into an EXITED status (#91), so `exits("pipe")` reads as `exits("interrupt")` already did; only the STOP default still falls through to the native branch's stop consumer. **[F3, P3]** the dead drain call (item (4) above, rewritten). **[F4, P3]** three contract sentences said an uncaught susp is never queued (`notes.h` tty-family header, `notes.c` `notes_name_terminate_latch`'s header, `notes.h` `notes_stop_note_name_locked`'s header) -- falsified by the path the maskstop fix made live; reworded: it is queued when caught, when every thread masks the family (POSIX pending), or when the Proc has no thread yet (the spawn window -- `job_stop_cb` posts on EVERY `!would_stop` reason, and the spawn-window `^Z` is posted with `susp_stop_armed` set and taken by the first thread's first EL0 return). **Prosecute on any change:** (1) BOTH class scans must keep the per-note gate INSIDE the scan -- an outer fixed-name gate is exactly the shape that let the terminate scan go ungated while its stop twin looked covered; (2) the phenotype terminate arm acts on the CANDIDATE only (mask-permitted by the peek), never scans -- a scan there would re-create F1's any-index shape on the Linux side; (3) `viv_signote_default_is_terminate` must NOT include KILL (answered by the kill branch above, before any disposition is read) or SUSP (the STOP consumer's), and adding a name to it does not touch the native latch table -- the two are DIFFERENT questions and #237 stays open; (4) native `pipe` behaviour is unchanged (no latch, no default terminate) -- do not "unify" it with the phenotype answer without resolving #237 as the ABI decision it is. Regressions: `notes.class_scans_read_phenotype_sigtab` (unit; positive control -> phenotype+`SIG_DFL` `[child_exit, tty:hup]` names the hup at index 1; A `[tty:susp, interrupt+handler]` -> terminate scan NULL, the stop consumer takes the susp, the interrupt survives; B the control's queue with SIGHUP caught -> NULL; C an interrupt queued under `SIG_DFL` then `SIG_IGN`ed -> NULL; D the stop consumer with a SIGTSTP handler declines and takes it once the row is `SIG_DFL` again; E native with the same table names the interrupt) -- sabotage: dropping the per-note gate from the terminate scan reddens A/B/C while the control and E stay green; and the L-6c gate's `L6C-J` (`yes \| head -n 1`, both ends writing into ONE fd-3 capture -- head its line, the writer its stderr -- so the capture is EXACTLY `y`: an exec'd all-`SIG_DFL` binary is killed before its write returns; pre-fix it appended `yes: write error: Broken pipe` and lived), `L6C-K` (POSITIVE CONTROL: a subshell writer that runs `trap "" PIPE` in its own process (the trap is set AFTER the fork -- and since the fork/exec rule of 2026-08-17 a phenotype fork inherits dispositions and an exec keeps SIG_IGN, so the parent shell's SIG_DFL SIGPIPE is what the writer starts from either way) -- so the write RETURNS EPIPE and the builtin echo reports `write error` after the line; proves the capture is armed and can see a message -- and it DID, on its first boot: the capture was broken by the `fcntl` errno defect recorded on the VIVARIUM row, J and L passed VACUOUSLY on an empty capture, K alone went red) and `L6C-L` (K with the trap removed, ONE variable away: the capture is exactly head's line again) -- sabotage: disabling the phenotype `exits()` reddens J and L while K stays green. Also OPEN from the same sweep: the tail's delivery-time SIG_IGN discard arm (the PHENO_LINUX branch, "a note queued BEFORE the install") is reached by nothing either -- the post-time hook prevents the state, and `viv-pheno-probe`'s L27/L28 install SIG_IGN with no note pending. Same class; needs a way to observe the queue from inside a phenotyped guest, so it is its own chunk. **INSTALL-TIME DISCARD ADDENDUM (2026-08-17, aux; closes the item above by moving the mechanism, not by reaching the arm).** ADDS `notes_discard_name(p, name)` (POSIX 2.4.3 / Linux `do_sigaction`'s `flush_sigqueue_mask`): under `q->lock`, remove EVERY queued note of one name regardless of any thread's mask, draining the class latch per removal (`notes_drain_intr_locked`, so an `interrupt` armed under SIG_DFL and then ignored while blocked does not leave a Proc whose every sleep is `*_INTR`); `kill` is refused (N-4). The phenotype `rt_sigaction` shell calls it AFTER `viv_sigtab_set` whenever the NEW disposition ignores (SIG_IGN, or SIG_DFL with `viv_signote_default_is_ignore`) -- including the no-table SIG_DFL shortcut, which now skips only the store. AND `notes_post`'s SIG_IGN disposition read moved UNDER `q->lock`: store-then-lock (installer) against read-under-lock (poster) leaves no interleaving with a stale ignored note (a poster that read SIG_DFL enqueued in a lock hold the discard follows; one that locks after the discard reads SIG_IGN and drops), so the tail's SIG_IGN arm is now DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH -- kept, because its absence would hand any stale note to the SIG_DFL-terminate arm. Why it moved: the deferred discard was correct for SIG_IGN alone and observably wrong for `pending -> SIG_IGN -> handler -> unblock` (Linux fires nothing; the tail ran the handler for a signal POSIX says died at the SIG_IGN). **Prosecute on any change:** (1) the shell's discard runs AFTER the store, never before (before, a poster racing in the gap re-queues an ignored note the discard already ran for); (2) the poster's read stays under `q->lock` (moving it back out re-opens the window the arm used to close -- and the arm is now documented as unreachable); (3) mask-blindness is POSIX ("whether or not it is blocked") -- a per-thread-mask filter would strand a blocked instance; (4) `kill` must remain un-discardable through this API even though no sigtab can name it; (5) SIG_DFL for a default-ignore signal must ALSO discard (Linux `sig_handler_ignored`), and the no-table shortcut must not skip it. Regressions: `notes.discard_name_purges_pending` (mask-blind removal, per-CLASS latch drain [tty:hup out -> TTY latch stays for tty:quit -> out -> clears], survivor order, `kill` refused, absent name no-op, a purged full ring is REALLY empty -- 16 out, 16 in) and viv-pheno-probe L205-L216 (in-guest, deterministic via the reader-less fd 0: round A pending -> SIG_IGN -> unblock SURVIVES with nothing fired [L209 is pre-stamped so a death names its leg] and a handler installed after is not handed a stale note [L210]; round B pending -> SIG_IGN -> handler -> unblock fires NOTHING [L215 -- the leg that separates install-time from delivery-time; RED before this addendum]; each round ends with a fresh SIGPIPE delivered exactly once). **7580c1f7-ROUND (Fable 5, 0/0/0/4 -- the mechanism re-derived SOUND):** F1 the SIG_DFL/default-ignore purge disjunct had no driver -> probe L229-L232 (block SIGCHLD, a child exits, `rt_sigaction(SIGCHLD, SIG_DFL)` purges the queued child_exit, a handler + unblock fires NOTHING; the positive control fires once; sabotage S13 drops the disjunct -> L231 red); F2 L210's comment over-claimed (reworded: L215 is the only install-vs-delivery discriminator); F3 RECORDED divergence, POSIX-permitted: the generation-time SIG_IGN drop is MASK-BLIND where Linux queues a blocked ignored signal and discards at dequeue (`block; SIG_IGN; raise; handler; unblock` -- Linux fires the handler, Thylacine nothing) -- 145-vivarium.md states it; F4 stale sentences fixed. **FORK/EXEC SIGNAL-STATE ADDENDUM (task #127, operator-voted 2026-08-17; (2) the child never shares the parent's pointer (the immortal-per-Proc lifetime, #254); (3) exec must not touch SIG_IGN rows or the mask on the phenotype, and must clear everything on native; (4) `in_handler` still clears (#247). Prosecute on any change: (5) the snapshot crosses fork WHOLE and only for the phenotype (a Plan 9 child is not notified -- native copies nothing); a partial copy (block without the flag, or the flag without the FP half) is worse than none. Delivery is UNCHANGED (the in_handler guard still holds every note; the mask cannot admit a nested delivery). **Prosecute on any change:** (1) the frame's uc_sigmask must be built from the PRE-handler mask (before the store) -- building it after would show the handler its own handler-time mask in the frame; (2) the restore's phenotype gate reads `t->proc->phenotype`, sound because a PHENO_LINUX Proc reaches delivery only through the Linux path and a native Proc never does -- a per-thread "which path wrote it" flag would be a second source of truth for the same fact; (3) an unconditional restore breaks the native rule (the unit test's control B); (4) a field added to the restore is added to the fork copy in the same commit; (5) exec keeps the handler-time mask on the phenotype (Linux keeps the current blocked set across execve) -- do not "restore" it at exec. Sabotages: SM1 (no handler-time store) -> L239/L242/L243; SM2 (no restore) -> L240/L241/L244 + the unit test's leg A; SM3 (the fork copy skips note_saved_mask) -> L244 only. **main#243 ROUND-2 (Fable) + RESIDUALS ADDENDUM (2026-08-17; `memory/audit_243_fable_closed_list.md`; the merge `8a58112d` brought aux's F1/F3/F4 fixes, the residuals commit landed F2/F5/F6/F7/F8):** the sigtab is reset IN PLACE at exec and freed only at `proc_free` (aux#254 = main#243 P1, found on both tracks); the ACCESS DISCIPLINE is now a construction in `kernel/vivarium.c` -- every entry field is one `__atomic_*` op on an aligned u64 (`_Static_assert`ed), an install publishes `handler` LAST with RELEASE and readers `ldar` it, a reset zeroes `handler` FIRST; the load-bearing sentence sits AT `notes_proc_has_live_handler`: a CROSS-Proc reader that acts on `handler` ALONE (the copy is discarded). **Prosecute on any change:** (a) a new cross-Proc reader that CONSUMES the copied flags/restorer/mask does not inherit the argument (the only inconsistent entry a reader can see is {old live handler, fields already zero} during that Proc's own exec); (b) `viv_sigtab_note_handler` must load the gate ONCE and gate on that value; (c) `viv_sigtab_set` must store the fields BEFORE the handler; (d) exec must clear `Thread.clear_child_tid` (F8) beside `in_handler` in `proc_exec_drop_image_state` (the ONE place); (e) `proc.exec_reset_dispositions` (all four fields + entry COUNT-1 seeded, every-byte-zero) and `vivarium.sigtab_fork_exec_rule`'s table-level arm are the discrimination -- sabotage S1 (reset stops one entry short) and S2 (gate field only) each went RED on the named assertions before landing. |
| Capability checks | **A POLICY ROW, NOT A FILE TRIGGER -- and that is now said out loud rather than left to be inferred from an empty column** (vault #170). It fires on any change to a capability GATE wherever one lives, so it cannot be reduced to a file list without becoming wrong the next time a gate is added. The gate sites today: `kernel/syscall.c` (the `syscall_dispatch` prologue + every `cap_*`/`perm_check` call in a handler), `kernel/include/thylacine/caps.h` (the `CAP_*` bit registry + `CAP_ELEVATION_ONLY`), `kernel/devcap.c` + `kernel/include/thylacine/devcap.h` (the `cap` device: the only growth path), `kernel/proc.c` (`rfork_internal`'s I-2 strip), `kernel/devproc.c` (the I-39 two-axis debug gate), `kernel/allowance.c` (the I-34 hardware axis). **If you add a gate anywhere else, this row still covers it -- audit, and add the site here.** | Privilege correctness |
| KASLR / ASLR | `arch/arm64/start.S`, `arch/arm64/kaslr.c` | Entropy quality, layout correctness (I-16) |
| ELF loader | `kernel/elf.c` | RWX rejection, relocation correctness |
| ELF loader: ET_DYN placement + AT_ENTRY (DISTRO D-2) | `kernel/elf.c` (the `e_type` gate widened to ET_EXEC-or-ET_DYN; `bias = is_pie ? ELF_PIE_LOAD_BIAS : 0` applied to every PT_LOAD `vaddr` + to `e_entry`, with the `ELF_LOAD_PIE_OOB` window/overflow check on the ET_DYN arm ONLY; `PT_DYNAMIC` refused iff `!is_pie`; `PT_INTERP` refused unconditionally), `kernel/include/thylacine/elf.h` (`ELF_PIE_LOAD_BIAS` 0x2000_0000 + `ELF_PIE_LOAD_LIMIT` 0x6000_0000 + the 64 KiB-alignment `_Static_assert`; `struct elf_image.load_bias` + `.type`; `ELF_LOAD_PIE_OOB` = -24; `AT_ENTRY` = 9), `kernel/include/thylacine/exec.h` (`EXEC_INIT_AUXV_COUNT` 8 -> 9 -- frame 176 -> 192, `AT_RANDOM` offset 160 -> 176; NEW `#include <thylacine/elf.h>` + the `ELF_PIE_LOAD_LIMIT <= EXEC_USER_STACK_GUARD_BASE` `_Static_assert`), `kernel/exec.c` (`exec_fill_auxv` gains an `entry_va` parameter + emits `AT_ENTRY` before the optional `AT_VDSO_CLOCK`; the count assert 8 -> 9) | **Audit-noted, NOT a focused round** (DISTRO §5), and the reason is structural: the bias enters at EXACTLY ONE place -- `elf_load`'s segment loop -- so every downstream consumer (the `exec_load_into` segment loop, `seg_geometry`, the `file_shareable` split, the #149 page-sharing refusal, `map_file_backed`'s burrow-offset-0 identity, the `AT_PHDR` translation) reads FINAL addresses and is byte-unchanged. **NO new invariant**; I-12 (W^X) and I-36 (REVENANT's 7 conditions) are untouched -- a PIE's segments take the same arms at the same permissions, only the numbers moved. Prosecute: the biased-vaddr overflow pair (`p_vaddr + p_memsz`, then `+ bias`) and whether `ELF_PIE_LOAD_LIMIT` can be stepped over by a hostile `p_vaddr`; that the ET_EXEC path is provably bias-free (`elf.pie_load_bias`'s control leg); that the entry-in-segment search compares biased-to-biased; that `e_entry == 0` means "null" for ET_EXEC and "the load base" for ET_DYN and the zero-check therefore runs on the FINAL value; that the 64 KiB bias alignment really preserves `p_vaddr == p_offset (mod p_align)` for a stock `p_align` of 0x10000 (else a segment silently changes arms); the auxv frame arithmetic at the new count (the `EXEC_INIT_STRUCTURED` / `EXEC_INIT_FRAME_SIZE` / `EXEC_INIT_STACK_MAX_SIZE` chain and its `_Static_assert`s). **The loader applies NO relocations and needs none** -- both ET_DYN shapes that reach it self-relocate before executing anything dependent on it (a static-PIE from `rcrt1.o`; stock ldso in `_dlstart`, PC-relatively, not from auxv). Validation: `elf.pie_load_bias` (bias applied / ET_EXEC control / PT_DYNAMIC both directions / PT_INTERP still refused on ET_DYN / `e_entry == 0` by type / window + wrap refusal), the boot-fatal joey `D2-A`+`D2-B` markers exec'ing the rootfs's OWN stock `ld-musl-aarch64.so.1`, and the PIE-path-only sabotage pair that proves the gate discriminates AND that AT_PHDR (not AT_ENTRY) is the load-bearing tag: AT_ENTRY:=0 -> gate PASSES, AT_PHDR:=0 -> gate FAILS at D2-A, unit suite 1363/1363 in both. **Correction carried here (task #186):** DISTRO §5 claimed musl discriminates direct invocation on the entry; measured against `ldso/dynlink.c:1834` it is AT_PHDR. **Seam (task #188, user-dispositioned):** no static-PIE from our own toolchain -- the pouch `libc.a` is non-PIC and the fork driver drops `-static-pie`. |
| `AT_HWCAP` exec-auxv CPU-feature word (the CF-4 A AEAD lever) | `arch/arm64/hwfeat.{c,h}` (`g_hw_features.linux_hwcap` — the Linux-uapi-numbered word derived from ID_AA64ISAR0_EL1 + ID_AA64PFR0_EL1 at `hw_features_detect`: FP/ASIMD [PFR0 inverted-sentinel fields] + AES/PMULL/SHA1/SHA2/SHA512/SHA3/CRC32/ATOMICS/ASIMDDP), `kernel/include/thylacine/elf.h` (`AT_HWCAP = 16` — the STANDARD SysV tag, unlike the deliberately-private `AT_VDSO_CLOCK`), `kernel/exec.c::exec_fill_auxv` (+1 unconditional entry; both frame shapes route through it), `kernel/include/thylacine/exec.h` (`EXEC_INIT_AUXV_COUNT` 7 -> 8; Shape-A frame 160 -> 176); consumers: pouch libsodium (`tools/build.sh` compiles the self-arming armcrypto TUs, runtime-gated on `getauxval(AT_HWCAP) & (1<<3)`), the Go fork (`os_thylacine.go` sysargs -> `internal/cpu.HWCap` + `cpu_arm64_thylacine.go`) | The CF-4 A AEAD lever (CONCURRENT-FS.md; measured: the go-build cold window was 20.0 s of 20.7 s inside SOFT AEGIS-256 decrypt while the CPU's AES extensions idled behind the missing feature word). **An exec-ABI addition — audit-noted, not privilege-bearing**: the word is read-only boot-derived info (I-15: derived from ID regs, like the banner), leaks no secret/layout (no KASLR interaction), and is APPEND-ONLY (a consumer treats unknown-clear as absent). Invariants: the bits must be TRUTHFUL (a set bit whose instructions extinct on this CPU = a SIGILL time bomb in every consumer; a clear bit = silent soft fallback — fail-safe by construction on crypto-less cores, RPi4's A72); **`hwcap_CPUID` (bit 11) must NEVER be set** while Thylacine lacks Linux's EL0 MIDR trap-and-emulate (Go's `hwcapInit` calls `getMIDR()` — an EL0 `mrs midr_el1` — when it sees CPUID; on Thylacine that is `snare:ill`); auxv consumers scan by tag to AT_NULL (verified: musl, libthyla-rs, the Go fork — no fixed-offset parser exists), so the +1 entry is layout-safe. RECORDED SEAM (audit F5): the word is BOOT-CPU-derived (before smp_init); a heterogeneous target must AND-in secondary ID regs at smp_init before the first exec (the Linux sanitized-view analog) — all current targets are homogeneous and the W1.5 LSE patcher carries the identical assumption. Proof: the `exec.setup_auxv` kernel tests (tag order + the FP\|ASIMD floor + the hwcap value) + the pouch-hello-sodium AGREEMENT probe (`sodium_runtime_has_armcrypto()` must equal the AT_HWCAP AES bit — proves the hardware path live on AES CPUs AND the fail-safe on AES-less ones, boot-fatal) + the bench re-measure. **No new §28 invariant; no new spec** per the 2026-05-23 broadening — prose in CONCURRENT-FS.md CF-4 A + this row + the focused audit + the SMP gate. |

The hazard: a tagged socket fd's `kernel_fd` is the `/net/<proto>/N/data` fd -- a REGULAR dev9p file, which the kernel `dev9p.poll` slot (net-6b-2b) treats as POSIX always-ready (it probes only a `QTPOLL`-marked Spoor), so the 0015 tag-dispatch (which polls `kernel_fd`) reports a `/net` socket ready unconditionally (a `poll(POLLIN)` returns instantly; `select()` reports every `/net` fd ready always). FIX: a new slot helper `pouch_sock_poll_fd` resolves the POLL TARGET (distinct from `pouch_sock_kernel_fd`, the I/O target) -- a FAM_INET socket polls its `/net/<proto>/N/ready` sibling (the `QTPOLL` readiness file, lazily opened on first poll + held for the socket's lifetime, closed at `close()` alongside `ctl_fd`), a FAM_UNIX socket keeps polling its `/srv`-stream `kernel_fd` (unchanged); the events bits pass straight through (the kernel threads them as the readiness `Tread` offset; POLLIN=0x001/POLLOUT=0x004 align across pouch/kernel/netd). `src/select/poll.c` routes its tagged-fd dispatch through `pouch_sock_poll_fd`; `select`/`pselect`/`ppoll` inherit (they route through `poll()` per 0005). NO new kernel surface (only a `SYS_open` of the readiness file). Composes the SAME I-1/I-5/I-23/I-28 + I-9 (the kernel dev9p.poll register-then-observe, `net_poll.tla`); adds NO new invariant. Prosecute (the formal net-6b audit, net-6b-4): the ready-fd lifecycle (opened once, closed exactly once on every close/error path; the concurrent-same-fd-poll + the close-vs-lazy-open race reconciled under the slot lock -- the inherited single-user-per-socket discipline); the FAM_UNIX non-regression (kernel_fd path byte-equivalent); the events-passthrough correctness. **v1.0 seam -- listener-poll (#220):** netd's `check_ready` reports `can_recv()` (false for a TCP listener), so a `poll(listener, POLLIN)` does not wake on a pending accept (the `select()`-server multiplex pattern) -- the blocking accept via `open(listen)` (net-3a) is the working path; no v1.0 in-VM consumer polls a listener; candidate fix = `check_ready` reports `accept_ready` for an ANNOUNCED TCP slot (proven via a loopback E2E), weighed at net-6b-4. Self-audit clean; **No new spec**; prose in `docs/reference/78-pouch.md` (the AF_INET backend, the poll subsection) + `docs/reference/121-netd.md` (the listener-poll seam) + NET-DESIGN §12.2 (the net-6b-3 as-built) + this addendum + the `/pouch-hello-net` poll control surface (a connected UDP socket POLLOUT-ready + POLLIN-times-out, the load-bearing wait proof) + 931/931 + boot OK + the net-6b-4 SMP gate (kernel byte-unchanged). |

Invariants: the caller's offset reaches the Dev verbatim (wire-asserted via the dev9p loopback Tread/Twrite offset capture) and the cursor is NEVER read or advanced on any path; `off < 0` + `off+len > INT64_MAX` (u64 arithmetic, no UB) + `!dev->seekable` (the POSIX ESPIPE shape — pread on a pipe fails up front, BEFORE the len==0 short-circuit, never silently a cursor-free stream op) all reject; the #844 ref-held lookup + #81 CWALKONLY reject (len 0 included) + the #3 errno clamp carry over byte-identically (the cursor arms of the shared inners are byte-identical to the pre-#37 bodies); NO weft fast-path (a weft flow is a stream — documented, deliberate). **#47 (same chunk, the #46 sibling): SYS_WSTAT's fd gate dropped RIGHT_WRITE -> kind-gate-only** — POSIX fchmod/fchown work on an O_RDONLY fd (omode-derived RIGHT_READ-only handle, A-3 F1); the write AUTHORITY is `perm_wstat_check` (the identity axis: owner-or-CAP), which is unchanged and now the ONLY write-authority gate on that path — do not weaken it; the #46 endowed-fd exception carries over (rights bound byte I/O + transfer, never the identity axis). Regressions: `sys_prw.{pread_devramfs_offset_and_cursor,pipe_not_seekable,rights_and_walkonly}` + `dev9p.prw_wire_offset_and_cursor` + `dev9p.wstat_readonly_fd` (owner chmod via READ-only handle succeeds; a stranger with FULL rights is still perm-denied) + the pouch-hello read–pread–read /welcome sandwich (in-guest, every boot; pre-#37 musl pread was ENOSYS so success IS the wiring proof) + the go4c gate builds (cmd/go buildid rides Pread; a cursor/offset bug resurfaces the #36 cache-miss cascade at build2-warm). Invariants: SYS_FSTAT kind gate (KOBJ_SPOOR, ANY rights, rejects KOBJ_SRV — #46 dropped the original RIGHT_READ tightening: POSIX fstat(2) works on any valid fd [Linux O_WRONLY/O_PATH; Plan 9 Tstat / 9P2000.L Tgetattr have no open/read requirement], an O_WRONLY create mints a WRITE-only handle per A-3 F1 and cmd/go's putIndexEntry fstats exactly such an fd for its truncate no-op gate [the -1 self-deleted every fresh go-cache index entry, #36 layer 4]; the tightening guarded nothing since metadata is reachable via an O_PATH re-walk per #81; follows the SYS_LSEEK rights-0 precedent; in-guest regression = joey `probe46`, boot-fatal, quiet + post-build-churned pool); struct t_stat layout pinned by `_Static_assert`s on every field offset (so a future kernel field add cannot land without bumping the ABI assertions); **#100 appended `u32 devno`@80 (Plan 9 Chan.dev / POSIX st_dev), filled from `spoor->devno` in `spoor_stat_native` (fstat + the stat leaf-Spoor fallback) + the `stalk.c` pounce fused-query leaf, mirrored in lockstep across libt / libthyla-rs `Metadata` (+`dev()`) / pouch patches `0010`+`0019`+`0021` / go-fork `Stat_t`+`sameFile` / gopls robustio `FileID{Dev,qid.path}` -- LESSON: a per-mirror `_Static_assert(sizeof==N)` verifies only THAT mirror's size, NEVER that it matches the kernel, so a stale mirror at the old size passes the build and OVERFLOWS at runtime (0019/0021 were caught by a boot pouch-hello segv + a `struct t_stat` grep, not the build); regression `spoor.stat_native_stamps_devno`; all six `.stat_native` impls zero the full `sizeof(*out)` so the new `_pad_dev` cannot leak (I-13)**; SYS_LSEEK overflow check on SEEK_CUR/SEEK_END (INT64_MAX/MIN bounds); SYS_LSEEK rejects new_offset < 0 (POSIX EINVAL); kernel partial-walk reject (`w->nqid != nname`) closes the missed-walk-returns-source-fd vector; devramfs walk now respects sys_walk_open's "return nc" contract (pre-fix it allocated its own spoor and walk_open silently failed). Pouch-side: open() → openat() forwarding closes the SYS_open=0xFFFF bypass; fstat() translates t_stat → musl's struct stat field-by-field. **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 spec-to-code broadening — prose validation in `docs/reference/87-pouch-fstat-lseek.md` + this row + the audit + joey's `/system.key` probe (walk + fstat + lseek SEEK_END/SEEK_SET round-trip; runs on every boot). |
| pouch abort -> _Exit override | `usr/lib/pouch/patches/0011-pouch-abort.patch` (overrides musl's `src/exit/abort.c` to `_Exit(127)` directly), `usr/joey/joey.c` (retry+drain+reap workaround that depends on the override for clean stratumd exit semantics) | P6-pouch-stratumd-boot sub-chunk 16b-γ-mount-close — the documented v1.x extension landed early because the bdev read-I/O fix unblocks downstream stratumd mount code paths that exercise `assert()` / `abort()`. Upstream musl's `abort()` reaches `a_crash()` (a deliberate NULL deref) as a last-resort kill; at v1.0 the kernel's FAULT_UNHANDLED_USER policy extincts the entire boot rather than terminating the offending Proc. Invariants: WIFEXITED + WEXITSTATUS == 127 replaces Linux's WIFSIGNALED + WTERMSIG == SIGABRT (behavioral delta documented in the patch header + `docs/reference/83-pouch-signals.md`); no raise(SIGABRT) means SIGABRT handlers do NOT see the synthesized note (the override is incompatible with programs that need to run cleanup in a SIGABRT handler — those need v1.x sigaction expansion); `_Exit(127)` is async-signal-safe (the only contract it must hold). **Multi-thread hazard** (audit-bearing v1.x lift per `audit_p6_pouch_stratumd_boot_16b_gamma_mount_close_closed_list.md::F1`): `_Exit` routes through `__NR_exit_group` → kernel `SYS_EXITS`, which extincts the kernel if the Proc has live peer threads. Safe-use envelope: abort() reachable only pre-thread-spawn OR post-thread-join. The fix is SYS_EXIT_GROUP -- now DESIGNED (scripture-first: ARCH §7.9.1 + invariant I-24; task #809, in progress): a new kernel syscall that cascades peer-thread termination + the cross-thread shootdown (flag-and-self-terminate at the EL0-return checkpoint; also closes the documented `kill -> -EIO in multi-thread Proc` from 13b R1-F9). Until the impl lands, the safe-use envelope above holds. Hard prerequisite for 16c (satisfied). **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 spec-to-code broadening — prose validation in `docs/reference/86-pouch-stratumd-boot.md` 16b-γ-mount-close section + this row + the audit. |

Invariant: every Stratum-side (or pouch-side) reproduction of `kobj-rights` bits MUST match `kernel/include/thylacine/handle.h`'s `RIGHT_*` definitions. The bug fixed in 16b-γ-mount-close was a mirror-drift: `bdev_thylacine.c` defined `T_RIGHT_SIGNAL = (1u << 3)` (collision with the kernel's `RIGHT_TRANSFER`) instead of `(1u << 5)`. Cross-project rights-mirror review should be done whenever the kernel adds / renumbers a RIGHT_* bit. **No new spec** — prose validation in `docs/reference/86-pouch-stratumd-boot.md` 16b-γ-mount-close section + this row + the audit. |
