| 9P-srvconn transport adapter | `kernel/9p_srvconn_transport.{c,h}` (new; parallel to `kernel/9p_spoor_transport.{c,h}`); call site in `sys_attach_9p_srv_handler` | P6-pouch-stratumd-boot 16c -- the second `p9_transport_ops` backend (after `p9_spoor_transport`). Wraps a byte-mode `struct SrvConn`'s `c2s` (kernel-client -> server) + `s2c` (server -> kernel-client) byte rings into the `send` / `recv` / `close` vtable that `p9_transport_round_trip` drives. **Invariants**: the wrapped SrvConn MUST be byte-mode (`__atomic_load_n(&cn->byte_mode, ACQUIRE) == true`) -- 9P-mode SrvConns already have an embedded kernel-owned `p9_client` driving Tread/Twrite on `client_fid`, and a second `p9_client` over the same rings would race. The adapter takes a `srvconn_ref` at init and `srvconn_unref`s at close (mirrors `p9_spoor_transport`'s owns_spoors=true discipline; SrvConn refcount is the lifetime authority). The adapter's `recv` blocks on `s2c` via `srvconn_client_recv` (deadline-bounded by `srvconn_set_client_deadline`); `send` writes `c2s` via `srvconn_client_send` (non-blocking; SRVCONN_RING_CAP is sized to a full msize frame so a synchronous single-frame-in-flight client never blocks). **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 spec-to-code broadening -- the framing + ordering invariants are inherited from `kernel/9p_session.c` + `kernel/9p_transport.c` (audited Phase 5); prose validation in the new `docs/reference/86-pouch-stratumd-boot.md` + this row + the audit + a new kernel-internal test. |
| `SYS_ATTACH_9P_SRV` syscall | `kernel/syscall.c::sys_attach_9p_srv_handler` (new; parallel to `sys_attach_9p_handler`), `kernel/include/thylacine/syscall.h::SYS_ATTACH_9P_SRV` (new number), `usr/lib/libt/include/thyla/syscall.h::t_attach_9p_srv` | P6-pouch-stratumd-boot 16c -- the bridge from a client-held `KObj_Srv` connection handle to a mountable `KOBJ_SPOOR` dev9p root. Composes: SrvConn handle lookup -> byte-mode gate (-1 if 9P-mode -- the embedded `p9_client` reservation prevents double-driving the rings) -> kmalloc `p9_srvconn_transport` adapter -> `p9_attached_create` (drives Tversion + Tattach) -> `p9_attached_root_spoor` -> handle_alloc as KOBJ_SPOOR with R\|W\|TRANSFER. **Invariants**: rights gate (`RIGHT_READ` + `RIGHT_WRITE` on the SrvConn handle -- the kernel 9P client writes Twalk/Tread/Twrite and reads Rwalk/Rread/Rwrite); aname bounds (`SYS_ATTACH_ANAME_MAX` same as SYS_ATTACH_9P); n_uname u32 bound check; full failure-path rollback (every kmalloc + every spoor / srvconn ref taken is released on any error). Adopts the same `p9_attached_install_transport` ref-discipline as SYS_ATTACH_9P. **The byte-mode gate matters**: a 9P-mode SrvConn (which corvus mints) is reserved for the kernel-owned p9_client's Tread/Twrite stream -- SYS_ATTACH_9P_SRV rejects it because a second 9P client over the same rings would interleave frames. Pouch sockets (sub-chunk 12) post BYTE-mode via `SYS_POST_SERVICE_BYTE`, so stratumd's `/srv/stratum-fs` listener naturally produces byte-mode SrvConns. **stalk-3b-β update (`46ff378` D + the 3b-β-E close `d6724a0`):** the source endpoint moved `KObj_Srv` -> a `KOBJ_SPOOR` CSRVCLIENT conn Spoor (C1 `42ce2e0`; reached by open=connect on a byte-mode `/srv` service via `kernel/stalk.c` -> `kernel/devsrv.c::devsrv_open_connect`, NOT SYS_SRV_CONNECT). The handler lookup is now KOBJ_SPOOR + CSRVCLIENT flag + `devsrv_conn_of`; the composition is the SHARED `kernel/9p_attach.c::srvconn_attach_dev9p_root` (which also drives `devsrv_open_connect`'s 9p-mode path). The embedded per-SrvConn `p9_client` is RETIRED (D `46ff378`) -- the byte-mode gate no longer reserves "the embedded client" (there is none); it enforces a clean separation: a 9P-mode service is connected via open=connect directly (yielding a dev9p root), so attaching one is meaningless and rejected. **3b-β-E F1 (`devsrv.c`):** `srvconn_attach_dev9p_root` calls `srvconn_set_kernel_attached` once the adapter commits, and `devsrv_read`/`devsrv_write`'s CSRVCLIENT branches MUST honor it (`kernel_attached -> -1`) -- the no-direct-I/O-on-the-rings guard the KOBJ_SRV r/w arms carry, which had to FOLLOW the endpoint from KOBJ_SRV to KOBJ_SPOOR (regression test `devsrv.kernel_attached_io_refused`). **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 spec-to-code broadening -- prose validation in `docs/reference/86-pouch-stratumd-boot.md` + `docs/reference/70-devsrv.md` + `memory/audit_stalk3b_closed_list.md` + this row + the audit + kernel + integration tests. |
| `SYS_PIVOT_ROOT` syscall + `territory_pivot_root` | `kernel/syscall.c::sys_pivot_root_handler` (new), `kernel/territory.c::territory_pivot_root` (new core; companion to existing `territory_chroot`), `kernel/include/thylacine/syscall.h::SYS_PIVOT_ROOT` (new number), `usr/lib/libt/include/thyla/syscall.h::t_pivot_root` | P6-pouch-stratumd-boot 16c -- the long-running-Proc root swap that joey performs LAST in bringup to flip its territory from the bootstrap devramfs root to the disk-backed Stratum FS root. Closes the v1.x note in `usr/joey/joey.c:293-304` ("v1.x adds SYS_UNCHROOT or a proper pivot_root"). **Semantics** (mirrors Linux `pivot_root(2)` minus the put_old argument -- the displaced root is simply unreferenced): under the Proc's territory lock, `spoor_ref(new_root_spoor)` then atomically replace `territory->root_spoor` with the new ref then `spoor_clunk(old_root_spoor)`. Idempotent on same-spoor (returns 0 without bumping). **Why distinct from re-`SYS_CHROOT`**: the existing `territory_chroot` allows the displaced root's ref to drop, but it is documented as "subsequent SYS_CHROOT replaces it"; a NEW syscall + core makes the long-running-Proc usage explicit and audit-trackable, and lets the per-Proc semantics evolve (e.g., post-pivot `/sbin` bind-survivor in v1.x) without re-litigating SYS_CHROOT's contract. **Rights gate**: source fd is `KOBJ_SPOOR` with `RIGHT_READ`. **Invariants**: I-1 (the new root_spoor is a tree the caller already has read rights on -- isolation preserved); I-3 (mount DAG is per-Territory; a pivot replaces one root without introducing cycles); territory-mount-refcount consistency (the displaced root's mounts in the per-Proc mount table are NOT carried over -- mount entries are root-relative and the caller is responsible for re-establishing any binds against the new root; for joey's v1.0 usage that re-establishment is none, since pivot is the last bringup step). **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 spec-to-code broadening -- prose validation in this row + `docs/reference/86-pouch-stratumd-boot.md` + the audit + kernel + boot-path integration tests. |
| `kernel_attached` SrvConn gate (16c-integration) | `kernel/srvconn.{c,h}::srvconn_set_kernel_attached` + `srvconn_is_kernel_attached`; `struct SrvConn::kernel_attached` (new field); `kernel/handle.c::handle_release_obj` KOBJ_SRV branch; `kernel/syscall.c::sys_attach_9p_srv_handler` setter call (at end, after all failure paths); `kernel/9p_srvconn_transport.c::srvconn_transport_close` (now does srvconn_teardown + srvconn_unref) | P6-pouch-stratumd-boot 16c-integration (`457f22d`). Closes the CORVUS-DESIGN.md section 6.2 "close handle = close connection" hazard for kernel-attached SrvConns: when the kernel 9P client wraps a byte-mode SrvConn, the c2s + s2c rings are LOAD-BEARING -- a userspace t_close on the now-redundant KObj_Srv handle MUST NOT EOF the rings. Pre-fix this was the smoking-gun bug: joey's idiomatic `t_close(sd_srv_fd)` post-attach triggered `srvconn_teardown` via handle_close, EOFing c2s before the very first Twalk could land. **Mechanism**: new one-way flag on `struct SrvConn` (false -> true); atomic store-release set by `srvconn_set_kernel_attached(cn)` at the END of `sys_attach_9p_srv_handler` (after all failure paths cleared, before the syscall returns the attach_fd to userspace); atomic load-acquire read by `handle_release_obj`'s KOBJ_SRV branch. If true: SKIP `srvconn_teardown`; only `srvconn_unref`. Teardown migrates to the adapter's `transport.close` (which now does `srvconn_teardown` + `srvconn_unref`); this fires at `p9_attached_destroy` time, when the LAST KOBJ_SPOOR handle referencing the attach session drops. **Invariants**: I-9 (no wakeup is lost; the release-acquire pairing on the atomic flag ensures a cross-CPU userspace close either sees the flag set OR the syscall has not yet returned the attach_fd -- userspace cannot close a handle it has not yet received); I-2 / I-6 (rights monotonicity unaffected -- the flag adds no privilege); teardown idempotency (the non-kernel-attached path is unchanged; `srvconn_teardown` is safe-to-call-twice via the `chan_set_eof` spin_lock pattern). **Regression test**: `kernel/test/test_9p_srvconn_transport.c::kernel_attached_skips_teardown_on_handle_close` (two-part: control teardown + kernel-attached no-teardown). **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 spec-to-code broadening -- prose validation in `srvconn.h::kernel_attached` field comment + the new test + this row + the audit. |
| Host-side pool populate via existing `stratumd + stratum-fs` (host build infra) | `tools/build.sh::build_stratum_pool_fixture` (orchestrates stratumd start, stratum-fs writes for each corpus file, stratumd stop); Stratum-side: NO new code -- uses already-shipped `~/projects/stratum/v2/build/.../stratumd` + `stratum-fs` binaries unchanged | P6-pouch-stratumd-boot 16c -- the host-side "installer" that pre-populates `pool.img` with Thylacine's boot binary corpus (joey, corvus, stratumd, pouch-hello-*, /system.key) before QEMU boot. Equivalent in role to a Linux distro's installer copying squashfs contents onto a fresh disk, run at HOST build time instead of at runtime. Without it, joey cannot pivot to the disk-backed FS (the binaries would be unreachable post-pivot, breaking every joey spawn that resolves a `/sbin/*` or `/pouch-hello-*` path from devramfs). **The 'installer' is shell orchestration of existing Stratum v2 tools**: `stratum-mkfs` (already wired) creates the empty pool; `stratumd` is started in the background listening on a temp Unix socket; `stratum-fs write` (the audited 9P-CLI client subcommand that does `lcreate + buffered Twrite + auto-fsync`) writes each corpus file under its target path; stratumd is shut down. NO new Stratum-side code; NO Stratum-side audit-bearing surface; the populate path exercises the already-audited stratumd Twrite/Tcreate + the already-audited stratum-fs 9P-client. **Invariants**: the populate is HOST-side only (read-only access to the host file tree; the only Thylacine-side artifact is the pool.img bytes); it does NOT modify Thylacine kernel state or affect runtime behavior beyond the static contents of pool.img; the populated FS is a snapshot of the host directory at build time (no incremental update -- pool.img is rebuilt from scratch each invocation). v1.x runtime installer (a Thylacine `/sbin/installer` that runs in an alternate boot path) would PROVE the same flow via the runtime 9P write surface from inside Thylacine -- 16c v1.0 ships the bake-at-build-time expedient that reuses the SAME Stratum-side Twrite/Tcreate code paths. NOT audit-bearing on the Thylacine kernel surface (host build infra). **No new spec** -- prose validation in `docs/reference/86-pouch-stratumd-boot.md` "### The 16c live-medium + host-bake + pivot design" section + this row. |
| FS-mutation syscalls (create / fsync / readdir) | `kernel/syscall.c` (`sys_walk_create_handler` / `sys_fsync_handler` / `sys_readdir_handler`), `kernel/dev9p.c` (real `dev9p_create` -- today a stub -- + new `dev9p_fsync` / `dev9p_readdir`), `kernel/devramfs.c` (create/fsync/readdir impls), `kernel/include/thylacine/dev.h` (new `.fsync` / `.readdir` vtable slots), `kernel/include/thylacine/syscall.h` (`SYS_WALK_CREATE = 54` / `SYS_FSYNC = 55` / `SYS_READDIR = 56` + ABI), `usr/lib/libt/include/thyla/syscall.h` + `usr/lib/libthyla-rs` wrappers | Convergence-detour FS foundation (IDENTITY-DESIGN.md section 9.2), pulled ahead of A-1b corvus persistence per the 2026-05-28 sequencing decision (real persistence needs create+fsync+readdir; none existed at A-1a). The kernel 9P client already implements the wire half (`p9_client_lcreate`/`mkdir`/`fsync`/`readdir`) and `Dev` already has a `.create` slot -- this is syscall wrappers + the real `dev9p_create` + two new vtable slots, NOT new protocol. **The create + write + fsync path is the AEGIS/mallocng-adjacent surface from Phase 6 -- prosecute hard.** Invariants: rights gates (`RIGHT_WRITE` on parent for create + on fd for fsync; `RIGHT_READ` for readdir); single-component name bounds + `/`/`\0` reject; `perm` reserved-`DM*`-bit reject; `DMDIR`-fold dispatch (mkdir vs lcreate); create carries caller `primary_gid` into the 9P gid field (full owner attribution = A-2c/A-2d/A-3 SEAM); `readdir` buffer bounds (<= `SYS_RW_MAX`) + Spoor-offset advance + EOD=0; durability "write-then-fsync" contract on the integrity FS; dev9p fid lifecycle on the failed-create path (no UAF / no fid leak). Per-file rwx ENFORCEMENT (no id bypass, I-22) is A-2d, NOT this surface -- there is nothing to bypass yet. **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 spec-to-code broadening -- prose validation in IDENTITY-DESIGN.md section 9.2 + this row + the audit + the runtime test suite. Split FS-alpha (create) / FS-beta (fsync+readdir) / one focused audit. **#99 addendum (the #102 create errno-loss fix; the authoritative copy is the ARCH section 25.4 row):** `dev9p_create` collapsed every Tlcreate/Tmkdir Rlerror to NULL and `sys_walk_create_handler` returned a bare `-1` (== `-T_E_PERM`, the generic EPERM/EIO sentinel), so a concurrent/duplicate create's `-EEXIST` reached EL0 as EPERM (gopls's parallel filecache logged "operation not permitted"). Fix: a transient `dev9p_priv.create_errno` records the real `-errno`; the handler returns `dev9p_create_errno(nc)` (self-gating -1 for non-dev9p/unrecorded; clamped to `[-4095,-2]`) so EL0 sees the true EEXIST and the go-fork's `os.OpenFile` retries Open. Prosecute: no stale/shared `create_errno` (the create-target `nc` is a fresh clone-walk priv, kzalloc'd 0, read once before spoor_clunk); no non-dev9p behavior change; the clamp fails safe over a hostile ecode. **F1 (SMP-gate + holotype catch):** errno-propagation alone spuriously failed 2/10 under a race -- a loser's Open->ENOENT installs a NEGATIVE (parent,name) dentry, so its retry-Open served the stale negative RPC-free -> ENOENT. Both dev9p_create failure arms now, on `rc == -T_E_EXIST`, `larder_dentry_invalidate_name(parent_path, name)` before returning NULL (EEXIST proves existence + the invalidate bumps the gen, closing the re-cache race; the success path already did this, the EEXIST arm returned NULL before reaching it) -> the loser's own EEXIST-invalidate precedes its retry-Open -> 10/10 under the go-fs 6b 8-way race. F2/F3 = mkdir fid_suspect symmetry + dir-create mid-sequence errno record. Regression `dev9p.create_errno_propagates_eexist` (the -17 return AND the dentry drop) + `go-fs` 6b (race) + 6c (deterministic O_EXCL-EEXIST). Holotype (Fable 5) 0/1(fixed)/0/4; `memory/audit_99_closed_list.md`. `docs/reference/96-fs-mutation.md`. |

| Universal death-interruptible sleep (`*_INTR`) | `kernel/sched.c` (`sleep`/`tsleep` generalized register-then-observe of `group_exit_msg` + the `SLEEP_INTR`/`TSLEEP_INTR` return), `kernel/include/thylacine/thread.h` (per-Thread `wait_lock` protecting `rendez_blocked_on`; ONLY the owner mutates it), `kernel/include/thylacine/rendez.h` (the `*_INTR` sentinel + contract), `kernel/proc.c` (`proc_group_terminate` walks `p->threads` + wakes each sleeper via `wait_lock`->`rendez_blocked_on`; `exits()` folds into the SAME universal wake), and EVERY blocking site's "on `*_INTR` -> cleanup -> return" arm: `kernel/poll.c`, `kernel/pipe.c` (read + write), `kernel/devnotes.c`, `kernel/srvconn.c` (client + server recv), `kernel/devsrv.c` (accept), `kernel/irqfwd.c`, `kernel/proc.c` (`wait_pid`) | Task #811 -- the F1=B completion of `SYS_EXIT_GROUP` (#809-audit F1); ARCH §8.8.1. **The wait/wake primitive itself -- prosecute hard.** Invariant: I-9 GENERALIZED (no death-wake lost between a sleeper's cond-check and its sleep, for EVERY rendez sleep -- register-then-observe under the per-Thread `wait_lock`, the Plan 9 `p->rlock` analog); completes I-24's "no Thread runs at EL0 after ZOMBIE" for the indefinite-sleeper class. Lock order: `wait_lock` is the OUTERMOST wait-lock (`wait_lock -> g_timerwait.lock -> r->lock`; waker `g_proc_table_lock -> wait_lock -> wakeup`); acyclic because only the owning Thread WRITES `rendez_blocked_on` and no sleeper holds `g_proc_table_lock` below `wait_lock` (`wait_pid` drops it first). Death unwinds at the EL0-return tail (`el0_return_die_check`), NEVER inside `sleep()` (would strand caller locks). SAK = serial BREAK (line condition, not data -> unforgeable + stateless recognizer). **No new spec** -- prose validation in IDENTITY-DESIGN.md §9.8 + this row + the audit + tests. |

corrects the same-day "no corvus lift" note):** the coordinator PULLS the DEK with the login-forwarded token over its own corvus connection (§6.3), enabled by (a) the pouch `connect()` walking to corvus's `ctl` fid (`usr/lib/pouch/patches/0006-pouch-sockets.patch`, audit-bearing boundary-line; | A-5c RECOVER recovery keyslot (corvus) | `usr/corvus/src/main.rs` (the new `VERB_RECOVER`=8 handler [`subject_kind` 0=system / 1=user]; the BIP-39 wordlist + phrase gen/decode/checksum; the per-user `recovery.corvus` mint at `handle_user_create` + the phrase returned in the OK response; the keypair re-wrap + rename-swap of `hybrid.corvus` / `system-wrap`; the fresh-phrase roll), `usr/login/` (the login recovery path + the create-time phrase surfacing); **A-5c-b host-bake (user-voted 2026-06-05)**: NEW `usr/lib/corvus-crypto/` (shared `no_std` crypto lib extracted from corvus -- the CRVS wrap layout + `argon2id_kek` + AEGIS wrap/unwrap + BIP-39 codec + keypair-gen, parameterized over an RNG), NEW `usr/corvus-mint/` (host-target minter, `OsRng`), `tools/build.sh` (build corvus-mint host-side + bake the `/var/lib/corvus` dir-chain + `system-wrap` / `system-recovery-wrap`, SYSTEM-owned), `usr/corvus/src/main.rs` (`system_identity_load` at boot + the real `handle_admin_elevate` argon2id+AEGIS unwrap of `system-wrap`, replacing the `b"thylacine"` byte-compare + RECOVER(`subject_kind=0`) console gate) | Convergence-detour A-5c (IDENTITY-DESIGN.md §9.9 design pass + CORVUS-DESIGN §5.5.1/§5.6/§6.4/§8/§9; user votes 2026-06-05: user-held-only [NO hostowner escrow] + mandatory enrollment + host-bake the system identity). **AEGIS/mallocng-adjacent recovery crypto -- prosecute hard.** A recovery keyslot = a 2nd wrap of the SAME hybrid keypair under a recovery-phrase KEK (`recovery.corvus` / `system-recovery-wrap`); it re-wraps the KEYPAIR not the DEK -> the Stratum DEK envelopes stay valid -> **NO Stratum / kernel surface**. Invariants: **C-28 no-escrow** (corvus stores no copy of a user's keypair/DEK recoverable by any authority other than that user's passphrase OR own phrase; the hostowner has NO user-data-recovery verb -> D3 mutually-encrypted-homes survives a malicious hostowner); **C-27** (per-user keyslot minted at USER_CREATE, displayed once, never persisted; same keypair as `hybrid.corvus`; domain-separated AD `"thylacine-corvus-recovery-v1"`); **C-20** generalized. Prosecute: the **gate** (RECOVER(user) = phrase + rate-limit ONLY, no session / no `CAP_HOSTOWNER`; RECOVER(system) additionally console-attached -- a token-only or non-console bypass is the bug); **phrase brute-force** (the C-16 rate-limit is the online defense; a weak-RNG / partial-checksum bug would matter); **secret hygiene** (mlock + `explicit_bzero` of phrase, recovery_KEK, keypair, new_KEK on EVERY path incl. error); the **twin-wrap atomicity** (re-wrap `hybrid.corvus` AND roll `recovery.corvus` -- a crash between must not strand the user un-recoverable nor leave the old phrase live; rename-swap, A-1.6); **no-DEK-rewrite correctness** (the recovered keypair must byte-equal the original so existing envelopes decapsulate); the **USER_CREATE OK-response growth** (callers parse the appended phrase; no overrun). **A-5c-b (host-bake) additionally**: the `corvus-crypto` extraction MUST preserve byte-identical wraps (the A-5c-a / A-1b / A-5b wrap + DEK-envelope paths are behavior-unchanged -- re-validate the boot self-test + USER_CREATE enrollment E2E); a host-minted `system-wrap` and the on-device unwrap must round-trip (same CRVS layout / AD / Argon2id preset -- no drift between `corvus-mint` and corvus); the real `handle_admin_elevate` fails CLOSED on a missing / corrupt / wrong-passphrase `system-wrap` (no byte-compare fallback survives); RECOVER(`subject_kind=0`) requires console attachment; the bake lands the files SYSTEM-owned where corvus's chroot resolves them (joey's runtime `mkdir_or_open` no-ops over the baked dir-chain). **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 broadening -- prose validation in IDENTITY-DESIGN.md §9.9 + CORVUS-DESIGN §5.5.1/§5.6 + this row + the audit + corvus unit tests + the boot E2E (create -> capture phrase -> lose passphrase -> RECOVER -> re-login -> home decrypts; system: ADMIN_ELEVATE + RECOVER(system)). |

Path resolution is a privilege boundary -- prosecute hard. **Invariant I-28** (path-resolution containment + per-component X-search) + **I-1** (per-territory `/srv` makes a 2nd user's coordinator unnameable). Prosecute: `..` escape above `root_spoor`; per-component X-search bypass (symlink / `..` / mount-cross tricks); Spoor lifetime across N hops on the `trail` AND across a cross (the `clone_walk_zero` transient) (UAF / double-clunk / leak); mount-key collision (two sessions same `dc`+`qid.path`, distinct `devno`); STALK_MOUNT-vs-cross final-element correctness (MREPL re-keys the same point); mount-cross into a tree lacking X (the MOUNTED root's perms govern); **[stalk-3a]** the per-Spoor `SrvRegistry` ref lifecycle (one ref per devsrv-Spoor-instance-with-aux=reg; clone+walk0 bumps, `devsrv_close` drops; drain-on-last-unref; no UAF/double-free; registry-scoped tombstone); **[stalk-3b]** the connection-handle reconciliation (KOBJ_SPOOR endpoint; `devsrv_conn_of` for `SO_PEERCRED`; non-dup-able conn Spoor; the 9P-unification's blocking attach-at-open + the dev9p `attached_owner` refcount reuse); **[stalk-3c]** per-territory `/srv` isolation + the ABI-break surface (AEGIS/mallocng-adjacent via the corvus DEK path it unblocks). **#79 ENOTDIR addendum**: resolution through a non-directory now answers `T_E_NOTDIR` (new errno 20) instead of `T_E_NOENT`, gated on `parent->qid.type & QTDIR` BEFORE the X-search (mode-independence -- the x bit on a non-directory is meaningless for traversal) plus the POUNCE partial-walk arm on `sts[k-1]`. The fail-ordering invariant is untouched (the gate only tests the already-resolved tip). The AUTHORITATIVE prosecution copy is the ARCH section 25.4 row. **#81 dot-gate addendum (the lexical-normalization twin):** `.` and `..` are path COMPONENTS, so the position they resolve in must be a directory too -- `/etc/passwd/..` is ENOTDIR, not a lexical pop back to `/etc`. stalk handles both tokens itself (they never reach `Dev.walk`), so the #79 gate never saw them and a file tip silently accepted both. `stalk_tip_is_dir` now gates both arms. Prosecute on any change: (a) the type is read UNCROSSED, unlike the #79 gate -- `..` pops a trail ENTRY (the pop lands on `trail[depth-2]` whichever Spoor occupies the tip, so crossing buys nothing) and `.` must leave the resolution exactly where it was, since under STALK_MOUNT `/mnt` deliberately does not cross and `/mnt/.` must equal `/mnt` (crossing on `.` would INTRODUCE a divergence; `stalk.dot_notdir_mount` pins the equality); (b) I-28 is STRENGTHENED, not touched -- the gate can only turn a success into a failure, never move a pop further up, so nothing that stopped at `start` can now pass it, and at depth 0 the subject is `start` itself (an `openat()` on a non-directory fd answers ENOTDIR); (c) there is no "type fetch failed" case to disposition -- `qid.type` is a field, so the gate takes no lock, issues no RPC, and cannot fail. SCOPE: absolute, dirfd-relative AND cwd-relative paths are all gated. The cwd-relative leg was the #83 gap -- `cwd_lexical_resolve` normalized the dots away before stalk saw them -- and is CLOSED (the #83 addendum below). A trailing slash was the same family and is CLOSED at #82 (the addendum below). **#84 dot X-search addendum:** `.` and `..` are lookups performed IN the tip, so POSIX requires X there -- measured: with `chmod 000 d`, `stat("d")` succeeds but `d/.`, `d/..` and `d/x` are all EACCES. `stalk_tip_may_search` gates BOTH arms (the task named only `..`; the two measure identically). Type gate stays FIRST (a 0000 regular file is ENOTDIR, not EACCES); UNCROSSED tip, same object as the type gate; I-28 strengthened not touched; no single-hop twin (SYS_WALK_OPEN rejects dots outright). Cost measured as two A/Bs: FREE on the pounce path (consumes `carried`), +1 stat on the per-component path -- an ordinary hop's cost, Larder-served on dev9p. An in-guest DENIAL probe is impossible (joey holds CAP_HOSTOWNER, which bypasses perm_check) -- `stalk.dot_xsearch` (Proc with CAP_NONE) proves the denial, `ergo-1(e)` proves a real user can still `cd ../..`. The AUTHORITATIVE prosecution copy is the ARCH section 25.4 row. **The single-hop twin also landed here** (the gate #80 recorded as owed to #81): `sys_walk_open_handler` + `sys_walk_create_handler` gate `src->qid.type & QTDIR` before their permission checks. #80 justified it as disambiguating the clone-walk NULL; MEASURING it showed that is not the reachable case -- a 0644 file answered EACCES (the X-search denied on the missing x bit) while a 0755 file would have answered ENOENT, so the gate's real job is #79's mode-INDEPENDENCE. All 18 Devs were checked: every walkable root stamps QTDIR (8 via `dev_simple_attach`, dev9p + devsrv at birth); the QTFILE roots are exactly the hierarchy-less leaves the gate should reject. Regressions: `stalk.dot_notdir` + `stalk.dot_notdir_mount` + joey's always-run boot-fatal `probe81` (all four ENOTDIR legs revert-probed INDEPENDENTLY -- with both gates removed the probe reports `file..=2 file.=3 basefd..=8 basefd.=9`, i.e. each resolved; the 0755 leg pins mode-independence, and the three directory legs are the regression a too-eager gate would trip). One benign constant bump: `JOEY_BLOB_MAX` 512->640 KiB (joey hit 524952 B against a 524288 B bound -- 99.87% full, so the next probe of any size was going to trip it; the permanently-resident static-BSS shape of that buffer is task #85). **#82 trailing-slash addendum (the third gate of the lexical family):** POSIX 4.13 -- a pathname holding at least one non-'/' character and ending in one or more '/' names a DIRECTORY. The tokenizer collapses separator runs, so the trailing '/' was simply DROPPED and `/etc/passwd/` resolved the file. `path_has_trailing_slash` records the fact once from the RAW path (nothing downstream remembers the separator existed) and THREE gates consume it -- because `stalk_core` has exactly three success exits and TWO never reach the quarry: (A) the ordinary `return quarry`, gated on `quarry->qid.type`; (B) the FID-LIFECYCLE cached-open `return co`; (C) the POUNCE STALK_STAT walk-query `return NULL`. B and C read the leaf's fused record `sts[nrun-1].qid_type`, which IS the quarry there: both exits are reachable ONLY after a scan proved no component in the run -- the leaf included -- is a mount point, so no cross is owed and crossed == uncrossed. All three sit BEFORE the final permission check (the #79 type-before-permission order: an unreadable `file/` is ENOTDIR, not EACCES). Prosecute on any change: (a) the subject is the CROSSED quarry -- the OPPOSITE of the #81 dot gate, and deliberately: `.`/`..` ask WHERE RESOLUTION STANDS (so `/mnt/.` must equal `/mnt`, uncrossed) while a trailing slash asks WHAT THE PATH NAMES (the crossed result -- `/mnt/` names the MOUNTED ROOT). OBSERVABLE, not academic: `territory.c::mount()` has NO type check, so a file mounted over a directory would make `/mnt/` wrongly legal and a directory mounted over a file wrongly ENOTDIR -- gating the uncrossed point is wrong in BOTH directions, and `stalk.trailing_slash_mount` builds both. Placing it on the quarry gets every amode right for free (STALK_MOUNT's deliberately-uncrossed quarry IS what SYS_MOUNT names). (b) all three sites must stay -- each was revert-probed INDEPENDENTLY and each fails at its OWN assertion. (c) "/" and "//" are EXEMPT (POSIX scopes the rule to a pathname with at least one non-'/' char; they have no component before the run) -- which is why the discriminator scans BACK from the end rather than testing the last byte. (d) a trailing slash on a MISSING path stays ENOENT -- the gate never pre-empts a real walk miss. SCOPE, same shape as #81 and closed the same way: absolute + dirfd-relative + (since #83) cwd-relative are ALL gated. Pre-#83 the join rebuilt the path component-by-component and never emitted a trailing separator, so `open("f/")` from the cwd resolved the file. `SYS_CHDIR` always cleans but has carried its own QTDIR check since LS-4, so it was never wrong. NOT COVERED -- the USERSPACE splitter: `unlink("f/")` never reaches this gate, because pouch's `__pouch_open_parent` rejects ANY trailing slash with EINVAL first -- wrong in both directions (EINVAL where POSIX says ENOTDIR for a file, AND a rejection where `rmdir("dir/")` must SUCCEED); task #86, a boundary-line bug, not a resolver one. That also CORRECTS #82's own enqueued headline ("`unlink("f/")` succeeds"): measuring showed it neither succeeds nor reaches stalk. Regressions: `stalk.trailing_slash` (the three sites + the exemptions + the directory regressions + nowa parity; the site-B leg asserts `g_fix_co_calls == 1` so it cannot silently re-test site A) + `stalk.trailing_slash_mount` (both mount directions) + joey's always-run boot-fatal `probe82` -- which MEASUREMENT showed reaches all THREE sites on the live FS (the O_PATH legs take A, the stat leg C, and the OREAD leg B, since a freshly-created file is fully page-cached; with only the B gate reverted the OREAD leg is the ONE that flips to resolving, which is how the routing was established -- correcting an earlier comment of mine that claimed B was unreachable in-guest). **#83 cwd-join addendum (the gates only bind what stalk SEES).** The #79/#81/#82 gates all live in `stalk`, so the LS-4 cwd join -- which resolved `.`/`..` and dropped a trailing separator BEFORE calling stalk -- bypassed every one of them for the commonest path form in a shell. Measured pre-fix from a cwd holding a file `f`: `open("f/..")`->fd, `open("f/.")`->fd, `open("f/")`->fd, `stat("f/")`/`stat("f/..")`->0, **`open("nope/..")`->fd**, and `chdir("f/..")`/`chdir("nope/..")`->0. The last two make this a RESOLUTION bug, not a conformance nit: a lexical `..` pops a component WITHOUT proving it exists, so a path through a directory that is not there opened successfully, and chdir passed its QTDIR check against the parent it had already massaged the path into -- checking the wrong object entirely. FIX = a UNIFICATION, not a fourth gate: the two jobs split into `cwd_join` (RESOLUTION -- verbatim; `.`/`..`/a trailing separator survive so stalk gates them) and `cwd_lexical_resolve` (CANONICALIZATION -- narrowed to ONE production role, the string SYS_CHDIR stores in `dot_path`). `territory_resolve_cwd` renames to `territory_join_cwd`; SYS_OPEN / SYS_STAT / `exec_resolve_from_namespace` take the join; SYS_CHDIR takes BOTH in three ordered steps -- (1) join verbatim, (2) stalk it, (3) canonicalize the ALREADY-STALKED join with `dot == NULL` so `dot_path` is NOT re-read and a peer thread's concurrent chdir cannot make the stored string disagree with what stalk validated. Prosecute on any change: (a) **I-28 is unaffected** -- containment never rested on the join; stalk clamps `..` at its trail floor exactly as it already does for an absolute path containing `..`, the case ARCH's LS-4 row explicitly reasons about ("a hostile un-cleaned join cannot escape"), so cwd-relative resolution now runs the SAME code as absolute rather than a parallel lexical one -- strictly LESS code participates in containment. (b) step (3) must stay, and must stay on the join with `dot == NULL`: without it `cd d; cd ..` stores `/dir/d/..` and grows unboundedly across repeated `cd ..` (resolution still works, so ONLY getcwd observes it); re-reading `dot_path` instead would reopen a peer-thread race. (c) the canonicalizer must NEVER return to the resolution path. (d) known consequences, all accepted: a cwd-relative path spelled with `..` no longer pounces (`path_has_dotdot` disables it -- the same cost an absolute `..` path always paid, on exactly the paths that were resolving incorrectly); the joined path is longer so `SYS_OPEN_PATH_MAX` (1024) is reached sooner, surfacing as a bare `-1` since ENAMETOOLONG is not yet registered (ER-x); a DELETED cwd now fails relative resolution, matching POSIX/Linux. Regressions: `territory.cwd_join` + joey's always-run boot-fatal `probe83` (every divergence leg + the `d/..`/`d/.`/`d/`/`.`/`..`/`f` regressions + both chdir gate legs + the `cd d; cd ..` -> `getcwd() == "/p83-cwd-dir"` canonicality leg). THREE independent revert probes, each failing at its OWN assertion: collapsing inside `cwd_join` -> the unit test fails; reverting only the production call site -> `probe83` fails its divergence + chdir-gate legs while the unit test still PASSES; skipping step (3) -> only the getcwd leg fails, reporting `cwd='/p83-cwd-dir/d/..'`. The **F1 amode guard in `stalk()` must gain any new amode** a sub-chunk adds (stalk-2 added STALK_MOUNT; stalk-3 adds none -- post/connect ride existing syscalls). One focused round per sub-chunk (stalk-1/2/3a/3b done; stalk-3c audit CLEAN 0/0/0/3 -- all P3 doc-staleness; stalk-3 ARC COMPLETE). **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 broadening -- prose in `docs/STALK-DESIGN.md` + ARCH §9.6.7 + this row + the audit + tests. |

Prose validation in `docs/LOOM.md` + `docs/reference/107-loom.md` (the Loom-6 section) + this row + the audit + the kernel tests. **Fid-lift audit F1 addendum (SQPOLL kthreads are I-32-charged; `d38db92f`):** `LOOM_SETUP_SQPOLL` spawns a per-ring `kproc` kthread pinning 32 KiB of unswappable kstack, and it ran under the EXEMPT kproc -- so an unprivileged Proc minted one per ring, uncounted, bounded only by its handle table. That was defensible at `PROC_HANDLE_MAX` = 64 (the original F4 disposition said exactly this: bounded transitively); the #198 lift to 1024 made the transitive bound 32 MiB and voided it. Now `proc_sqpoll_charge` charges the CREATOR before `loom_start_sqpoll`, `proc_thread_cap_ok` sums `thread_count + loom_sqpoll_count`, and `loom_free` settles it exactly once under the `Loom.sqpoll_charged` latch. Prosecute on any change: the count lives in a SEPARATE field from `thread_count` deliberately -- exit/reap logic must never wait on a phantom thread, so do not merge them; the charge must precede the spawn (a started-then-refused kthread is a leak); `sqpoll_charged` must gate the uncharge on EVERY teardown path including a start FAILURE (a refused setup that still uncharged would let the budget drift upward); `sqpoll_owner` is lifetime-safe only because `KObj_Loom` is I-5 non-transferable, so the ring cannot outlive its creator's Proc -- prosecute any future transferability grant as breaking that argument first. `struct Proc` 384 -> 392 (assert bumped deliberately). Regression `loom.sqpoll_charges_thread_budget` (refusal at the cap, no over-settle by the refused ring's free, plain rings uncharged, and the close -> uncharge -> fits-again round trip). |

| Proc exit handle-close (#926) | `kernel/proc.c` (`proc_close_handles_at_exit` -- the static helper, since #68 setting `exit_close_active` internally; the live_peers-gated close windows in BOTH `exits()` and `thread_exit_self` [the #926 top-of-exits `thread_count==1` call is RETIRED -- #68 round-2 F2]; the `proc_free` fallback comment), `kernel/notes.c` (`thread_die_pending`'s `exit_close_active` gate), `kernel/include/thylacine/thread.h` (`exit_close_active`), `kernel/handle.c` (`handle_table_free` lockless-safety comment -- the #844 justification updated for the live_peers==0 ALIVE-Proc call sites) | #926 (U-6f command-substitution prerequisite; user-voted "fix the kernel" 2026-06-08). The kernel previously closed a Proc's handle table ONLY at reap (`handle_table_free` in `proc_free`, ZOMBIE-gated), so a child's inherited pipe write end stayed open in the zombie until the parent reaped it -- a shell draining `$(cmd)`'s stdout to EOF hung forever (no `write_eof`). FIX: a SINGLE-thread Proc closes its handle table at the TOP of `exits()` while still RUNNING + ALIVE + `thread_count==1` -- the only sound spot (t RUNNING so a sleep-capable close hook like a 9P clunk is legal -- sleeping while EXITING trips sched()'s "not RUNNING" assert; p ALIVE so `wait_pid` cannot reap+thread_free mid-close; `thread_count==1` so no peer shares the table). Inherited fds thus close at process termination (correct Unix/Plan 9 semantics); the parent's drain-before-reap sees EOF immediately. **Death-path surface (the #788/#806/#807/#808/#860/#809/#811 lineage) -- the most bug-prone in the tree.** Invariants: **I-7** (the #847 per-Burrow dual refcount -- free only when handle_count==0 AND mapping_count==0 -- makes the inverted handle-close-at-exit / vma-drain-at-reap ordering safe: dropping handle_count at exit while a VMA still maps does NOT free; vma_drain at reap drops the last mapping ref); no double-free (`handle_table_free(NULL)` no-ops; `proc_close_handles_at_exit` NULLs `p->handles`, so `proc_free`'s call no-ops; it remains the real close for multi-thread + the direct `state=ZOMBIE; proc_free()` orphan/rollback paths); no reaper UAF; no sleep-in-EXITING; the `thread_count==1` lockless read is stable (decrement is reap-only via `thread_unlink_from_proc`<-`thread_free`, and the sole caller-thread is not spawning). MULTI-thread Procs keep close-at-reap: their last-Thread EXITING mark is atomic-under-lock with the last-Thread determination, leaving no RUNNING window for a sleeping close -- a v1.x EXITING-protocol restructure (which also closes the killed-single-thread asymmetry: a single-thread Proc killed via `proc_group_terminate`->`thread_exit_self` defers to reap too). **SMP-gated** (default+UBSan x smp4/smp8 N=10, 0 corruption) + focused Opus audit (0 P0 / 0 P1 / 0 P2 / 3 P3, all doc: F1 the handle.c lockless justification updated, F2 the kill-path asymmetry documented, F3 `$errstr`-unchanged-on-subst-spawn-fail cosmetic). Verified by the `u-test::flow_process_pipe` part-(c) regression (drain a child's stdout to EOF BEFORE reaping -- the exact pattern formerly documented as "block forever") + the rewritten `test_sys_spawn_with_fds_child_rights_subset_of_parent` (spawns pipe-sink which BLOCKS reading fd 0, keeping it live for the rights inspection) + `/u-subst-test`. **No new spec** per the 2026-05-23 broadening -- prose in `docs/reference/14-process-model.md` + ARCH section 25.4 + this row + the audit + the regression tests. **#68 ADDENDUM (2026-07-14): the multi-thread + killed close-at-exit -- the v1.x seam PULLED FORWARD.** The Go toolchain promoted the documented asymmetry to a v1.0 defect: every Go binary exits MULTI-thread via SYS_EXIT_GROUP (thread_count decrements only at reap), so its pipe write ends delivered EOF only at REAP -- a parent that drains the child's stdout to EOF BEFORE reaping deadlocked (EOF needed reap, reap needed the parent's wait, the wait waited on EOF: the nora gofmt-on-save :wq hang + the ut `$(go ...)` substitution wedge, session-unrecoverable since ut self-manages notes; fingerprinted by native-substitution-completes vs go-substitution-wedges + hang3=0 [ut pipelines pass because the WNOHANG sweep reaps the Go element while a SIBLING waits EOF -- reaper != EOF-waiter]). FIX at BOTH exit sites (`thread_exit_self` AND -- round-2 F2 -- `exits()`, whose #926 `thread_count==1` gate is RETIRED: thread_count counts unreaped EXITING peers, so it skipped a joined-then-exits native multi-thread program): the LAST live Thread out (live_peers == 0, determined under `g_proc_table_lock`) closes the handles in a deliberately-opened RUNNING+ALIVE window -- drop the lock, `proc_close_handles_at_exit(p)`, re-take, assert the determination held (structural: every peer has committed EXITING [whose residual execution -- clear-child-tid + sched() -- never touches the table] and no new peer can spawn without a RUNNING thread), THEN `proc_become_zombie_locked`. Converges the multi-thread last-out to exits()'s proven three-property shape (RUNNING => sleep-capable close legal; ALIVE => no reap mid-close; sole toucher) and closes the killed-single-thread asymmetry by the same stroke. The close runs under `exit_close_active` (#68-audit R1-F1 + R2-F1 [both P1], Fable catches; set/cleared INSIDE proc_close_handles_at_exit so BOTH sites are covered -- exits()'s is reachable with the LS-5 interrupt-terminate latch deliberately still armed, and a racing kill can set gmsg mid-close): group_exit_msg is set on EVERY SYS_EXIT_GROUP -- a clean exit_group(0) included -- so without the flag thread_die_pending() read true for the closer and the dev9p wb close-flush DROPPED its staged bytes (silent data loss for a file left open at a multi-thread exit; accepted write()s survive exit -- the page-cache contract) + the close-time Tclunk was never sent (F2 [P2]: a server-side fid leak per open dev9p fd on every Go exit). The flag makes thread_die_pending() false for the closer during the one bounded close pass (checked FIRST in notes.c; every caller passes self), restoring the pre-#68 reap-time behavior; the re-admitted wedged-server strand equals the old reap-time exposure, relocated from the parent's wait_pid onto the already-dying Proc -- and NOT breakable by a further kill (the flag suppresses both death legs); precondition = a wedged TRUSTED server; the bounded/abortable close-flush is the recorded v1.x seam (R2-F3). Prosecute on any change: the flag must stay owner-set/cleared + bounded to the close pass + checked before both die legs; a caller passing a non-self thread would break the no-synchronization read. proc_free stays the fallback (orphan/rollback + skipped hooks). Prosecute on any change: the last-out determination stability argument (EXITING peers never touch the table; no spawner exists); the window's three properties; the re-lock assert stays an extinction (a peer appearing = structural corruption); the close must PRECEDE proc_become_zombie_locked (EOF-before-reapable is the contract). Regressions `sys_spawn.killed_child_delivers_pipe_eof_before_reap` (kill a pipe-parked child; EOF must arrive BEFORE any reap; bounded poll; the thread_exit_self site -- REVERT-PROBED) + `sys_spawn.joined_multithread_child_delivers_pipe_eof_before_reap` (/thread-probe joins its workers then t_exits with thread_count > 1 + live_peers == 0; drain to EOF BEFORE reap; the exits() site -- REVERT-PROBED against the retired gate) + the go6.exp E2E (nora gofmt-on-save over an all-piped Go child) + the gofmtdbg7 substitution probe. ADDS a bounded TX ring + the PL011 **TX interrupt** (`uart_rx_handler` -> a `MIS`-dispatching `uart_irq_handler`), a **writer role** across `cons_output_write` (the audited `srvconn.c::chan_role_acquire` #354 shape), and a deadlined room-wait -- closing #75 (pre-existing since P4-B: the loop was byte-by-byte into a lock-free `uart_putc`, so two CPUs interleaved at BYTE granularity and tore a 3-byte glyph + an SGR escape in 10/40 gate boots; the torn continuation byte is what blinded #74's grep, and the same tearing reaches aurora's drain ring, so it corrupts a live TUI, not just a log). **A NEW IRQ PATH ON THE CONSOLE -- the I-27 trusted-path surface, and two new park/wake legs on the wait/wake lineage; prosecute hard.** Contract: a `/dev/cons` write is atomic w.r.t. other console writers (the Linux `atomic_write_lock` position; POSIX guarantees atomicity only for `<=PIPE_BUF` PIPE writes, not terminals). **No new §28 invariant** (a `Dev.write` semantic; both new parks are **I-9** obligations) and **no new spec** (#354's role-park is prose+audit-validated; `cons_poll.tla` untouched + re-run as the gate). Headline obligations: the role + room-wait I-9 register-then-observe (no lost wake, no stale hook, #811 unwind -> short write never a strand); the IRQ drain may use ONLY `wakeup()` on a `Rendez` (the LS-8a IRQ-safety boundary, now load-bearing for a SECOND producer); the TX ring lock stays an irqsave LEAF (never nested with `g_cons.lock`/`g_uart_rx_lock`); the **#67 loss discipline preserved** (a stalled host consumer yields a bounded short write, NEVER a wedged writer -- the deadline is load-bearing); the TXIM enable/disable race (a non-empty ring must never be left with TX interrupts off = a silent-console wedge); Halls/extinction keep the direct bounded `uart_putc` (HX-I) with a bounded pre-dump flush; `cons_output_write` MAY sleep (`spoor_write_common` holds no lock across `dev->write`) but `cons_emit` from echo may NOT -- the two ring callers keep OPPOSITE blocking contracts. Regressions: `cons.tx_role_serializes_writers` (two-writer atomicity; revert-probed: it MUST fail on the pre-P1-F lockless loop); `cons.tx_room_wait_and_deadline` (the audit-F2 item, LANDED 2026-07-28 -- the ring-full short-write-on-deadline leg + the room-wait I-9 wake, both revert-probed against PRODUCTION [an instant drop fails the elapsed assert; deleting `cons_tx_drain_from_irq`'s `freed`-gated `wakeup()` fails the wake]; it drives the real IRQ drain for the wake, DISCARDS its filler so the boot log stays clean, and asserts only OUTSIDE the stalled window so a failure cannot wedge the ring for the rest of the boot); TXIM re-arm; and the `⊢`-tearing gate signature at 0/40. **#76 ADDENDUM (SYS_PUTS joins the role -- the P1-F writer set completed; the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH §25.4 row).** P1-F deliberately left `sys_puts_handler`'s byte-by-byte lock-free `uart_putc` loop as a documented seam; #76 closes it (stage via one `uaccess_copy_in` into `u8 scratch[SYS_RW_STACK]`, then emit through `cons_output_write`). The seam was LIVE and mis-priced: LS-CI caught `"password: "` (login, fd 1) shredded byte-for-byte by `"tapestryd: mode "` (tapestryd, `t_putstr`) -- a login prompt destroyed on the I-27 trusted path -- and the UNSTATED cost was worse than the stated interleave: `cons_drain_tap` fires from `cons_emit`/`cons_emit_wait` ONLY, so the whole native diagnostic stream (83 binaries via `t_putstr`) was INVISIBLE to the G-4 renderer, normal on serial and absent on the framebuffer. The independence rationale never required the direct path (`cons_output_write` is the shared cons layer, not the Dev -- no fd/Spoor/handle/namespace). Prosecute on any change: the copy-in stays BEFORE the role (a page-in under the role stalls every console writer); a copy-in fault emits NOTHING (whole-op EFAULT); the return may now be SHORT (#67 deadline / #811 death), which no caller inspects; ONLCR is load-bearing not cosmetic (the newly-visible output would staircase on aurora's VT, #36). Regression `cons.sys_puts_uses_shared_console_path` must spawn a REAL EL0 binary (the handler takes a user VA; kproc has `pgtable_root == 0`, so no in-kernel caller can reach it) and must sample the NON-BLOCKING `cons_test_drain_count()` before any `cons_drain_read()` (which sleeps on an armed-empty drain -> a pre-fix run would HANG the boot instead of failing). Revert-probed: 1216/1217 FAIL on exactly `SYS_PUTS output reached the drain tap`. **#55 WINSIZE ADDENDUM (console winsize + `tty:winch`; design ARCH §23.5.3, user-voted 2026-07-22; the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH §25.4 row).** The kernel-held console winsize + the ptyfs-grammar consctl `winsize <cols> <rows>` verb/render + the CHANGED-only `tty:winch` to the console owner's PGRP (`notes_post_pgrp`; the console has no ct/fg — the fg refinement is the recorded seam) + the ungated read-only `/dev/winsize` leaf + the consctl MINT-gate widening to attached-OR-renderer -- the renderer-minted consctl is WINSIZE-VERB-ONLY (CCONSWINSZONLY; #55 audit F2: consfeed dominates a winsize report but NOT a global-termios flip that also governs the serial RX path, so +echo from a compromised renderer must not unmask a serial password into the drain; the attached login/ut chain stays full-grammar; cons/consdrain/consfeed gates byte-unchanged) + `cons_stat_native` (the is-a-cons qid contract, disjoint from `PTS_FLAG`; fixes the statless-cons `isatty()==false` pouch latent). Aurora is the geometry authority (writes winsize at first present + every reweave — AURORA.md §4); serial posture = `winsize 0 0`, CPR unaffected. NO new §28 invariant (composes I-27/I-19/I-9); NO new spec (`cons_poll.tla` untouched — state + a post, not a wait/wake protocol). **#95 DROP-COUNTER ADDENDUM (input-path silent-drop instrumentation, 2026-08-03).** ADDS three per-site counters to `struct cons_input` (`rx_drop_raw` / `rx_drop_flush` / `rx_drop_line`, all written under `g_cons.lock`, which every drop site already holds), a `drop_report_pending` flag drained by `console_mgr` into ONE loud line, and the read-only `/ctl/cons` leaf (`cons_rx_drops` / `cons_tx_drops`; the #75/#126 TX counters get their first surface here too). **Diagnostics only -- no control-flow change to the cooking, the ring, or the wake protocol**; the mgr predicate gains a disjunct, which can only ADD a wake, never lose one, and `boot_is_complete()` (the report gate) is a plain atomic load, legal from IRQ context under `g_cons.lock`. Why it exists: a dropped input byte truncates a command that then RUNS ANYWAY (#95 saw `sleep 30` arrive as `sleep 3`), and all three sites were previously silent, so a recurrence produced no evidence at all. **Prosecute on any change: a NEW drop site on this path MUST get its own counter** -- attribution is the whole point, since the three sites have different causes (raw = an ungated producer, `cons_feed_write` being the one that exists; flush = the #174 gate admitting one byte while the flush pushes `line_len + 1`; line = past `CONS_LINE_MAX`) and a lumped counter only moves the question from "did we drop?" to "where?". The report gate must stay tied to boot-complete: the test suite deliberately overflows this ring, so an ungated report would cry wolf every boot AND spend the one-shot latch, disarming the instrument with its own test. Regression `cons.rx_drop_counters` drives all three sites and asserts both the exact count and the NON-attribution to the other two; revert-probed twice (killing `(*site)++` fails the raw leg; lumping all three into `rx_drop_raw` fails the LINE leg -- two different sabotages, two different failures). `devctl.read_cons_format` proves the surface is reachable. The ptyfs twin (`drop_flush`/`drop_line`/`drop_echo` + its own arm-after-selftest gate) is in `docs/reference/136-ptyfs.md`. **#129 ADDENDUM (RX admission: back-pressure on both producers + the ring sizing, 2026-08-03; the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 row).** Closes the two #95-instrumentation defects plus a third the fix found: (1) `cons_feed_write` (the G-4 renderer's entire keyboard) was the one UNGATED producer, returning `n` while the ring dropped the overflow -- the serial console had #174 back-pressure, the framebuffer console had a lie; (2) `cons_rx_can_accept()` was per-BYTE while the canonical Enter flush is per-LINE (up to CONS_LINE_MAX + 1 = 257 in ONE call) -- **the #126 category error on the RX side**; (3) the SIZING -- `CONS_RING_SIZE` (256) < `CONS_LINE_MAX + 1` (257), so a maximal line did not fit an EMPTY ring, and the obvious gate would have refused it forever (RX paused permanently = a bounded drop traded for a console wedge). Fix: ring 256 -> 512 with a `_Static_assert` tying it to CONS_LINE_MAX; `cons_rx_input` returns `bool` with the room check UNDER `g_cons.lock` atomically with the push; `cons_feed_write` returns a SHORT count; the pre-check reserves the worst-case flush unconditionally (consulting neither termios nor line_len -- both can change under it); and a **1-byte holdback** in `uart_rx_drain_locked` because a peer producer can take the room after the lockless pre-check, by which point the byte is out of DR -- without it the fix would only NARROW the window, invisibly. Headlines: a refusal must change NOTHING (echo moved inside the accepted branch); the SAK + cooked Ctrl-C stay unconditionally accepted (I-27 -- a full ring must never suppress the trusted-path trigger); the under-lock check is the guarantee, the pre-check only an optimization; the holdback is re-offered before the FIFO and can never strand; `rx_drop_ring` must stay ZERO (an invariant witness). The #95 counter list above is PARTLY SUPERSEDED -- the ring-full sites became `rx_bp_raw`/`rx_bp_flush` (back-pressure, RENAMED not reinterpreted) and the report latch is armed only by real loss, since reporting routine back-pressure would spend the one-shot latch and disarm the instrument. Six regressions, four revert-probed to distinct failures (one of them a BUILD failure -- the right place for a sizing invariant). **#136 ROUND-2 ADDENDUM (the audit of #129's own fixes; 0 P0 / 1 P1 / 1 P2 / 3 P3; the AUTHORITATIVE list is the ARCH section 25.4 row).** Headlines: **F1 [P1]** the drain could fall out of its budget loop in the ONE state the #129 addendum declares unreachable (`g_rx_held_valid && !g_rx_paused`) -- the last unit spent on a refusal that then LIFTS -- and it does not self-heal, because a held byte lives in a software variable, not the FIFO, so it raises no RXRIS and `uart_rx_pump` short-circuits on `!g_rx_paused`; the invariant is now restored at that exit. **F2 [P2]** the #133 leaked-state sweep keyed on `g_rx_paused`, which is PRODUCTION state, so a human typing during the test phase would redden an innocent test AND make the backstop discard the very keystroke it exists to protect -- it now keys on a genuinely test-owned flag. **F3 [P3]** the publish/re-observe pair emitted `stlrb` + a plain `ldr`, which ARMv8 does not order (only `STLR`->`LDAR`), so it got the SEQ_CST fence the identical Weft-4 readiness ring already carries -- and the property that actually kept the terminal state unreachable turned out to be a different one, now written down as a do-not-move obligation on `cons_input_read`'s unconditional pump. **F4/F5 [P3]** the `UART_RX_DRAIN_MAX` rationale re-derived (~320 MMIO, not ~128), and aurora's selftest claimed by `ls-gfx.exp` + its write-error arm split from its over-bound arm so the log names the event.  **#152 ADDENDUM (the kernel's own diagnostics join the role).** #76 above says it completed the P1-F writer set and MEASUREMENT at #151's smp8 gate showed it had not: the KERNEL's `uart_puts` callers were never in it, and one prints `Thylacine boot OK`. The gate FAILED on the shredded ABI line (the banner woven through tapestryd's scanout line), and since the harness greps that exact string a HEALTHY guest was reported as a 300s timeout. The #75-audit F3 `cons_tx_flush` fixed only half -- flushing drains what is ALREADY queued while a peer starting a write right after it refills the ring mid-`uart_puts`. ADDS `cons_kernel_writer_begin`/`_end`, which takes the SAME role `cons_output_write` takes and flushes UNDER it; `boot_mark_complete` brackets its whole output. NOT routed through `cons_output_write` because ONLCR is flag-conditional and `g_cons.termios` is BSS-zero at boot, so the wire bytes of a tooling-ABI line would depend on how far the boot had got. Prosecute: never for the extinction/Halls path (HX-I1/HX-I2 -- lock-free + bounded on a dying machine); the flush stays INSIDE the role; a false `begin()` (a #811 death) emits unserialized rather than dropping the line; IRQ echo remains a roleless second producer (unreachable at the banner, recorded residual). **#187 test-side addendum (2026-08-08): every test read of a deferred flag (`cons_test_intr_pending` / `_sak_pending` / `_pollwake_pending`) MUST sit inside a `cons_test_mgr_hold(true/false)` window, in BOTH directions.** The producers end in `wakeup(&g_cons_mgr_rendez)`, so a peer CPU can drain the flag between the produce and the read: a POSITIVE read then fails spuriously, and a NEGATIVE read PASSES spuriously (a wrongly-set flag is consumed before the read, so the assertion is satisfied by the bug it names). Idiom is sample-then-assert -- readings inside the window, release, then assert, because `TEST_ASSERT` returns and an assert inside the window strands the hold onto the NEXT test via `cons_test_release_owned_state`. Exactly three reads are legitimately unheld, all asserting the mgr DID consume. Prosecute a new cons test by running the census (any `cons_test_*_pending()` not preceded by an unmatched `mgr_hold(true)` in its enclosing function) -- the #129-audit F6 fix asserted this sweep was already done when seven sites were bare, and one of them extincted the suite on ~1-in-8 SMP boots. The AUTHORITATIVE copy is the ARCH section 25.4 row. **MODE-WRITE DELIVERY ADDENDUM (PTY-DESIGN "Mode writes deliver, never discard", 2026-08-17; supersedes the LS-8b audit-F1 remedy):** `cons_set_mode_cmd` no longer zeroes `line_len` on every write. A write that CLEARS ICANON delivers the half-assembled `line[]` to the ring as raw bytes (`cons_deliver_partial_line_locked`, under `g_cons.lock`, the RX push's own reader/mgr wakes after the unlock); a write that leaves ICANON as it was leaves the assembly alone; raw->canonical has nothing pending. Why: the F1 remedy rested on "no consumer flips mid-line", and the pts job-control shell flips around every foreground job -- type-ahead landing between the job's last output and the PROMPT-mode re-arm was cooked-echoed, then discarded, PARTIALLY (`sleep 30\r` -> `sle` dropped, `ep 30\r` executed; LS-CI pty-4 via the 11173762 probe). Plan 9 devcons `rawon` and Linux `n_tty_set_termios` both deliver on canonical->raw and discard on nothing. **Prosecute on any change:** (1) the delivery is under `g_cons.lock` and its wakes are AFTER the unlock -- the same I-9 register-then-observe pairing as `cons_rx_input`; (2) a mode write CANNOT back-pressure (no producer holds the bytes), so a short delivery is a REAL DROP under its OWN counter `rx_drop_modeflush` (the #95 rule; NOT `rx_drop_ring`, which must stay zero, and NOT `rx_bp_flush`, which counts refusals) -- do not fold it; (3) canonical->canonical must NOT flush (POSIX; type-ahead into a `-echo` password prompt behaves as on Linux) -- re-adding an unconditional reset re-opens the type-ahead loss; (4) `cons_test_set_termios` carries the SAME rule, or the cooking tests verify a path production does not take; (5) the F1 hazard (a fragment prepending the next line across canonical->raw->canonical) is closed BY delivery -- a change that stops delivering must re-close it some other way. Regression `cons.cook_mode_flip_delivers` (three legs, each drain guarded by the non-blocking depth so a regression reads as red, not as a hang). **ccb597b8-ROUND ADDENDUM (Fable 5, 0 P0 / 0 P1 / 2 P2 / 6 P3, all on the new drop site's WITNESS, none on the delivery):** F1 [P2] the counting path had only a NEGATIVE (leg B, an empty ring) -- a misattribution to `rx_drop_ring` or no count at all read green; `cons.rx_drop_counters` gained legs (d) (512 filler + a 10-byte pending line + `-icanon` -> `rx_drop_modeflush == 10`, `rx_drop_ring`/`rx_bp_flush`/`rx_drop_line`/`rx_bp_raw` unmoved, the 512 filler drains intact) and (e) (507 + 10 -> the 5-byte PREFIX delivered in order after the filler, 5 counted). F3 [P3] the loud one-shot printed `line= ring=` only, so a mode-flush drop spent the latch WITHOUT naming its site -- the line now carries `modeflush=`. F4 [P3] "reachable only by a wedged reader" was FALSE: ut re-arms PROMPT_MODE before it drains, so a paste of a ring's worth of complete lines into a non-reading job plus a trailing partial line meets a full ring at the re-arm -- comments reworded (a hold-and-back-pressure design that would avoid the loss is recorded, not built). F5 [P3] OPEN, a scripture decision: an ISIG-consumed INTR/QUIT/SUSP char does NOT flush the pending canonical line (POSIX NOFLSH-clear default and Linux `n_tty` DO; Plan 9 does not) -- the old unconditional reset masked it, delivery makes it visible (`rm -rf x` typed into a job, then ^C, now arrives at the prompt as pre-typed input). F6/F7 [P3] stale TCSAFLUSH comments (ptyfs selftest, `console.rs`, pouch patch 0021) + 111-cons.md's deleted-test entry / counter table / report example refreshed. F8 [P3] pty-4's type-ahead leg had no ARMED witness (the bytes could land raw before CHILD_MODE or after the re-arm and the anchor still matched) -- see the LS-CI row. **Prosecute on any change:** the positive control must keep driving the site to a NON-ZERO count with every sibling counter asserted unmoved; the report line must name every real-loss site. **ISIG-DISCARD ADDENDUM (F5, operator-voted 2026-08-17; PTY-DESIGN "ISIG characters DISCARD the pending line"):** the ISIG arm of `cons_rx_input` now zeroes `line_len` when ICANON is set (under `g_cons.lock`, in the same arm that sets intr-pending) -- POSIX NOFLSH-clear; a disposition, NOT a drop (no counter), and deliberately narrower than POSIX's full flush: the ring (Enter-committed lines) and the TX side are never flushed. Prosecute: the discard is gated on ISIG-CONSUMPTION (a `0x03` under `-isig` is data and must be buffered -- the test's control); it must not touch the ring or arm any drop counter; a mode write racing the ^C cannot deliver what the ^C discarded (same lock hold). Regression `cons.cook_isig_discards_pending_line` (A discard / B the `-isig` control / C a committed line survives). The AUTHORITATIVE copy is the ARCH section 25.4 row.**UNIT-ATOMICITY ADDENDUM (the byte-atomic tear; ARCH 23.5.2 "UNIT ATOMICITY", 2026-08-17; handed to aux by main after thyla-pi's `ttaappeessttrryydd`):** every TX producer now pushes a UNIT under ONE `g_cons_tx.lock` hold (`cons_tx_push_bulk`) and taps it under ONE `g_cons_drain.lock` hold (`cons_drain_tap_bulk`); the two locks are NEVER nested (tap, release, push, release). Units: a kernel diagnostic LINE (`struct cons_diag_line`, caller-stack, `CONS_DIAG_LINE_MAX` 256, ALL-OR-NOTHING, `dropped += len`; the per-token `cons_diag_puts/putdec/puthex64` trio is DELETED -- a per-CPU accumulator would splice a nested IRQ line into a process line, a caller-owned object cannot); the echo unit (`cons_rx_input`'s staged `echo[]`, all-or-nothing); the role writer's staged chunk (`cons_output_write`: <= `CONS_TX_STAGE` 512 cooked bytes, cut back to the last NL when the input continues, push-what-fits + the #67 room-wait for the rest, a short accept mapped back to input bytes by re-staging with cap = accepted). Closes #79 (echo exclusion for a ring-fitting write) as a corollary. **Prosecute on any change:** (a) the two leaf locks stay UN-NESTED (an emit that taps under the ring lock or pushes under the drain lock adds an ordering edge to two IRQ paths); (b) all-or-nothing must count the WHOLE unit under the same hold and push NOTHING (a partial IRQ unit is the tear reborn); (c) the writer's chunk boundary: the stage cut must fall AFTER a NL never inside a CR-NL pair, and the short-write mapping must never count an input NL whose CR went out but whose LF did not; (d) the pre-arm path stays the direct bounded `uart_putc` loop (boot output byte-identical; the tooling-ABI lines untouched); (e) the diag line stays capture-blind (`g_cons_echo_capture`) and the emits capture in unit order; (f) the tap fires FIRST and unconditionally (a serial-less board's only sink) -- a deadline short-write therefore leaves the renderer up to one chunk ahead of serial (was: one byte), a documented mirror divergence, not a defect; (g) `CONS_DIAG_LINE_MAX`/`CONS_TX_STAGE`/`CONS_ECHO_MAX` <= `CONS_TX_RING_SIZE` (an all-or-nothing unit larger than the ring is dropped forever) -- `_Static_assert`ed. Regressions: `cons.tx_unit_diag_line_atomic` (contiguous + all-or-nothing at the exact boundary; S1 per-byte -> the count moves), `cons.tx_unit_echo_atomic` (`\b \b` whole or not at all; S3 -> two bytes land), `cons.tx_unit_smp_no_tear` (two producers on two CPUs, the stalled ring read back as 64-byte frames via `cons_test_tx_ring_peek`, sequences per producer ascend; S1/S2 -> torn frames; SKIP-noted on one CPU; the overlap witness says whether the interleave was exercised), the pre-existing `cons.tx_role_serializes_writers` + `cons.tx_room_wait_and_deadline` + `proc.orphan_diag_uses_cons_path` unchanged. Residual, named: a write longer than one stage spans chunks and a FULL ring splits at a chunk boundary (Linux-equivalent; progress beats atomicity under congestion). **EXTINCTION RING-CLAIM ADDENDUM (2026-08-18; the full write-up is on the "Halls of Extinction crash dump" row -- read it before touching `cons_tx_*`. This entry is a POINTER, not LS-8 content: the console-claim family's authority is that row and it has no ARCH 25.4 mirror, per the 2026-08-16 precedent -- so the "AUTHORITATIVE copy is ARCH 25.4" sentence below governs the UNIT-ATOMICITY text, not this paragraph)**: the extinction winner now takes `g_cons_tx.lock` by a bounded RAW try-spin (`cons_tx_claim_for_dump` / `cons_tx_claim_core`) and HOLDS IT FOREVER, IRQ-masked, so the `EXTINCTION:` ABI line cannot be torn by a peer's ordinary console write. Consequences for THIS row's mechanisms: the ring lock's steady-state contract is unchanged (the claim only ever runs on a dying machine and never returns), but it is now a lock that can be held by a context that will never release -- so any NEW ring-lock acquirer must stay bounded-or-IRQ-safe and must never be reachable from the crash path; the claim is RAW because the counted variants deref `current_thread()`, which a crash may have destroyed (main#244: that deref made `recursive_kernel_fault` print NOTHING for a month); and `cons_tx_flush_for_dump` is GONE -- its one non-extinction caller, `exception.c::el1_sync_runaway`, now takes the claim plus the console word. Residual outside the ring's reach: direct-`uart_puts` kernel diagnostics (main#243). The AUTHORITATIVE copy is the ARCH section 25.4 row. |

Central obligations: serve/invalidate SMP atomicity (serve copies the t_stat out under the leaf lock; the lock is a pure leaf, never with `c->lock`/across an RPC); the **populate GEN guard** (captured pre-RPC, re-checked at install -- skips a populate that raced an invalidate, closing the populate-after-invalidate resurrection = the impl realization of the spec's atomic `Open`); the **create-reuse child-invalidate** (a reused-ino create carries a stale prior-occupant attr, and create never runs `walk_attrs` -- `dev9p_create` invalidates the child too; the `stalk-2` E2E failure, regression `dev9p.create_invalidates_reused_child`); the `qid.path`=0 root key (explicit `valid` bit); Bounded LRU (I-32); never-populate-from-readdir (L1a-2 audit-F1). **L1d dentry (the AUTHORITATIVE list is the ARCH §25.4 L1d ADDENDUM):** the dentry cache is **OWN-WRITE INVALIDATION ONLY, NOT cvers-gated** -- the L1d GROUND-TRUTH CORRECTION: Stratum surfaces no directory-content version that tracks a dirent change (verified `src/fs/fs.c` -- a child create/unlink does NOT `stm_inode_set` the parent, so the parent `si_cvers` does not bump; only rename stamps it), so a parent-cvers gate would falsely match a stale negative dentry after a create; coherence = drop the mutated (parent,name) binding on `dev9p_create`/`rename`[both endpoints]/`unlink` (the named binding only -- siblings preserved; O(1) via the serve hash; the whole-parent scan retired). Maps onto `fs_cache.tla`'s `Read`+`OwnWrite` subset (the attr discipline keyed by `(parent,name)`; no `Open` gate). Prosecute: the multi-hop serve atomicity (the dentry chain + each hop's attr lookup under ONE lock hold); the positive-serve attr coupling (a hop's reply attr comes from the attr cache -- an attr miss bails to the RPC); the query-only full-serve (a bind-form full walk MUST RPC to bind the fid; only the query form + any-form negative skip it); Territory-independence (raw dev9p-tree bindings; the resolver applies the mount overlay; a negative dentry can't be a mount point). **Spec-first RE-ENABLED (`specs/fs_cache.tla`, L1b)** is the pre-commit gate; **No new spec at L1c/L1d** (L1c's gen guard = prose realization of the modeled atomic Open; L1d's dentry cache is the modeled `Read`+`OwnWrite` subset -- the sketched parent-cvers gate REMOVED as unsound). Same-priv reads OVERLAY the run (below stage_off = server/cache, complete by the append-anchor discipline; within = staged, short to the run end; at/past the run end = fall through to the normal path, which answers EOF honestly -- never a synthesized 0); fstat on the staging fd patches size = max(server, staged end); other-priv/path readers are close-to-open-legal until the close flush. Attr+page invalidates move per-write -> per-FLUSH. ERROR semantics (the voted NFS model, ABI-bounded): a flush failure LATCHES on the priv (subsequent writes/fsync return it; fsync is the reliable channel; `Dev.close` is void at v1.0 so close-flush is best-effort -- documented seam, v1.x grows the slot; the cap flushes bound the silent tail <256 KiB). STRICT mounts keep write-through byte-identical; non-cacheable/byte-mode/weft/cached-open privs untouched; no wire/server change. Prosecute: the LOST-STAGE class (every path out of a priv flushes or deliberately discards -- close/fsync/cap/non-append/death [#926 closes handles -> the close flush]); the OVERLAY completeness (a same-priv read can never see pre-staged bytes where the run covers -- the below/within/past split's boundary arithmetic); the VISIBLE-RUN flush SMP discipline (a flusher count freezes the run under the priv lock, wire I/O OUTSIDE it -- blocking 9P never under a spinlock; the frozen run pins the buffer [no growth realloc under a live flusher]; a concurrent APPEND goes write-through [order-free: disjoint, past the frozen end]; a concurrent read overlays the still-visible run; flushes are SINGLE-FLIGHT -- a second flush-needing party [fsync/non-append/wstat on a dup-shared fd] yield-waits at the flush entry until the in-flight flush retires, the on_cpu-spin wait class [bounded by the flusher's independent progress incl. its #811 death-unwind; no Rendez -> no park/wake surface, no single-waiter hazard, no new I-9 leg]; duplicate concurrent flushes are FORBIDDEN, not idempotent -- a completed duplicate lets an ordering-dependent through-write land while the FIRST flusher's stale residual chunks still fly, silently overwriting an interior write that overlaps the frozen run [the SA-F1 race]); the flush-before-async-clunk ordering (a Tclunk racing ahead of the flush Twrites writes to a dead fid); the global-budget charge/uncharge balance on every path (grow, fallback, error, close, death); RT accounting: consuming ops (Tlcreate transitions the fid; Tlopen mode-binds) keep their 1 fused RT -- the 0-RT wins are the NON-consuming by-name ops + repeat parent resolutions. Tests `hwdebug.dfr0_enumerate` + `hwdebug.arm_disarm_roundtrip` + the in-guest `/hwbp-verify`. **8a-2b LANDED (the HW bp/step/wp tier; the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 addendum)**: per-Proc `struct debug_hw` (lazy `kzalloc`, freed at `proc_free` only, `__atomic` `bp_count`/`wp_count`, slots mutated stopped-only) + `hwdebug_switch_in` (`kernel/sched.c`, the per-CPU-MDE isolation -- a bp/wp fires ONLY while the debugged Proc runs) + the EC routes (0x30 bp / 0x32 step / 0x34 wp) in `arch/arm64/exception.c` (never-debugged -> `SNARE_ILL`; STALE benign -> disable+resume; the wp gated on `wp_count>0` not an exact FAR since arm64 FAR is imprecise-within-block) + single-step (the only real protocol growth -- `specs/debug_step.tla`, model-first; per-thread `MDSCR.SS` survives a mid-step migration; the step-over-breakpoint dance) + watchpoints (`hwdebug_wp_encode` -> `DBGWVR`/`DBGWCR`, BAS covering `[va,va+len)` in one doubleword; DISABLED during a step). Verbs `hwbreak`/`hwrmbreak`/`step`/`hwwatch`/`hwrmwatch` gated identically (kproc-refuse -> I-39 -> slot-owner -> stopped-only) + the lazy `debug_hw` pre-alloc discipline. W^X/I-36 preserved (bps are HW `DBGB*`, never a `BRK` in text). **8a-2c CLOSE + SA-1 (self-found; fixed)**: a hardware fire delivered the stop by calling `proc_debug_stop_deliver` directly from the EC handler (no lock, no owner check) while a detach cleared `debug_owner` + the table + `proc_debug_resume` under `g_proc_table_lock` -> a fire racing a detach could set `debug_stop_req` AFTER the detach's resume cleared it, parking the target with no debugger left (the strand; `debug_stop.tla` NoStrand). FIX: the 3 EC handlers now call `proc_debug_fault_stop(p)` (takes `g_proc_table_lock`, delivers ONLY while `p->debug_owner != NULL`; NULL owner -> benign STALE/run-free). Model gained `StopImpliesOwned == sflag => attached` + the `FaultStop` action + the `fault_stop_ungated` buggy cfg (TLC-green; clean 2264 unperturbed); regression `devproc.debug_stop_start_resume` SA-1 leg (revert-probed: ungated -> 1134/1135 FAIL). Prosecute (per the ARCH list): the SA-1 gate completeness (2 gated callers only) + the fix's lock-order (EC handler holds no lock -> GPTL outermost, deadlock-free); per-CPU-MDE isolation; `debug_hw` lifetime + no torn `(count, slots)` under detach-while-running; the ctx-switch install (SS survives migration; no stranded regs); `hwdebug_wp_encode` math; the verify/real coexistence; the new-verb I-39 gates + user-VA bounds. **8a-2c CLOSE: 0 P0 / 1 P1 / 0 P2 / 2 P3, NOT dirty** (Fable-5-max + self-audit converged): F1 [P1] a stale `debug_ss_armed` leaks a spurious single-step when a `step` is superseded by a peer's whole-Proc stop (the always-multi-threaded Go trap, below `debug_step.tla`'s single-thread abstraction) -> `proc_debug_stop_deliver` cancels pending steps for every thread (regression `devproc.debug_step_cancel_on_stop`); F2 [P3] the GLOBAL `hwverify` slot could swallow another Proc's real bp -> boot-only (`boot_is_complete()` gate); F3 [P3] the detached-step leg cleared only SS -> symmetric `hwdebug_disable_this_cpu`. AS-BUILT `docs/reference/134-debug-fs.md` (the 8a-2a/2b/2c sections); gates default 1136/1136 + `/debug-probe`(bp/step/wp) + `/hwbp-verify` on HVF + specs TLC-green + the SMP gate; closed list `memory/audit_8a2_closed_list.md`. **8b-1 ADDENDUM (settled-thread kstack inspect; DEBUG-FS §5b; user-voted 2026-07-16)**: `/proc/<pid>/kstack` (`devproc_kstack_walk_cb` drops `fully_stopped`; `devproc_format_kstack` gates on `on_cpu==false` -> `<running>`) relaxes to the SETTLED-thread inspect (Linux `/proc/stack` + Plan 9) -- I-39 auth + kproc-refuse ONLY, NO debug-stop, so a thread blocked DEEP in a syscall (`sleep -> rendez`) is inspectable. mem/regs/kregs/wait KEEP `fully_stopped`. Prosecute (8b-1d): memory-safe regardless of concurrent execution (bounded to the thread's own kstack via `halls_walk_kernel_frames` + the GPTL lifetime pin; a torn `th->ctx`/stack read is garbage-but-in-bounds, never UAF/OOB); best-effort-consistent (coherent for a sleeping thread -- the "why is it hung" diagnostic); NO new stop-machinery race (`debug_stop.tla` UNCHANGED + re-verified clean 2264 -- a gate condition, not an SM transition). NO new §28 invariant (I-39 REFINED, §5b.2). Kernel DWARF DEFERRED to 8c. Test `devproc.debug_kstack_settled` (revert-probed: restoring `fully_stopped` -> 1136/1137 FAIL). **8b-1d CLOSE (Fable holotype + self-audit): 0 P0 / 1 P1 / 0 P2 / 2 P3, NOT dirty.** F1 [P1, holotype -- the self-audit missed it]: the raw kstack `addr`+`link` DISCLOSE the KASLR slide (`koff`) to the UNPRIVILEGED OWNER axis (an I-16 secret `/ctl/kernel-base` gates behind CAP_HOSTOWNER; pre-existing in 8a via owner-attach-and-stop, widened by 8b to a self-read) -> FIXED: `devproc_format_kstack(bool raw)` emits raw columns ONLY to CAP_DEBUG/CAP_HOSTOWNER; the owner axis gets the KASLR-independent symbolic form (`#N name+soff`).
