F3 [P3] the EXITING-head empty-read test leg. 1137/1137 + boot OK + `/stack-probe` PASS (owner SYMBOLIC chain, no koff) + SMP gate. Closed list `memory/audit_8b_closed_list.md`. **8c-2 ADDENDUM (the stop-of-a-sleeper; DEBUG-FS §5c; user-voted)**: the multi-thread-Go-target fix -- a nested stop-detour INSIDE `sleep()`/`tsleep()` (`kernel/sched.c`; gated `r != &debug_rendez` + `t->proc`, die-check-FIRST so DEATH WINS) + `proc_debug_stop_sleeper_park` + the `proc_debug_stop_deliver` sleeper-wake walk (`torpor_wake_all_for_proc` + the per-peer `wait_lock`->`rendez_blocked_on`->`wakeup` cascade, #811-verbatim, arming the detour not the die). So this row's "stop parks ONLY at the EL0-return tail ... NEVER inside `sleep()`/`tsleep()`" line above is SUPERSEDED for a SYSCALL-BLOCKED sleeper (an idle futex-parked M never reaches the tail on its own, so without the detour a blocking `stop` hangs; the syscall is PRESERVED -- parks-and-reparks, not the #811 unwind). **#88 (fixed in THIS close)**: `devproc_build_regs` reads the head thread's `debug_trapframe`, which ONLY `el0_return_stop_check` set (from its vector ctx at the TAIL) -- a detour-parked thread never reached that tail while stopped, so its trapframe stayed NULL -> `/proc/regs` (`bt`/`print`) intermittently EPERM'd on a multi-M Go target whose head M was sleeping-in-a-syscall at stop-time (`goroutines`, a `/proc/mem` read, always worked -- the ground-truth signature). FIX: `arch/arm64/exception.c::exception_sync_lower_el_impl` records the EL0-entry frame (`current_thread()->debug_trapframe = ctx`) at the single EL0-sync choke point, so a syscall/fault-blocked detour-parked thread reports the correct 'ptrace at the syscall boundary' frame; read-gated on fully-stopped (a parked thread always has a FRESH frame -- tail-park overrides with its return ctx, detour is mid-syscall), no new field; verified 16/16 smp8. F3: the `proc_debug_stop_sleeper_park` corrupt-t guard -> `extinction`. `debug_stop.tla` gained the `sleep` PC + `StopWakesSleeper` + `BUGGY_STOP_SKIPS_SLEEPER` (TLC-green). Closed list `memory/audit_8c2_closed_list.md`. **8c-3 ADDENDUM (the #89 reader-role release; DEBUG-FS §5c.6; on the 3-round-audited #841 elected-reader machinery -- prosecute hard; the AUTHORITATIVE list is the ARCH §25.4 8c-3 addendum)**: the elected-9P-reader role (#841 `reader_active`) held across the detour-park froze system-FS (`/bin`,`/lib`) for survivor Procs sharing the SYSTEM Stratum client for the stop duration (per-user home clients isolated -- A-5b `--single-session`). FIX = the death-path role-release, THREE mechanisms: (1) `Thread.stop_unwinds` (a per-Thread bool in the `debug_ss_armed` padding -- no size change) makes the `sleep()`/`tsleep()` detour RETURN `SLEEP_INTR`/`TSLEEP_INTR` (reusing the death-interrupt propagation the transport recv already tolerates) instead of parking IN PLACE, so the reader's recv unwinds to `client_wait`, which clears `reader_active` + `client_handoff_reader_locked`s the role + parks role-free (`stop_unwinds` cleared) + re-elects on resume; every other sleep (clear) parks in place (8c-2). (2) a top-of-loop stop guard so a fresh-arriving debug-stopped thread parks promptly (never elects; re-hands-off if `be_reader`). (3) `client_handoff_reader_locked` skips a debug-stopped owner (`p9_rpc.owner->debug_stop_req != 0`) so the released role lands on a SURVIVOR, never bounces to a stopped sibling (whose `rpc->rendez` waiter has parked -> a lost wakeup); no survivor -> dropped -> future self-election. Prosecute: the I-9 no-lost-stop-wake (the recv detour reads `debug_stop_req` under `wait_lock`, register-then-observe = the 8c-2 park's I-9); the stream-desync (the unwind reuses the EXACT death path -> identical-to-death, NOT a #89 regression; unreachable anyway -- `srvconn_client_recv` sleeps only on an EMPTY ring, a whole frame fits the >=2x-msize ring, so the reader is interruptible only at a frame boundary [`got==0`, no bytes consumed]); DeathWinsOverStop at every branch; the `owner` lifetime (alive while inflight, as safe as `r->done`; only `client_run`+`submit_async` install non-NULL, both set owner; death path unchanged -- dying owners carry no `debug_stop_req`, F6 handles them); `stop_unwinds` scoping (cleared before ANY park, owner-only, never leaks past `client_wait`). NO new §28 invariant + NO spec change (the reader-role is below the `9p_client.tla` + `debug_stop.tla` models, both re-verified GREEN). Validated by the focused 8c-3 Fable holotype + `9p_client.handoff_skips_debug_stopped_owner` (revert-probed: pre-fix 1137/1138 FAIL) + the SMP gate; the full multi-Proc-survivor-during-stop deterministic E2E is the SAME owed cross-Proc SMP harness carried across #841/#845/#349/Loom. Closed list `memory/audit_8c3_closed_list.md`. **EXITKILL ADDENDUM (die-with-launcher; designed 2026-07-23, user-voted "kernel EXITKILL on gfx-4, full treatment"; the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution copy is the ARCH §25.4 EXITKILL addendum)**: the NoStrand-resume is correct for an ATTACHED target, WRONG for a debugger-LAUNCHED one -- a Plan 9 resume orphans a launched debuggee to init to run forever (the HVF-idle ~6.6% leak). A per-Proc `Proc.debug_exitkill` (a pad-slot bool near `debug_stop_req`) marks a launched target via the debugger's new owner-gated ctl `exitkill` verb (sent after attach+stop; `Attach` does NOT send it); `devproc_debug_release_cb` (the ctl-fd-close release on debugger DEATH via `devproc_close` + the #68 close-at-exit) `proc_group_terminate`s a marked ALIVE target -- the #811 cascade wakes the debug-parked threads to die at the die-check, safe under `g_proc_table_lock` per the `devproc_kill_walk_cb` precedent -- instead of `proc_debug_resume`, closing the leak for EVERY debugger-death mode (clean EOF, blocked-`Wait` timeout, the probe's `amb.kill()`). Only the IMPLICIT death-release honors the mark; explicit `detach` clears it + resumes (the debugger's deliberate choice). Ambush already issues the graceful `kill` (both `/ambush-probe` stages, STOCK Delve, traced end-to-end -- the "fix ambush" framing was overturned by ground truth); the death-path resume was the ONLY gap. NO new §28 invariant (refines I-39). `debug_stop.tla` model-first (`MarkExitkill` + the `ReleaseSlot` exitkill branch + `EventuallyLaunchedDies` liveness + the `exitkill_ignored` buggy cfg; clean GREEN 5633, buggy violates; the cfg count is now 6). Regression `devproc.debug_exitkill_terminates_on_close` (revert-probed). Ambush half: `pkg/proc/native/proc_thylacine.go::Launch` sends `ctlWrite("exitkill")` (best-effort). | Process creation: `execve` + shared address spaces + COW `fork` (the LINEAGE arc, L-1..L-7; I-44) | **Files** (vault #170 -- same defect as the JIT row: the mechanism was described in prose and no code file was named, so `kernel/cow.c` and the table entry "Process creation" never appeared on the same line): `kernel/addrspace.c` + `kernel/include/thylacine/addrspace.h` (the `struct AddrSpace` extraction), `kernel/cow.c` + `kernel/include/thylacine/cow.h` (the COW fault path), `kernel/exec.c` (`execve`), `kernel/proc.c` (`rfork_internal` + the thread-group teardown), `kernel/vma.c` + `kernel/burrow.c` (the mapping halves), `kernel/syscall.c` (the handlers), `kernel/test/test_addrspace.c` + `test_cow.c` + `test_fork.c` + `test_exec.c`. **L-1 through L-5 LANDED (stock `fork()` works); L-6 NEXT (the VIVARIUM clone/execve/wait4 rows); L-2b RESERVED** (`docs/LINEAGE.md`; user-voted 2026-08-01, the full arc through COW fork, so VIVARIUM's "an Alpine container runs a shell" target STANDS rather than being narrowed): NEW `struct AddrSpace` (refcounted; `Proc.as` replacing the inline `pgtable_root`/`context_id`/`vmas`/`vma_lock`/`page_count`/`vma_count`), `execve` (image replacement in a LIVE Proc), `rfork(RFPROC\|RFMEM)` + the VFORK suspend, the COW fault arm + a real per-page share count, `SYS_RFORK` + child-context restoration | **Invariant I-44; touches I-7/I-12/I-21/I-24/I-31/I-32/I-36 -- the address space itself; prosecute hard. The AUTHORITATIVE prosecution copy is the ARCH section 25.4 row, which joins when L-1 lands.** Three measured findings shape the arc and each REDUCES it: (a) **`execve` is the common core** -- a forked child execs and a vfork child execs, so this is not fork-vs-vfork; (b) **the AddrSpace object is the spine** -- both RFMEM and COW are blocked on the same extraction, so it is stage 0; (c) **text needs NO COW** -- REVENANT already shares text + R-only rodata read-only via the Image cache (I-36), so only WRITABLE ANON is copy-on-write, which is what keeps I-12 trivially true across the break. Prosecute: the `pgtable_root == 0` -> `as == NULL` conversion is COMPLETE as a set (13 measured sites; a missed one does not fail to build, it silently treats a live user AS as a kernel Proc); the AddrSpace refcount balances on every path (fork, exec, exit, rollback) with no UAF/leak/double-free; `execve` builds-new-then-switches (a FAILED exec must return with the caller intact) and completes the I-24 peer-thread cascade -- every peer OFF-CPU (#788) -- BEFORE the swap; the VFORK suspend is #811 death-interruptible in BOTH directions (a killed parent unwinds; a child dying without ever execing wakes the parent rather than stranding it); the COW break yields a private page equal to the shared page at fault time and leaves every other sharer unchanged, under break-vs-break and break-vs-teardown on `-smp 8`; the I-32 charge follows the AddrSpace (one charge for shared pages; the BREAKER charged at the break; a fork bomb still bounded at N address spaces); `context_id` moved to the AddrSpace leaves I-31 unaffected in substance (`asid.tla` re-run unchanged as the gate). **WATCH**: `struct page.refcount` LOOKS like the COW share count and is not -- it is the `T_E_PIPE` zero-emitter class from #100, a field whose name states a contract nothing keeps. **The measurement L-4 was told to take CORRECTED the two facts this row used to assert** (that it is "written ONLY by the buddy/magazine layers" and "never incremented above 1"): SLUB writes it at seven sites as a slab INUSE count reaching `objects_per_slab` (85 for a 48-byte cache), which the very line being quoted says -- `page.h:48` is `"BURROW refcount placeholder; slab: inuse count"` and the old text stopped at the semicolon. Also measured: it is written PER-BLOCK-HEAD (`buddy.c:209`), so every tail page of an order>0 block holds stale values. **And the counter was never the obstacle**: eager anon owns ONE indivisible buddy block, so there is no per-page thing for any count to index -- see the ARCH section 25.4 row's *L-4 AS-DESIGNED* clause (the authoritative copy) for the split into L-4a (exec's stack + writable data/bss -> `burrow_create_anon_lazy`, which also closes #130 and subsumes #49) and L-4b (the share count + break arm). **The WATCH above forbids REUSING `refcount`, and L-4b does not** -- it adds a NEW `struct page.cow_share` in the free `_pad` (user-voted 2026-08-02; `sizeof` stays 48), because a slot-indexed count is unimplementable once a break makes a Burrow's slot and its counted page diverge: take-in-place survives (the entry is the SUM over the groups sharing it) but the FREE decision corrupts. Neither live objection reaches the new field -- the allocator never touches it, so per-block-head staleness cannot arise, and it carries ONE meaning -- and the third ("promotion still would not give eager anon per-page ownership") is MOOT, since L-4a delivered exactly that. What it inherits instead is the WATCH's own hazard: `cow_share` must be ESTABLISHED-NEVER-INHERITED at each of the three sites that put a page into an anon slot, or it becomes the same zero-emitter class it was named to avoid. **L-2a (execve) LANDED** -- `SYS_EXECVE = 101`, build-detached-then-commit, single-threaded callers only. The AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 row's L-2a addendum. Headlines: the ORDERING is the safety argument (everything failable precedes the INFALLIBLE `proc_exec_replace`; a rollback there would be a design smell, not a missing feature); the arguments are copied out of the OLD address space first; `sched_activate_addrspace` runs BEFORE the old AS is torn down and its `isb` discharges `addrspace_unref`'s no-CPU-still-translating precondition -- and its failure is SILENT, not a fault (the old ASID's TLB stays warm at the same stack VA -> plausible-but-wrong argv through freed page tables, revert-probed); the swap + its single-thread re-check share ONE `g_proc_table_lock` section, which is also what makes dropping the old AS safe against the cross-Proc `->as` readers; the per-image resets (TPIDR_EL0, FP/SIMD) must touch the HARDWARE since execve erets without a context switch; and the multi-thread refusal must NOT be relaxed by suppressing `group_exit_msg` for the execer -- that flag is set-once-never-cleared (I-24), so suppressing it would permanently break a LATER kill. **NOT asserted, deliberately: that exec charges the I-32 PAGE axis -- measured, it does not** (that axis bounds the repeatable anon vectors; the exec image is one-shot). **L-3a (the share substrate) LANDED** -- the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 row's L-3a addendum. Headlines: the chunk's substance is a TEARDOWN MOVE -- the VMA drain goes from the callers into `addrspace_unref`'s LAST DROP, because draining at A PROC'S DEATH and draining at THE LAST REFERENCE coincide only while `ref` cannot exceed 1, and BOTH callers had the bug latent (`proc_free` would have had the first sharer to die unmap the survivor; `proc_exec_replace` would have drained the space out from under a suspended vfork parent, which is the ORDINARY posix_spawn case); `proc_free`'s drain-before-handle-close ordering is preserved deliberately (the #847 mapping_count argument) while the page-table destroy moves earlier, which nothing orders against; the device quiesce's VMA walk gates on SOLE ownership for the same reason as the drain; `RFMEM` without an address space is REFUSED, not downgraded, so an honoured flag and an ignored one are distinguishable; I-31 needs NOTHING because `asid_resolve` keys on `as->context_id` (an L-1 dividend -- `asid.tla` re-ran unperturbed). A SUCCESSFUL RFMEM rfork has no kernel-test witness (kproc has no address space by construction, and lending it one would make every `as == NULL` gate answer wrong) -- the end-to-end proof lands at L-3c, the L-2a split again. **THE L-3 GATE NEEDED FOUR MECHANISMS, NOT ONE** -- measuring musl's `clone.s` showed the raw `clone` RETURNS TWICE, so child-context restoration is common to the vfork and fork shapes and moves from L-5 to L-3b (LINEAGE section 5.4's correction); hence L-3a/b/c/d. **Spec-first RE-ENABLED for this surface** (re-enabling point (a), the `asid.tla`/`death_wake.tla` precedent): the model lands TLC-green BEFORE the L-4 impl, with buggy cfgs for break-vs-break, break-vs-teardown and the lost-VFORK-wake. **L-3b (child-context restoration + `SYS_RFORK = 102`) LANDED** -- the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 row's L-3b addendum. Headlines: the tree's FIRST syscall that RETURNS TWICE, so `fork_frame_init` must stay exactly two edits (`regs[0] = 0`, `sp = child_sp`) with everything else verbatim -- `elr` verbatim IS 'resumes at the same instruction', `spsr` verbatim carries live NZCV and is not EL0-forgeable, x1..x30 verbatim because the child CONTINUES the parent's C frame; the copy stays FIELD-BY-FIELD (a 288-byte struct assignment compiles to a `memcpy` the freestanding kernel does not link); `thread_fork_trampoline` stays IN `vectors.S` so it can branch to the deliberately-LOCAL `.Lexception_return` and reuse the one audited KERNEL_EXIT rather than hand-rolling a second eret; the ABSENCE of a GPR-zeroing sweep is deliberate (this child continues its parent's EL0 frame -- zeroing would break the fork, not harden it); FP/SIMD comes from the LIVE registers, not the caller's stale saved Context; the argument gate lives in the HANDLER ahead of `rfork_forked` so a kernel test can reach every rejection; `RFPROC` alone is REFUSED until COW exists; the child's handle table is fresh + EMPTY at L-3b (the copy is L-3c-1, #119 its I-5 hazard) -- **and the parenthetical this clause used to carry, "= posix_spawn's shape", was WRONG and is corrected at L-3c-1: Linux's `CLONE_VM` without `CLONE_FILES` gives a COPIED table, which is why posix_spawn's file_actions do not disturb the parent (LINEAGE section 2.4 measured it one section away). Empty and copied are different shapes.** **THE GATE THIS CHUNK WAS GIVEN WAS UNPROVABLE**: L-3a's table said a KERNEL-DRIVEN child resumes, and measuring showed a kernel test cannot observe an eret to EL0 at all (kproc has neither an address space nor a trapframe), so `SYS_RFORK` had to land here rather than at L-3c. Revert-probed to the arc's sharpest pairing: ignoring `child_sp` leaves the unit suite FULLY GREEN while only `/fork-probe` fails. **And a probe that fails to sabotage reports the same green as a correct system** -- verify the sabotage is PRESENT before building, never infer it from the script having run. **L-3c-1 (the handle-table copy) LANDED** -- the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 row's L-3c-1 addendum. Headlines: the copy is gated on the FORK SHAPE (`fc != NULL`) because the two primitives make opposite promises -- SYS_SPAWN_* endows an explicit `fd_list` (a capability hand-over), SYS_RFORK continues the parent (so the child must see what the parent sees) -- and every existing rfork call site passes a kernel `entry`, so the spawn family is untouched BY CONSTRUCTION, not by a flag; SLOT INDICES ARE PRESERVED and a skip leaves a HOLE (which is why this cannot be a loop over `handle_dup`, whose first-free install would renumber every fd after a skip); the admissibility test is the SAME `handle_slot_may_alias` `handle_dup` uses, EXTRACTED so the two cannot drift, and its two clauses are independently load-bearing (the KIND clause for I-5/Srv/Loom, the OBJECT clause for a devsrv Spoor `dc=='s'` -- the only case where the kind is admissible and the object is not); SKIP not REFUSE, because I-5 pins a handle to the Proc that CREATED it rather than forbidding forks, so the child simply does not hold what it was never eligible to hold -- refusing would leave a driver unable to create a process at all, and the resulting hole (EBADF there) is the honest report. **Three revert probes show the coverage layers are blind to each other in BOTH directions**: admitting every kind fails TWO unit assertions in TWO tests; removing rfork's CALL leaves the unit suite at a FULL 1285/1285 PASS while only the in-guest leg fails; compacting fails at the HOLE assertion. The kernel test reaches `handle_table_copy_into` directly, but nothing kernel-side can reach rfork's call to it (an RFMEM gate kproc cannot pass) -- the rule and the wiring need separate proofs. **L-3c-2 (the VFORK suspend) LANDED** -- the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 row's L-3c-2 addendum. Headlines: **the suspend is NOT requested by a flag** -- every `RF*` bit answers "what does the CHILD get?" under a stated polarity of `set == share`, so a bit for "suspend the PARENT" would be a category error; keying on `RFMEM` is not an arbitrary coupling because RFMEM is EXACTLY the precondition of the hazard (sharing the space is the only way the child reaches the parent's frame), and the fail-safe direction is one-sided (an unwanted suspend blocks visibly and terminates; an unwanted concurrency is corruption three layers from its cause). Linux puts `CLONE_VFORK` beside `CLONE_VM` only because its clone word has no polarity to protect; Plan 9 has no vfork at all. The gate is `fc && (flags & RFMEM)`, which keeps L-5's `RFPROC`-alone fork correct WITHOUT a future edit. **The release condition is not a RECORD of the release, it IS the release** -- `child->as` is already the fact "the child no longer maps my space", so a flag set at fork and cleared at exec/exit would be strictly worse (a third release path added later would silently strand every vfork parent); the pointer comparison is sound only because the parent still holds a reference, so the outgoing AddrSpace cannot be freed and its address cannot be recycled -- a direct dividend of L-3a's teardown move. Parking on `child_waiters` makes the DEATH release free; only the EXEC release needed a new wake. **Two revert probes, three blind layers**: removing the park leaves the unit suite at a FULL 1286/1286 PASS while only leg I fails; removing the exec wake leaves it green AND HANGS THE BOOT -- and that hang is the bug's real production symptom, not a probe artifact, since a `posix_spawn` parent whose child execs would park forever the same way. **L-4b (the per-page COW share count + the clone + the break arm) LANDED** -- the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 row's L-4b AS-DESIGNED + L-4b-2 AS-BUILT clauses. Headlines: **BUILDING IT OVERTURNED THE SCRIPTURE** (section 5.4's per-slot array is not implementable -- after a break the slot and its counted page diverge, so the FREE decision corrupts; take-in-place survives, which is why the first derivation of the mechanism was wrong in a way that still condemned the design); a fork CLONES the Burrow per address space (Plan 9 `dupseg`) and the decide runs under a GLOBAL leaf lock, because two sharers hold DIFFERENT Burrow locks. **`mmu_install_user_pte` REFUSES a mismatching install over a valid leaf** (`mmu.c:1649`), and both break outcomes mismatch -- so the break must uninstall first or the first COW write after a read kills the Proc (found by READING the MMU contract, revert-probed 1297/1298). The fork's write-protect is an UNINSTALL, and its pass runs ON SUCCESS ONLY. The I-32 charge is taken at the FORK (over-counting in the safe direction) so the break cannot fail on the cap. **A SABOTAGE THAT PASSES IS THE FINDING**: the refuse-leaves-parent-intact test asserted 'a failed fork flags nothing' while holding only the eager VMA that caused the refusal -- not a COW candidate, so the flag pass skipped it either way and the probe could not fire; the test now needs a MIX of a lazy and an eager VMA to observe the property it names. Also: **two page-accounting instruments were wrong before one was right** (`PG_FREE` passes VACUOUSLY -- buddy coalesce anchors on the lower-pfn buddy; `phys_free_pages()` is BLIND to order-0 frees -- the magazine intercepts them; `magazines_drain_all()` first). Prose in `docs/LINEAGE.md` + ARCH section 28 I-44 + the L-7 focused audit + the SMP gate + **the L-6 arc gate (an Alpine `/bin/sh` runs a command)** -- and the VIVARIUM section 9 ladder entry moves OUT->IN at THAT gate, never at an earlier chunk's landing. **L-7 CLOSED @`b647a6c4` -- the arc-close holotype, 0 P0 / 0 P1 / 1 P2 / 6 P3, NOT dirty.** Six fixed (the stale `cow.tla` spec map + its unrecorded global-leaf-lock refinement; `execve` leaving hardware breakpoints armed at the OLD image; a NULL `q->as` deref in the weft reaper; the exec I-32 charge leak; a header asserting a lock the code never takes; a stability comment naming a caller state #68 retired), one tracked (#164). **Two of the six are a premise a LATER commit voided -- invisible to a chunk-scoped round, which is the argument for the arc round.** F2 and F4 have NO test and the green suite does not cover them (#165). The AUTHORITATIVE prosecution copy, with the per-finding "prosecute on any change" list, is the ARCH section 25.4 row; the do-not-re-report set is `memory/audit_l7_closed_list.md`. |
| VIVARIUM: the syscall-entry phenotype branch + the spawn-time declaration (V-1b; I-43) | `kernel/syscall.c` (`syscall_dispatch`'s phenotype branch -> `viv_linux_dispatch`; the TIER-2 shells `viv_tier2` / `viv_measure_user_path` / `viv_stat_copy_out`; the extracted `sys_fstat_for_proc`; `SPAWN_PHENO_LINUX` in `sys_spawn_args.pheno_flags` -> the thunk stamp), `kernel/include/thylacine/syscall.h` (the ABI: `pheno_flags` consumed the must-be-0 `_pad_allow` slot at offset 92), `kernel/vivarium.{c,h}` (the PURE table + translators, landed at V-2), `kernel/exec.c` (the section-12.1 rule-4 advisory brand diagnostic -- `elf_brand_hint`'s first caller), the mirrors (libt / libthyla-rs `T_SPAWN_PHENO_LINUX`), `usr/viv` (the manifest's `org.thylacine.phenotype` annotation -> the entrypoint spawn) | **Invariant I-43 -- the privilege boundary of the whole Vivarium arc; prosecute hard at V-8.** A mis-branded Proc decodes every syscall number wrong, so the fail-safe direction is forced: outside a vivarium the phenotype is ALWAYS native (section 12.1 rule 3), no ELF byte may decide (rule 4 -- the hint is consulted only on an already-FAILED load, where it can explain an outcome but never change one), and the declaration rides ONLY `SYS_SPAWN_FULL_ARGV`. Prosecute: **I-43 completeness** -- NO translated path may bypass a gate (the T1 rows must keep FALLING THROUGH to the native `switch` rather than growing a parallel handler; each T2 shell must keep calling the same `sys_*_for_proc` core, which is why `sys_fstat_for_proc` was extracted rather than duplicated); the ungated declaration's soundness (every Linux number in the table ALSO exists as a live native number -- 56 openat vs SYS_READDIR, 64 write vs SYS_CONSOLE_OPEN, 94 exit_group vs SYS_TTY_SIGNAL -- so a mis-declaration mis-decodes the mis-declarer's OWN calls behind its OWN gates and reaches nothing new; a NEW table row must be re-checked against this argument); the **argument-arity** property (the branch copies all six argument registers verbatim, so a T1 row is sound only while its native target reads NO MORE arguments than the Linux call supplies -- true of all five current rows [read/write/close/lseek/exit_group], and a property a new row could break silently, since the extra register would carry whatever the Linux caller left there); the T2 uaccess shells (`viv_measure_user_path` is bounded by `SYS_OPEN_PATH_MAX` and validates every byte's VA before loading it, since the length is unknown up front; the `newfstatat` re-read rejects an embedded NUL exactly as `sys_stat_handler` does, so a peer thread rewriting the buffer between measure and copy cannot produce a malformed kernel path); the DECIDE-BEFORE-MEASURE order (a forwarded call must not take a user fault in the kernel fast path); the 128-byte `viv_linux_stat` copy-out is I-13-clean (zeroed wholesale by `vivarium_stat_to_linux` before filling); the phenotype is set ONCE in the thunk before EL0 and never by a running Proc (no runtime self-modification of the ABI); the FORWARD arm stays a distinct case from ENOSYS so V-3's supervisor is a one-line change. **No new spec** -- prose in `docs/VIVARIUM.md` section 8/12.1 + `docs/reference/145-vivarium.md` + this row + the V-8 focused audit + the `vivarium.*` + `sys_spawn_full_argv.validate_req_pheno_flags` tests + the boot-fatal two-vantage gate + the SMP gate. **V-2d ADDENDUM (the memory rows; `mmap`/`munmap` T2, `mprotect` ENOSYS).** V-3 is DEFERRED (VIVARIUM.md section 4.1: no Proc can mutate another's address space, handle table or process tree, so a peer-supervisor's servable set is EMPTY -- the peers work only because a compat supervisor either IS the guest [Starnix restricted mode] or OWNS it [Plan 9 linuxemu over /proc; gVisor]), so FORWARD now means ENOSYS and a too-narrow T2 domain is a COMPATIBILITY FAILURE rather than a slow path -- the inverse of section 6.19's 'declining is always safe'. Prosecute: the mmap admission is an ALLOW-LIST of PROT_READ\|PROT_WRITE, never a PROT_EXEC deny-list (aarch64 musl also defines PROT_BTI/PROT_MTE and generic musl PROT_GROWSDOWN/GROWSUP -- a deny-list admits all four silently); PROT_EXEC stays REFUSED not degraded (I-12 W^X + I-42/CAP_JIT -- a phenotype must never mint an executable anonymous mapping); the flags word is an EXACT match (MAP_FIXED/FIXED_NOREPLACE make `addr` a REQUIREMENT and so must never be admitted while the target picks the address); the PROT_NONE-yields-RW degradation is FIDELITY not AUTHORITY (the guest's own pages, no gate changed, nothing crosses a Proc boundary) and must stay published in section 9's DEGRADED tier + pinned by prover leg L23; the shell must keep translating the target's bare -1 into -ENOMEM (a Linux libc reads -1 as -EPERM) and keep answering EINVAL for len==0 / a misaligned munmap addr, so Linux's own argument errors are not collapsed into the decline; munmap must keep DECLINING everything outside the exact match rather than faking Linux's unmapping-nothing-succeeds (telling that apart from a PARTIAL overlap needs a range scan the VMA API does not expose -- vma_lookup is a point probe blind to a VMA strictly inside the range -- and claiming success on a partial overlap would leave a mapping the guest believes is gone). Coverage: `vivarium.mmap_domain` + the mmap/munmap-are-T2 and mprotect-ENOSYS rows in `rejects_are_deliberate` + prover legs L16-L23 (map/write/read-back/unmap, PROT_EXEC and MAP_FIXED refused in-guest, mprotect ENOSYS which musl DEPENDS on being ENOSYS specifically). Revert-probed three ways: reverting the table row or admitting PROT_EXEC fails exactly two unit tests; breaking only the SHELL leaves the unit suite at a full 1239/1239 while the in-guest leg fails at L16 -- the pure tests prove the DECISION, the guest legs prove the PLUMBING, and neither sees the other's bugs. RE-CHECK PERFORMED (the obligation this row states): 215/222/226 collide with NO native number -- **CORRECTED 2026-07-31 (#97): the highest assigned native SYSCALL number is 100 (`SYS_WEFT_UNSHARE`), not "256, sparsely"** (256 is `SYS_ATTACH_ANAME_MAX`, a bound; 4096 is `SYS_RW_STACK`, a buffer size -- a naive `SYS_*=<n>` grep reads both as syscall numbers). Everything above 100 is free by construction, so the mandated re-check is a one-line argument. The collision-table clause does not apply and the argument holds by a different one -- a mis-declared Proc issuing 222 receives an anonymous mapping where it would have received -1, which is NOT new authority because both targets (SYS_BURROW_ATTACH_LAZY 83, SYS_BURROW_DETACH 38) are UNGATED syscalls acting only on the caller's OWN address space and the Proc could call either directly; mprotect dispatches nothing. Arity: the mmap shell reads exactly args[0..5] (six supplied), the munmap shell args[0..1] (two supplied). **V-5 ADDENDUM (sockets as a T2 family; VIVARIUM.md section 5.5, user-voted 2026-07-31): the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 row's V-5 addendum.** Headlines: sockets translate to `/net` IN THE KERNEL and build NO supervisor (section 4.1.1 measured that a supervisor is unnecessary -- every step is work the caller could do for itself, so V-3's fork moves to #93); **`handle_replace`** swaps a live fd in place at `connect` (ctl -> data) and lands on the HANDLE TABLE audit surface -- it must not launder rights (derive from the new object's omode, never inherit the outgoing slot's), must refuse every hardware kind (I-5: replacing a hardware slot is a transfer by another name), must release the outgoing object exactly once under the #844 discipline, and must have NO EL0 caller; **`Proc.socktab`** holds `(proto, N, state)` in the `sigtab` shape, bounded (I-32), with a phenotype-gated `close` hook whose absence is the chunk's sharpest bug (fd reuse serving a stale `(proto, N)` writes a connect verb to a stranger's connection); I-43 holds because every `/net` op resolves through `stalk` in the CALLER's Territory, and **no path may decode netd's qid layout** (`/net` is a mount point and need not be netd -- decoding a foreign server's qid is the silent mistranslation the argument-domain rule forbids, which is why `proto` is remembered, not recovered); the connect swap opens `data` BEFORE releasing `ctl` (the clone-minted ctl fid holds the connection's only ref, `slot_ref` 0->1) and cannot open it earlier (netd DEFERS the `Rlopen` to ESTABLISHED, #257) -- both measured properties of the server, to be re-measured if netd's lifetime model changes. Sockets add no T1 rows, so the arity clause is not engaged. **V-8 (the arc CLOSE) LANDED -- the AUTHORITATIVE close record is the ARCH section 25.4 row.** Headlines: the round scoped itself by MEASURING what the two prior rounds left (V-1b + V-2d + V-6a/b/c + V-7 -- V-6 is audit-bearing and had never had a round) and returned 0 P0 / 1 P1 / 2 P2 / 4 P3, NOT dirty, all closed. F1 [P1] every T1 byte-I/O target returned a flat `-1` = EPERM to stock musl (#100) -- and underneath it, `T_E_PIPE` had been ABI-pinned since the errno scripture with **zero emitters tree-wide**, so a write to a closed pipe reported EIO everywhere, not just under a phenotype. F2 [P2] a phenotyped handler lives in the sigtab, so the terminate-latch exemption missed it and a frame-push failure spun the guest forever. F3 [P2] the fixed diorama name failed OPEN under concurrency; fixed SERVER-side in `h_attach` (kernel byte-unchanged), because the runner cannot learn its own peer identity at that moment. F4-F7 [P3] (#102). Residual, published in VIVARIUM.md section 9's DEGRADED tier: `lseek` on a non-seekable Dev still says EPERM where POSIX says ESPIPE (`T_E_SPIPE` unregistered; signoff-bearing, task #106). **The durable testing lesson: the coverage layers are blind to each other in BOTH directions** -- reverting F6's fix leaves the unit suite at a full 1272/1272 PASS while only the in-guest leg fails, and reverting F3's leaves selftest AND unit suite green while only the joey leg fails; a behaviourally-inert fix needs a test that MANUFACTURES the state, and a leg satisfiable by over-fixing needs its pair. **#150 ADDENDUM (the startup batch; LINEAGE L-6c) -- the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 row.** The measured set busybox issues between `_start` and its first useful instruction: `getpid` T1; `writev`/`getcwd`/`getppid`/`getuid`/`getgid`/`uname`/`set_tid_address`/`setuid`/`setgid` T2; `fcntl` ENOSYS. Unserved 13 -> 3. Headlines: `writev` is the ARITY RULE's sharpest case (three registers that line up with SYS_WRITE's and mean something else entirely -- a renumber would write the guest's own iovec ARRAY to the fd), and `getuid`/`getgid` are the counterexample from the other side (exact native twins, matching arity, still T2, because the PRINCIPAL_SYSTEM -> 0 mapping has nowhere to live in a renumber); the writev shell validates in TWO passes for Linux's error semantics, keeps O(1) storage (1024 iovecs x 16 B = the whole kernel stack), and lets `iovcnt == 0` reach the fd check; the uid mapping confers NOTHING (every gate reads the real principal -- I-22) and cannot collide (PRINCIPAL_INVALID == 0); `uname` claims release 4.4.0 as the newest kernel promising nothing we lack, with the truth in the never-parsed `version` field, and its 390-byte copy-out is I-13-zeroed. `fcntl` is ENOSYS BY MEASUREMENT (busybox issues only F_SETFD(FD_CLOEXEC) and F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC; Thylacine has no close-on-exec, and exec preserves every fd) -> **task #151**, which also VOIDED VIVARIUM.md's justification for ignoring `O_CLOEXEC`: it rested on spawn's explicit fd list, and L-2a + L-3c-1 made fork+exec preserve the table. **A round is scoped to one commit, so a premise a LATER commit voids is invisible to it.** Coverage: 4 tests revert-probed FOUR ways in one build, each failing at its OWN assertion (1306/1310, no cross-talk). **#155 ADDENDUM (the `pipe2` row) -- the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 row.** A shell cannot build a pipeline without it, and aarch64 has no legacy `pipe` number, so musl's `pipe()` IS nr 59 with flags 0. The domain `{0, O_CLOEXEC}` was MEASURED off the gate's own busybox (six call sites: four `mov x1, #0`, two `mov w1, #0x80000`), not reasoned from what Linux permits; both are reproducible exactly (0 = SYS_PIPE; O_CLOEXEC = the #151 descriptor flag, on BOTH ends). An ALLOW-LIST per the V-2d mmap lesson -- `O_NONBLOCK` and `O_DIRECT` are flags Linux really accepts and devpipe has no counterpart to either. Prosecute on any change: the allow-list stays an allow-list; decide + range-check precede the allocation; **the copy-out cleanup must survive** (the range check proves only the VA band, so the store can still fault, and an error returned with two live descriptors the guest never saw is an unreachable leak). The below-the-ceiling collision (native 59 = `SYS_WSTAT`) is refused twice over -- every legal `valid` mask is outside the domain, and a garbage 0 would use an fd index as the write VA, i.e. page zero. Coverage: `vivarium.pipe2_domain` + prover legs L187-L192 (incl. the leak assertion: 200 EFAULT calls burn 400 descriptors against a 256-slot table). Revert probes blind in OPPOSITE directions -- no cleanup leaves the unit suite at a full 1319/1319 and fails only in-guest at L192; a deny-list fails the unit test at 1318/1319 on the bare `1u` case. **The next blocker was measured BEFORE this row was written**, so fixing site N could not hide site N+1: `dup3` (nr 24) is a deliberate FORWARD because it FREES the fd it overwrites and the socktab close hook must be extended in the same commit that serves it -- task **#157**. pipe2 is fd-CREATING (openat's class) and needs no such extension. **#157 ADDENDUM (the `dup3` row) -- THE L-6c ARC GATE PASSES; the AUTHORITATIVE prosecution list is the ARCH section 25.4 row.** Same shape as pipe2 and reached the same way: aarch64 has no `dup2` number, so musl compiles dup2 into dup3 and a shell's redirection has no other route; the domain `{0, O_CLOEXEC}` was MEASURED off four call sites in the gate's own busybox. **This row's domain is COMPLETE where pipe2's was a subset** -- Linux's own `ksys_dup3` refuses everything else with EINVAL -- so an out-of-domain flag gets **EINVAL, not the ENOSYS decline** (V-2d's munmap distinction: Linux's own argument errors are reproduced rather than collapsed). Needs a NEW primitive, `handle_dup_to`: an install at a CALLER-NAMED index that may be free OR live, which none of its three neighbours does, and NOT close-then-dup (the freed index is not reserved -- `handle_replace`'s own argument for `connect`). Rights VERBATIM; cloexec SET FROM THE FLAG, never inherited from the source (POSIX clears it) nor from the previous occupant (the reused-identity failure); the SOURCE through `handle_slot_may_alias`, the DESTINATION ungated by kind because it is merely being CLOSED and `handle_close` places no kind restriction on closing. **The socket case DECLINES** -- an unrefcounted fd-keyed socktab cannot give two descriptors one socket's state, and both alternatives are wrong IN WRITING (copy -> two diverging state machines over one connection; omit -> an fd that reads but cannot connect). **The fd-freeing drop is paid INSIDE THE SHELL, not the entry hook** (a dup3 can be refused while `new` is a live socket), and AFTER the install rather than before (the number is never free, so `viv_sock_accept`'s opposite ordering does not transfer; dropping first would let a refused call destroy live state). #158 rode along because the dup3 socktab work rests on it: the lock-free premise said 'clone is not a table row', which L-3d falsified WITHOUT ANYTHING FAILING -- seven sites, two of which claimed a guest 'cannot fork', which the passing gate disproves every boot. Revert-probed both ways: no drop -> **1321/1321 green** and boot-fatal only in-guest at `marker=L199`; deny-list -> **1320/1321** on the bare `1u`, which the guest legs cannot see. `L6C_GATE_FATAL` flips to 1 -- a gate that cannot redden is a disabled test, and six landed fixes now have nothing else watching them. Enqueued from the PASSING run rather than noted in prose: **#159** (`viv_report_unserved` emits seven unbracketed `uart_puts` and a live log shows two reporters interleaved mid-word -- the #75/#76/#152 writer-set family one site further) and **#160** (`ioctl` 29 + `sendfile` 71, the next unserved set, neither blocking). **fcntl-errno addendum (the c8ab2744 close, 2026-08-17; found by a POSITIVE CONTROL):** the `VIV_FCNTL_DUPFD` arm answered `EMFILE` for BOTH of `handle_dup_posix`'s folded failures ("no such fd" and "table full"), on the argument that a guest which just used the fd knows it exists. busybox ash's `redirect()` probes the TARGET fd of every `N>&M` with `fcntl(N, F_DUPFD, 10)` precisely to learn whether N is open -- `EBADF` = not open, nothing to save; ANY other errno = "strange", `ash_msg_and_raise_perror`, the whole command aborted (`fcntl(3,F_DUPFD,10): No file descriptors available`, once per `3>&1`). The L-6c SIGPIPE legs' `err=$( { ... 2>&3 ...; } 3>&1 )` fixture therefore never ran; the two legs asserting an EMPTY capture passed VACUOUSLY and only the positive control (`L6C-K`) reddened -- aux#215's class, caught by its own remedy. Fixed: after a failed dup the arm re-checks liveness (`handle_get_cloexec`, the GETFD arm's own lookup): closed fd -> `EBADF`; the residual (table full, a non-dup-able kind, a rights failure) -> `EMFILE`; `min_fd >= PROC_HANDLE_MAX` stays `EINVAL`. POSIX says exactly that. **Prosecute on any change:** (1) both errnos are LOAD-BEARING to a shell and must stay distinct -- a fix that always answered `EBADF` would strand a table-full guest with a bogus fd complaint, and one that always answered `EMFILE` re-breaks every redirection to a not-yet-open fd; (2) the liveness re-check is a second lookup, so a peer closing the fd between the dup and the check misreports one errno -- unreachable for a single-threaded phenotype Proc (CLONE_THREAD is outside the admitted domain), and harmless if it were not; do not "fix" it with a lock; (3) `viv_fcntl_for_test` hands the arm a NULL frame and is safe ONLY because the FCNTL case never reads ctx -- it must not become a general T2 driver. Regression `vivarium.fcntl_dupfd_errnos`: control (a live fd dups at >= 10), closed fd -> `EBADF` for both spellings, a live fd with a FULL table (proved full two ways) -> `EMFILE`, `min_fd` at the table size -> `EINVAL`. **INSTALL-TIME SIG_IGN DISCARD (2026-08-17):** the `VIV_LINUX_RT_SIGACTION` shell calls `notes_discard_name` after the store when the new disposition ignores (SIG_IGN, or SIG_DFL + default-ignore; the no-table SIG_DFL shortcut skips the store only) -- the prosecution list is on the Notes / signals row (INSTALL-TIME DISCARD ADDENDUM). Kernel-side: `viv_signote_note_name` (the inverse decode; NULL for NONE). |
| File-backed EL0 mmap: the phenotype FILE arm (DISTRO D-3; I-36 GENERALIZES, I-12 + I-32 on the line) | **D-3a + D-3b + D-3c AS-BUILT.** **D-3c adds**: `kernel/image.c` + `image.h` + `kernel/exec.c` + `kernel/syscall.c` (`file_limit` on every Image Burrow -- `spoor_file_size` sampled at create; guest-facing arms REFUSE unknown/hostile-band sizes [-EIO], exec admits UNKNOWN for the immutable baked ramfs only), `arch/arm64/fault.c` (the #194 SIGBUS-past-EOF refusal at `file_demand_page_slow` BEFORE any allocation + the read-ahead cluster clamp [a past-limit slot pre-filled resident would serve the fast path unchecked]), `kernel/syscall.c` (#193 both success-path `burrow_unref`s moved OUTSIDE `as->lock`; #199 `detach_one_locked` factored from the exact detach [returns LOCKED -- the S-boot preempt-underflow taught the pair rule] + NEW `sys_munmap_range_for_proc` [whole-VMAs-only range detach, atomic straddle/CODE refusal, empty-range = Linux no-op; the NATIVE exact ABI unchanged] + the `VIV_LINUX_MUNMAP` row repointed), `kernel/vma.c` + `vma.h` (NEW `vma_next_overlap_in` -- the range scan), `tools/build.sh` + `usr/joey/joey.c` (the #189 gate: `D3-A-getconf-pagesize-4096` in the L-6c leg list, boot-fatal; its first run surfaced #201 [shell write-redirects refused on EXISTING files -- O_CREAT unconditional refusal]). #192 VERDICT: document-not-enforce (Linux semantic; Debian 0644 libs; ARCH 6.6 wording corrected from "only the JIT path") -- **PARTLY OVERTURNED AT D-CLOSE by the ARC ROUND (Fable 5, F1 [P1]); read this before touching the R+X arm.** The Debian-0644 half STANDS and is why the fix is NOT an X-bit check. What fell is the authority argument: "the caller could already run them (exec-from-namespace)" holds only for a file the caller has X on -- `exec_resolve_from_namespace` walks `STALK_OPEN`/OEXEC and REFUSES a non-executable file, whereas `sys_mmap_file_for_proc` looks the fd up with `RIGHT_READ` alone and contains **ZERO `perm_check` calls** (measured across `syscall.c:4854-4960`) before installing `VMA_PROT_READ\|VMA_PROT_EXEC`. **R+X mmap therefore runs bytes exec would decline** -- authority exec does not grant, which is what I-42 exists to gate. And the coupling that made "document" feel safe -- recorded in `memory/audit_d3_closed_list.md` F4 as "#201: phenotype cannot create/WRITE files" -- was FALSE: `VIV_LINUX_WRITE -> SYS_WRITE` is a TIER-1 DIRECT ROW (`vivarium.c:149`) and `openat O_RDWR -> VIV_OMODE_RDWR` is translated (`vivarium.c:532`), both landed before this arc. #201 blocks CREATION only, and a container does not need to create a file it can write. So `openat(F,O_RDWR)` + `write(shellcode)` + `mmap(F, R\|X)` + jump is native execution with NO `CAP_JIT`; the sole thing blocking it is that OUR rootfs is baked SYSTEM-owned against a non-SYSTEM invoker, since `root.readonly: true` is PARSED AND DISCARDED (`usr/viv/src/main.rs:210-213`) -- one bundle's DAC, not a kernel invariant, and it does not survive a user-supplied bundle. **DISPOSITION (user-voted 2026-08-10): correct the record now, enforce via per-mount `noexec` later (task #217)** -- what Linux itself gates `PROT_EXEC` on (`path_noexec`), and the only shape that survives 0644 libraries. **UNTIL #217 LANDS THE HOLE IS OPEN for any bundle we do not bake ourselves.** **The old tripwire is RETIRED because it could never fire** -- "the instant D-4/D-5 grants file writes, revisit #192" waited on writes granted years earlier; **the replacement: #192 re-opens at #217, or at any change admitting a new `PROT_EXEC` file-mapping shape, whichever comes first.** Full analysis: `docs/DISTRO.md` section 6. Sabotage record: S1 entry-check / S2 cluster-clamp / S3 straddle-refusal / S4 dispatch -- four distinct signatures, S4 with the unit suite fully blind (1383/1383 PASS through it) and the gate reddening on exactly its own leg. **D-3c FOCUSED round (Fable 5, 0 P0 / 1 P1 / 0 P2 / 3 P3): F1 [P1] FIXED** -- detach/munmap/vma_drain freed a bypass FILE Burrow UNDER as->lock, and the FILE arm's spoor_clunk may sleep (a 9P Tclunk) -> the lock-across-sleep extinction (the teardown twin of #193). Regression: `burrow.{detach_file,munmap_range_file}_frees_outside_lock` (stub Dev `.close` records `current_thread()->preempt_count`, asserts 0). That close was DIRTY (a P1 returned + an invasive teardown restructure), so a re-audit was owed on the fix -- **it ran TWICE and the chain is now CLOSED**. F2/F3/F4 P3s: `file_limit` cache-key coverage (defense-in-depth), the split-vs-fault comment (corrected), the #192-verdict/#201 D-4 tripwire (recorded). **RE-AUDIT 1 (Opus, deliberately non-Fable -- the F1 code was Fable-authored; `c9046b91`) found F1 SOUND at its three sites but INCOMPLETE: F5 [P1] FIXED** -- it missed the FOURTH inline-free-under-`as->lock` site, `vma_replace_range_in`'s exact-cover arm, reached via MAP_FIXED (`sys_mmap_fixed_{file,anon}_for_proc` -> `burrow_map_fixed`): a MAP_FIXED that EXACTLY covers a bypass FILE mapping freed its Burrow INLINE -> the same `spoor_clunk` lock-across-sleep extinction, guest-constructible (fill the 128-entry image cache with live mappings, mmap a 129th 9P file for an un-cached `{h:0,m:1}` bypass Burrow, then MAP_FIXED-cover that address). **This is the "a fix on site N stops you asking about site N+1" trap, and it is this row's standing lesson: the deferred-free census is FOUR sites and a fifth must be hunted, never assumed absent.** Fix = thread `struct Burrow **out_free` through `vma_replace_range_in` / `burrow_map_fixed{,_in}`; the exact-cover `vma_free(old)` becomes `vma_free_deferred` handed back to the two MAP_FIXED arms, which free it past the unlock (the F1 pattern). `out_free` stays MANDATORY on all three F5-plumbed functions, because tolerating NULL is a LEAK rather than a fallback; the CODE refusal stays wherever a range op can remove or split a VMA; and #205 (the exact-cover I-32 uncharge gap) is re-audit-verified benign in the OVER-charge direction ONLY -- any change that inverts that direction turns it into a real I-32 bound violation. `kernel/include/thylacine/vivarium.h` (`VIV_MMAP_FILE_FLAGS_ADMITTED` = MAP_PRIVATE ALONE + `VIV_MMAP_FILE_PROT_ADMITTED` = R\|X -- an ALLOW-LIST, so PROT_WRITE/BTI/MTE/GROWSDOWN and MAP_SHARED/MAP_FIXED/MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE/MAP_ANONYMOUS all decline WITHOUT being enumerated; `vivarium_mmap_file_decide` + `vivarium_mmap_arms_disjoint`), `kernel/vivarium.c` (the second decider -- a SEPARATE function from `vivarium_mmap_decide` so the anon arm's 18 domain tests still exercise byte-identical code; READ is exactly the authority `pread(2)` already grants, so the mapping confers nothing new], the plain-file gate `qid.type & (QTDIR\|QTSYMLINK\|QTAPPEND)` [explicit, per the #184 lesson that 'a symlink fid is not byte-I/O-able' was FALSE against Stratum's `h_lopen`], the `CWALKONLY` O_PATH refusal [#81 -- mapping through a navigation handle IS byte I/O by another name], the `dev->read` presence gate [report an errno now rather than a snare:bus at first touch], `image_lookup_or_create` [the SAME qid-keyed cache exec uses -- one copy of a library's text serves every container], `BURROW_ATTACH_MAX` length cap [4x TIGHTER than the lazy path's `BURROW_RESERVE_MAX`; task #191 owns the uncharged `filepages[]` allocation this arm sits beside and does NOT widen], `vma_find_gap` in `EXEC_USER_BURROW_BASE..TOP` under `as->lock`; the dispatch site tries the FILE arm first, an order made FREE by the pinned disjointness), `arch/arm64/fault.c` (**the #190 retirement**: `file_install_locked` + `file_install_cluster_locked` now VERIFY the post-sleep geometry -- `page_va` still inside the VMA AND `(vma->burrow_offset + (page_va - vaddr_start))/PAGE_SIZE == freq->slot` -- and BAIL otherwise. The R-5 F2 premise 'a FILE Burrow has ONE fixed VMA at burrow_offset 0' is RETIRED, not re-argued; F2's prescribed remedy [recompute the slot] is WRONG, since `freq->file_offset` came from the same pre-sleep geometry and the page already holds those bytes -- recomputing only the index files stale bytes under a fresh slot), **D-3b adds**: `kernel/vma.c` + `kernel/include/thylacine/vma.h` (NEW `vma_replace_range_in` -- the MAP_FIXED surgery, and the FIRST producer of a non-zero `vma->burrow_offset` in the tree [every other `vma_alloc` passes a literal 0 or copies one that can only be 0]. TWO admitted shapes: wholly-inside-one-VMA -> split, or an entirely FREE range -> plain insert. The free arm is #196: omitting it made an unmapped-address MAP_FIXED answer ENOMEM, a WORSE reply than the ENOSYS it replaced, because ENOMEM cannot be told from real memory pressure and an allocator reads it as OOM. NO HOLE ON ANY PATH -- the old Vma is REUSED as the surviving remainder [shrunk in place, never removed], so an insert failure restores a bound rather than having to put back a mapping it already tore out; only the exact-cover case removes, and its restoring re-insert is provably infallible [same lock hold, into the range just vacated, count strictly below its entry value]. Survivor BYTE IDENTITY -- `burrow_offset + (va - vaddr_start)` -- is invariant across the cut, which is ALSO what makes D-3a's #190 post-sleep geometry check correct against a concurrent split: it passes exactly when the pre-sleep bytes still belong at that slot. A flagged survivor [SHARED_IN = another Proc's memory carrying a per-span budget charge; COW = per-page share counts across the cut] is REFUSED rather than handled), `kernel/burrow.c` + `burrow.h` (`burrow_map_fixed{,_in}` -- owns the address arithmetic and the PTE teardown; the teardown comes FIRST because hardware resolves a leaf PTE without taking `as->lock`, so a thread already in EL0 could read the window through a stale PTE at any instant; a REFUSED surgery therefore costs one wasted teardown of an unmodified window, which is benign [every page refaults from unchanged backing] and is the deliberate price of keeping the VMA-shape rules in ONE place), `kernel/include/thylacine/vivarium.h` + `vivarium.c` (`vivarium_mmap_fixed_file_decide` + `vivarium_mmap_fixed_anon_decide` -- two MORE arms, each demanding EXACT equality against a DISTINCT flags word, so all four arms are pairwise disjoint and the dispatch order is free; W+X declines at the domain edge as well as at `vma_alloc`; PROT_NONE declines on BOTH fixed arms, DIVERGING from the non-fixed anon arm's documented degrade-to-writable because a FIXED PROT_NONE over an existing mapping is a GUARD and a writable page is a hole, not a degradation), `kernel/syscall.c` (`sys_mmap_fixed_file_for_proc` + `sys_mmap_fixed_anon_for_proc` + `mmap_fixed_window` + `mmap_eager_copy`. **The writable arm-2 request is served by an EAGER PRIVATE COPY, never a writable file mapping** -- musl WRITES into that mapping at `dynlink.c:849` [the partial-tail memset] before arm 3 runs, and a writable private FILE mapping would need COW over shared Image-cache pages where a bug leaks one container's writes into another's view of the same library. Eager copy is a conforming MAP_PRIVATE [POSIX and Linux both leave post-mmap file changes unspecified for private mappings] and is the FULLY CHARGED path -- `burrow_lazy_populate` takes the I-32 page charge for the whole run up front, so an over-cap request is refused whole rather than half-populated, unlike the read-only arm [#194]. A SHORT READ IS NOT AN ERROR: arm 2's length comes from `p_memsz` not `p_filesz`, so the window legitimately runs past the file's data and those pages must read as zero -- which the untouched KP_ZERO slots already are. `burrow_unref` runs OUTSIDE `as->lock` here, unlike the D-3a arm [#193]), `usr/viv-pheno-probe/src/main.rs` (L21 CHANGED CONTRACT: was 'MAP_FIXED returns ENOSYS', now asserts the mapping lands at EXACTLY the requested address -- a strictly stronger claim, since 'not an errno' would pass on any address the kernel felt like picking, the one thing MAP_FIXED forbids -- plus a new L21b write/read-back through the LAST page proving the whole span is real memory) | I-36 relaxes: 'kernel-internal, never a userspace writable file mapping' -> 'kernel-internal OR a read-only/exec phenotype mmap; writable file mappings stay banned'. I-12 (W^X): two independent gates -- the decide refuses PROT_WRITE, and `vma_alloc` refuses W+X. I-32: demand-paged FILE pages keep the R-5 uncharged-at-v1.0 posture (shared -- charging per-mapper would count one physical page N times); the VMA-count axis IS charged in `vma_insert`. Tests: `vivarium.mmap_file_domain`, `vivarium.mmap_arms_disjoint` (a cross-product sweep with a non-vacuity check on BOTH arms), `demand_page.file_geometry_shift_bails{,_single}` (one per install path; each revert-probed against its OWN check -- sabotaging one reddens one and not the other). D-3b adds `vivarium.mmap_fixed_domain` (each admitted case asserted as THE CALL STOCK LDSO MAKES; the R and R+X arm-2 cases are asserted HERE because NO producer for them exists on this rootfs -- all 18 ELFs are two-PT_LOAD `R-X`+`RW-`, so the in-guest gate cannot reach them and no gate coverage is claimed), the 4-arm `vivarium.mmap_arms_disjoint` sweep (now with a PER-ARM non-vacuity guard, not a total -- a total is satisfied by one arm admitting everything while another admits nothing), and eight `burrow.map_fixed_*` cases (split_left / split_right / split_three_way / exact_cover / carries_offset / refusals / successive / into_free_space), each asserting survivor BYTE IDENTITY rather than merely bounds -- a split with right bounds and a wrong offset yields a VMA that looks perfect and serves the wrong page. Revert-probed with TWO sabotages reddening DIFFERENT legs: dropping the right-remainder offset advance reddens ONLY `split_right` (the three-way case computes its right piece through a different `vma_alloc` call, so its passing is correct rather than blind); reverting the free-space arm to a refusal reddens ONLY `into_free_space` while all seven split cases pass. **NOTE the split tests were STRUCTURALLY BLIND to #196** -- each attaches a mapping first, so none could reach the no-covering-VMA arm; the in-guest phenotype probe caught it, which is why `into_free_space` exists |
| DISTRO D-4: the PT_INTERP rewrite to the interpreter (exec dispatch) | `kernel/exec.c` (NEW `exec_interp_argv` -- the rebuilt vector `[interp, "--argv0", orig_argv0, "--", orig_path, orig argv[1..]]`, `argc + 4` slots, bounds-refusing and packing-validating rather than truncating; the rewrite block inside `exec_load_into` immediately after the first `elf_load`, gated on `nsp->phenotype == PHENO_LINUX`; `exec_load_into` SPLIT into a thin wrapper + `exec_load_body` so the two things the rewrite allocates -- the interpreter's pinned Spoor and the rebuilt argv block -- have exactly ONE disposal site covering all dozen early returns, out-param-reported [the D-3c F1/F5 lesson applied ahead of time]; `exec_load_into`/`exec_setup_from_spoor` gained `nsp` + `prog_name`/`prog_name_len`), `kernel/elf.c` (NEW `elf_read_interp` -- the bounded PT_INTERP walk, now SHARED: `elf_brand_hint` is its caller, so the 'inside the prefix / terminated' judgement has one home; every absent case returns 0 and clears the buffer, NEVER a truncated path), `kernel/include/thylacine/elf.h` (`ELF_INTERP_MAX` 255), `kernel/include/thylacine/exec.h` (`EXEC_ARGV_MAX` / `EXEC_ARGV_DATA_MAX` -- the argv mirror LIFTED out of two bare literals in `exec_build_init_stack` and now `_Static_assert`ed against `SYS_SPAWN_ARGV_MAX` / `SYS_SPAWN_ARGV_DATA_MAX` in `syscall.c`, which pays down a slice of #178 instead of adding to it), `kernel/syscall.c` (`struct spawn_full_argv_args` gains an inline `name[]` + `name_len`; the thunk copies before its `kfree(sa)`; the two native-only spawn thunks pass `prog_name = NULL`; `sys_execve_core` passes its own `path`), `tools/build.sh` + `usr/joey/joey.c` (the boot-fatal `D4-A-byname-getconf-4096` + `D4-B-argv0-is-the-program` legs) | **Audit-noted, joins the exec/REVENANT row** (DISTRO section 7.1): resolution runs under I-28 through the SAME `OEXEC`-gated `exec_resolve_from_namespace` the program came through, and the loaded image is the interpreter -- everything downstream is D-2's already-audited single-image path. **NO new invariant.** **I-33 is load-bearing here and was the design constraint**: `orig_path` is threaded from the callers as `prog_name`, NOT read from `exe->path`, because the Spoor's `Path` is cosmetic and no syscall result may turn on it -- an exec that failed because a path-alloc OOM'd would be the invariant's own counterexample. Prosecute: the `exec_load_body` out-param disposal contract on EVERY early return (the interpreter Spoor leaks or double-frees if one path is missed -- and the caller still owns the ORIGINAL `exe`, so the two must never alias); the one-level rule (straight-line, not a loop -- an interpreter carrying its own PT_INTERP must reach the unchanged refusal); `exec_interp_argv`'s NUL-count-vs-argc identity (`exec_build_init_stack` EXTINCTS on a mismatch, so an off-by-one is a DEAD KERNEL, not a failed exec) and its behaviour at both ceilings and at `argc == 0`; `elf_read_interp`'s absent-not-truncated bar (a truncated `/lib/ld-musl-aarch64.so.1` is `/lib/ld`, a DIFFERENT file the kernel would then exec); the `prog_name == NULL` + PHENO_LINUX arm (fail-loud, unreachable today); that `nsp` is never WRITTEN through. **Fidelity ledger re-measured** (DISTRO 3.2): `argv[0]` HOLDS via `--argv0`; `/proc/self/exe` CLOSES (the rewrite is below the Proc-side stamps, which still hold the original `exe`); `argc == 0` becomes 1 and a mode-0111 dynamic binary is refused, both inherent to a userspace loader. Validation: `exec.interp_argv_shape` (byte-for-byte, incl. the DISCRIMINATING argv0-differs-from-path case) + `elf.read_interp` + the two boot-fatal gate legs + **three-way discrimination measured 2026-08-10**: control green both layers; S1 (rewrite disabled) reddens exactly `D4-A` while `D3-A` stays green and the unit suite is FULLY BLIND (1389/1389 through it); S2 (`argv[0] := path`, the vote's literal shape) is FULLY GREEN through the gate (GATE PASS) while the unit test reddens on exactly its own leg -- the two layers are complementary and neither alone is sufficient. |
| The executable-mapping vouching gate: `Dev.may_back_exec` (the floor) + per-mount `MNOEXEC` (the refinement) (#217; I-12 PROVENANCE half) | `kernel/include/thylacine/dev.h` (`Dev.may_back_exec` -- the ALLOWLIST floor, set true by `devramfs` + `dev9p` ONLY), `kernel/include/thylacine/territory.h` (`MNOEXEC` 0x0010 -- a bit in the EXISTING `PgrpMount.flags`, so `struct PgrpMount` stays 40 bytes and its `_Static_assert` is untouched; the granularity rationale lives on the `#define`), `kernel/territory.c` (NEW `mount_noexec_covers(territory, dc, devno)` -- the SOURCE-side twin of `mount_is_point_id`, same `ns_lock` discipline; plus the ` noexec` suffix in `territory_format_ns`), `kernel/syscall.c` (NEW static `exec_map_vouched(p, sp)` -- the ONE place the rule is spelled; `SYS_MOUNT_VALID_FLAGS` widened to admit the bit; the gate at **THREE** call sites: `sys_mmap_file_for_proc` [exec arm], `sys_mmap_fixed_file_for_proc` [sited on `prot` BEFORE the writable/demand-paged split so a future arm inherits it], and `exec_resolve_from_namespace` [on the RESOLVED quarry, after every mount cross]), `usr/lib/libthyla-rs/src/lib.rs` + `territory.rs` (`T_MNOEXEC` + `MountFlags::NOEXEC`), `usr/viv/src/main.rs` (`T_MREPL \| T_MNOEXEC` on EVERY mount viv makes -- the diorama at `/dio` and all recipe mounts; plus the corrected `root.readonly` comment) | **Realizes the I-12 PROVENANCE half + narrows ARCH 6.5 arm (b); NO new invariant number.** W^X bounds what a PAGE may be at one instant and says NOTHING about where its bytes came from, so a file that is writable now and mapped executable later never violates it -- #217 adds the missing half. **Prosecute on any change:** (1) the check must stay OUT of `image_lookup_or_create` -- that cache is keyed on `(spoor, offset, length, exec)` and NOT on the Territory, so a hit seeded by a permitted Proc would hand the executable Burrow to a forbidden one; the verdict is per-namespace and the cache is global, so the gate belongs strictly BEFORE the cache, in each caller. (2) A FOURTH exec-mapping site is a NEW site, not a variant -- the census that found the three is `grep VMA_PROT_EXEC kernel/*.c` and it must be re-run, never assumed. (3) `SYS_MOUNT_VALID_FLAGS` is an allowlist: a flag missing from it is SILENTLY UNUSABLE (the mount just fails -1 with nothing naming the cause) -- this bit it during development and `sys_mount.rejects_invalid_flags` is what caught it. (4) The granularity is per DEVICE INSTANCE `(dc, devno)`, chosen because `spoor_clone` propagates `devno` so nothing has to REMEMBER to carry a flag and no Dev can silently forget; the price is that one instance mounted twice cannot carry two verdicts. (5) A NULL Territory answers false (fail-OPEN) DELIBERATELY, but the arm is **DEFENSIVE AND UNREACHABLE**, not load-bearing: `kproc` gets `kpgrp()` at `proc_init`, `exec_resolve_from_namespace` returns on `territory_root_ref(NULL)` BEFORE the gate, and the mmap path is PHENO_LINUX-only where every Proc carries a cloned Territory. **This point previously read "kernel boot exec runs in that state; do not 'harden' it to true without tracing boot" -- FALSE, and it survived the very commit that corrected the same sentence in `territory.h` (#217 round-2 F1).** Recorded rather than silently patched, because it is this row's own #123 lesson landing on this row: the fix's commit message claimed the claim was corrected at every home, and the grep missed the binding prosecution reference and `docs/reference/18-territory.md`. When you correct a claim, enumerate its homes by grepping the SENTENCE, then re-grep after the edit to prove zero remain. (6) **THE FLAG MUST STAY UNREACHABLE BY THE CONFINED PROC. It is NOT "unsheddable" in itself -- an earlier version of this point said exactly that, and it was an overstatement of the same family as F1 (was #221; CLOSED 2026-08-13 -- user signoff was DOCUMENT-NOW, and the disposition plus the lock's trigger and shape now live in `docs/DISTRO.md` section 6. No code owed: the lock waits for the first confined NATIVE Proc that owns a namespace, and no such caller exists).** A per-mount restriction is worthless if the restricted process can re-mount without it -- but the thing that makes that true here is REACHABILITY, not durability. For a NATIVE Proc the flag is freely sheddable and always was: `SYS_MOUNT` and `SYS_UNMOUNT` carry NO capability gate whatsoever (`sys_mount_for_proc` checks only `RIGHT_READ` on the SOURCE handle; `sys_unmount_for_proc` checks nothing past resolution), which is Plan 9's model and correct there. So three ungated sheds exist: `unmount` the entry, `MREPL` over it (that arm overwrites `flags` wholesale), or re-mount the pair without the bit (the #219 converge -- which ADDS no authority, since the first two already sufficed). `territory_mount.noexec_covers` asserts the unmount shed AS INTENDED ("authority conferred by a namespace edit is revoked by the inverse edit"), so this is a deliberate semantic, not a defect. What confines a vivarium container is therefore THE PHENOTYPE GATE, not the flag's durability -- which is what the measurement below actually establishes, and why its conclusion is the load-bearing sentence in this point. Measured 2026-08-11: no vivarium row reaches `SYS_MOUNT` / `SYS_UNMOUNT` / `SYS_PIVOT_ROOT` / `SYS_CHROOT` (the only occurrence in `vivarium.c` is a comment), and `viv_linux_dispatch`'s `default:` arm is FAIL-CLOSED (`viv_report_unserved` + `-T_E_NOSYS` + `return false`) rather than falling through to the native switch -- so a PHENO_LINUX Proc reaches native syscalls ONLY via an explicit T1 renumber row, and none targets a namespace mutator. **Adding any mount/unmount/pivot_root/chroot row to the phenotype table re-opens #192 by itself**, independently of anything in this row's own code, because the container could then drop the flag it was confined by. Lock ordering, also measured: `mount_noexec_covers` takes `ns_lock` as a leaf with NO lock held at any of the three call sites (`as->lock` is taken strictly after both mmap gates), so the gate introduces no new lock-order edge. **What this does NOT close, and must not be claimed to:** a container's root arrives by `chroot`, not by mount, so no mount flag describes it -- and it is the one tree the container must exec FROM, so noexec there is both unrepresentable and unwanted. A bundle that OWNS its executable tree can still author its own code; it can equally overwrite one of its own 0755 binaries and `execve` it (`VIV_LINUX_EXECVE` is a served tier-2 row), so that path crosses no boundary. Making the root unwritable is a SERVER or bundle-STAGING mechanism (the Plan 9 read-only-export idiom, as `usr/diorama` already does by refusing Twrite), not a mount flag's job. Reachability bound, measured: `sys_mmap_file_for_proc` has EXACTLY ONE caller (`VIV_LINUX_MMAP`), so the R+X arm is PHENO_LINUX-only and CAP_JIT is not bypassable this way outside a vivarium. Validation: `territory_mount.noexec_covers` (the predicate, with controls on BOTH key axes + clone-inherit + unmount-revokes), `exec_ns.noexec_mount_denied` (the exec site; mutates kproc's LIVE Territory, so it restores the namespace BEFORE asserting -- a failed assert must not leave the boot's own exec path mounted noexec), `mmap_file.noexec_mount_denied` (both mmap sites + a non-exec control proving noexec bounds execute and not read; MEASURES FIRST and ASSERTS LAST, because `TEST_ASSERT` returns and inline asserts stranded Image-cache entries that turned one red test into SEVEN), `sys_mount.rejects_invalid_flags` (the allowlist, re-pointed to `0x20` + a valid-ORed-with-junk case + the positive MNOEXEC accept), `territory_mount.format_ns` (the suffix renders on the flagged entry ONLY), `territory_mount.idempotent_converges_flags` (**#219**, landed 2026-08-12 -- the idempotency arm used to return 0 having never read `flags`, so a re-mount ADDING `MNOEXEC` reported success and left the pair unrestricted; it now converges, symmetrically, and BOTH directions are proven discriminating by A/B: deleting the converge reddens only the TIGHTEN assert, making it monotone (`flags \|= flags`) reddens only the LOOSEN assert, each sabotage failing exactly one test at 1393/1394). **FIVE sabotages, five distinct attributions** (2026-08-11): predicate-always-true reddens ONLY the control; predicate-always-false reddens ONLY the deny; each of the three call-site gates removed independently reddens ONLY its own test while the other two stay green. Gate evidence: both arc gates PASS with noexec live on every viv mount (Alpine 3.21.0 still forks/execs/pipes its own busybox), and the unserved-syscall set is unchanged (nr=134/214, both pre-existing) -- so the flag costs a working container nothing. **PROVEN IN-GUEST since #218** (2026-08-12), closing the last link: `viv-pheno-probe` legs L200-L204 witness a LIVE container's mounts from inside it. The pair is chosen so ONLY THE MOUNT FLAG DIFFERS -- `/bin/viv-pheno-probe` (dev9p, `may_back_exec`, arrived by CHROOT) maps `PROT_READ \| PROT_EXEC` fine, while `/proc/meminfo` (dev9p, `may_back_exec`, on the MNOEXEC diorama instance) is REFUSED; a non-exec control proves the same fd maps cleanly without `PROT_EXEC`, so the deny cannot be satisfied by a bad fd, an unmappable file or a broken mount. **`/env` and the `/dev` leaves CANNOT witness this at all** -- the `may_back_exec` floor refuses them BEFORE the flag is consulted (F1 above), so picking a target the floor does NOT cover is the entire point of the pair, and a green on `/env` would prove nothing about MNOEXEC. **DISCRIMINATION PROVEN BY A/B**: dropping `T_MNOEXEC` from viv turns the boot red at exactly `V-1b linux-phenotype leg FAILED marker=L204`, with the kernel suite still 1394/1394 and the native V-7 leg still PASS, so the failure isolates to the new leg alone; restoring it returns `Thylacine boot OK`. **BOTH viv call sites must be sabotaged to see the flip** -- `mount_noexec_covers` matches the mount SOURCE's `(dc, devno)` and `/dio` + `/proc` are the SAME diorama 9P instance, so dropping only the `/proc` bind leaves the `/dio` entry still covering the device and the experiment reports "no discrimination" while having sabotaged nothing (a sabotage that quietly passes). The asserted errno is a flat `-1`, which is correct HERE and must not be "fixed" to `T_E_ACCES` on the strength of errno.h's standing "DO NOT RETURN `-T_E_PERM`" warning: that warning is about the pouch boundary line's `-1 -> EIO` sentinel, and `sys_mmap_file_for_proc` has exactly one caller (the vivarium dispatch), so this value only ever reaches a PHENO_LINUX guest for whom `-1` IS `-EPERM` by Linux's own numbering. Because the probe is POOL-RESIDENT, a run under `THYLACINE_MKFS_PRESERVE=1` executes the STALE binary and these legs silently do not exist (#126) -- re-bake the pool when changing them, and confirm from the build log's `viv bundles baked at /vivarium` rather than from a green boot. **ROUND 1 (Fable 5, `MODEL(start) == MODEL(end)`, no fallback): 0 P0 / 1 P1 / 1 P2 / 3 P3, all fixed. THE P1 FALSIFIED THIS ROW'S OWN HEADLINE CLAIM and is the standing lesson here.** **F1 [P1]**: `MNOEXEC` is STRUCTURALLY INCAPABLE of covering `/env` -- the very surface the scripture named as its reason for existing. The flag is consulted through the `(dc, devno)` a file shares with its mount source, which assumes devno names the mounted INSTANCE; `devenv_walk` instead stamps the CALLING Proc's env devno (an I-1 fix -- per-Env ids restart at 1, so without it two Procs' variables collide in the REVENANT Image cache). A container's `/env` files thus never match the `/env` mount source viv installed, no entry matches, and the R+X map was ADMITTED. Fixed by `Dev.may_back_exec`, an ALLOWLIST floor beneath the flag: only `devramfs` + `dev9p` (the Devs serving real file content) may back executable pages; every other Dev is refused mounted or not. Allowlist not denylist, so a later Dev is refused until it opts in. **F2 [P2]**: the three mount-flag tests were STRUCTURALLY BLIND to F1 because `noexec_source_for()` FORGES the `(dc, devno)` match that devenv cannot produce -- they prove "IF a file shares its source's identity the gate fires" and never prove the antecedent. Fixed by `mmap_file.devenv_never_exec_backs`, which manufactures nothing (a REAL entry via devenv's own attach+walk into the real syscall body) and REDDENS on the pre-fix kernel at 1392/1393, isolating exactly itself. **F3 [P3]**: `spoor_next_devno`'s comment said the mount key is "not a security boundary ... not a capability" -- #217 made it exactly that; corrected (the wrap stays benign, but because a collision only ADDS coverage, not because devno is non-security). **F4 [P3]**: #219's note under-recorded that the idempotency miss is fail-OPEN. (**#219 FIXED 2026-08-12** -- and F4's insistence on naming the DIRECTION is what made the fix choice legible: the arm converges rather than refusing, because refusing would have bought no security next to two ungated sheds while inviting the belief that a mount flag is a lock. See point (6) and #221.) **F5 [P3]**: `reference/18-territory.md` still said `sizeof(PgrpMount) == 32`; it is 40. **PROSECUTE ON ANY CHANGE, added by the round:** (7) a new Dev DEFAULTS to non-exec-backing and must opt in -- never add `.may_back_exec = true` without asking whether that Dev's "files" are real file content; (8) **never re-derive `/env` (or any per-Proc-devno device) coverage from the `(dc, devno)` key** -- that inference is what F1 was; (9) the mount-flag tests' forged identity means they can NEVER witness a per-Proc-devno device, so a Dev-level claim needs a Dev-level test. **THE STANDING LESSON:** this row asserted a door was shut while it stood open, and the chunk's own five-sabotage matrix stayed green because every sabotage sat downstream of a manufactured identity. A test battery can be rigorous about the wrong antecedent. |

the fenced lane -- slots, globally-monotone fence ids, error-response-retires-fence, refuse-not-block; the G-5 wait discipline with the sync-only deadline), `usr/tapestryd/src/server.rs` (the warp tree: `Conn.root` qid-space split, one-ctx-per-conn + `owner_conn` at every resolve [I-45], `wbo_create` reverse unwind, `wbo_retire`'s unshare-then-detach-then-close order + leak-on-wedge, the **deferred-retire machine** [`retiring` + `warp_pump_retires`, R1-F5 -- never block the serve loop inside a 9P dispatch], the **poison/vindication state machine** [fenced slot -> ctx -> ctx SLOT, and the vindication that walks it back], the per-ctx `WARP_CTX_BACKING_MAX` cap + `leaked_bytes`, the `PendingFence` park machinery + its 4 cancel sites), `usr/tapestryd/src/main.rs` (the fence pump + the 1 ms poll clamp + the per-root listener arming), `usr/warp-prove` (the gate prover). The vindication mechanism specifically produced a finding in rounds 2, 3 AND 4; the release profile is `overflow-checks = true` + `panic = "abort"`, so any client-reachable arithmetic overflow here KILLS THE CONSOLE.** **The test-mode determinism surface is IN SCOPE, not test scaffolding** (`warp-hold on/off`, `warp-abandon`, and the read-only `fenced-free` / `fences-in-flight` / `fence-signaled` ctl fields): it SHIPS (`default = ["test-mode"]`) on a mode-0666 ctl, so every lever must be bounded to the CALLER'S OWN ctx -- #178 was exactly this, global levers handing any unprivileged client a permanent box-wide wedge of the fenced lane, and round-8 F1 was a hold that was scoped in effect but GLOBAL IN STORAGE. Prosecute each lever for displacement, foreign release, and cross-client blast radius; and prosecute each OBSERVABLE for whether a regression could satisfy it vacuously -- #184 was a leg asserting a gauge reads 0, which "nothing was ever queued" satisfies as well as "the fence retired". the #215 fix):** `gpu.rs` `FENCED_SLOTS` 4 -> 16 (per-ctx share `WARP_CTX_FENCE_MAX` 2 -> 8) with the controlq widened 16 -> 64 (its QEMU device max; ring offsets repacked inside the same 2-page DMA region, every region bound re-asserted) and the cursorq SPLIT to its own `CURSORQ_SIZE=16` (16 IS its QEMU max -- writing 64 there fails negotiation; `setup_queue` takes the size per queue); `FREQ_LEN` shrinks 64KiB -> 36KiB in the same change (the lane is ONE plain SYS_DMA_CREATE and 16x64KiB+page overshot the kernel's 1 MiB `KOBJ_DMA_MAX_SIZE` by one page -- the alloc failed and the warden restart-looped a console-less tapestryd; ONLY the GL-host capset gate can see flane sizing, a 2D boot never allocates it; compile asserts now pin `FLANE_DMA_SIZE <= 1 MiB` and `FREQ_LEN >= 32KiB+hdr`, and 36KiB swallows any single-Twrite submission, `fenced_begin` refusing larger cleanly), so `FLANE_DMA_SIZE` = 16x36KiB+page = 580 KiB (virgl-negotiated boots only). `server.rs` `MAX_WARP_BOS_PER_CTX` 128 -> 1024 (#213) with BOTH per-ctx BO rows moved to HEAP (`bos` a boxed slice built by `warp_bo_row()` [try_reserve -> mint fails clean]; the leak graveyard `[Vec<WarpBo>; 8]` try_reserve'd to the FULL cap at ctx mint so `warp_park_leaked`'s push NEVER allocates -- the round-6 F1 no-record-dropped argument now rests on that reserve; prosecute any path that could shrink/steal the capacity while a slot is poisoned, and the mint-order [rows BEFORE `gpu.ctx_create`, nothing to unwind]); the #204 census (`bo_backed_peak` per ctx + `Comp.warp_bo_peak` global, per-ctx ctl `bo-live`/`bo-peak`, global ctl `bo-peak`) and the global ctl `fence-lane` advertisement. Witnesses: the wedge-gate (both legs progress), the warp-prove battery (churn bound now 1024+1 via ctl discovery), `bo-peak` read after a quake run, the #215 re-measure vs the GPU-DESIGN section 13 bar. **#218 addendum (unmint-on-refused-create3d; the #198 second stage):** a create3d that does not return OK CONSUMES the mint record — hooked at the ctl arm (ONE chokepoint, so the parse/OPNOTSUPP arms that never reach `wbo_create` consume it too), implemented by `wbo_unmint_refused` bounded three ways (owner-conn [I-45], UNBUILT `dma_fd < 0`, non-retiring) so no error path can unmint a live backing, cross a client, or race the deferred-retire pump; `wbo_create`'s contract note says record disposal is the CALLER's — prosecute any NEW caller for honoring it, any new refusal family for corpse-leaking, and any change to the guards for what can RESET/BYPASS/OUTLIVE them (an unbacked record carries no bytes/census/graveyard charge, so removal must stay accounting-free); the `!built` write-back arm now unwinds device state (unreachable single-threaded — prosecute if dispatch ever grows concurrency). Witness: the warp-prove corpse-reclaim leg (cap+1 refusals PER family read from ctl `bo-cap`, mint-survival the discriminator, the built-bo repeat-refusal guard leg; A/B-proven — pre-fix fails at exactly `size-align attempt 1025`). Known battery caveat (tracked): the poisoned-churn R6-F1 discriminator currently fires via the `dma-create` handle-pressure arm (~237), NOT the count-cap it was built to prove. **#198 addendum (the FOUR-TABLE fid ceiling chain; `afa90839` + `61566af1`):** a GL client holds one 9P fid per live BO mapping (the map fid's clunk is what drops the mapping, I-7), so a Quake-class texture set walks into FOUR nested static tables in series -- tapestryd's per-conn `MAX_FIDS` (32 -> 512), the kernel's `P9_SESSION_MAX_FIDS` (256 -> 1024), and `PROC_HANDLE_MAX` (256 -> 1024) on BOTH glq AND tapestryd itself. Lifting one moved the binder to the next: stock 2323 OOMs at 0.6 fps -> handles-only byte-identical (the exoneration) -> tapestryd-512 alone 1059 OOMs (moved, not fixed) -> all four 0 OOMs / 0 storm / 44.7 fps, a 75x end-to-end. **The method is the durable part, and it generalizes to every ceiling hunt on this surface: the kernel's session-fid refusal (`9p_session.c` `fid_alloc`) was INVISIBLE TO BOTH INSTRUMENTED ENDPOINTS** -- the client saw a generic failure, the server never saw a T-message, and three rounds of theorizing at either end (admission E_AGAIN, silent server arms, write-never-arrived) all died because the refusal sat in the LAYER BETWEEN them. Enumerate every layer a request crosses and instrument each one BEFORE theorizing at either end; a refusal below both probes is not a mystery, it is an un-probed layer. The `create-refused` / `create-refused-noctx` / `diag-arms` / `diag-noctx-arms` ctl fields exist for exactly this -- a refusal counted in the NOCTX twin but not the per-ctx one localizes the chokepoint ABOVE the ctx. Prosecute on any further lift: `MAX_FIDS` is per-CONN (a client that leaks fids costs only its own conn, which is why 512 is affordable where a global would not be), and the `fid_full_diag_once` latch must be RE-ARMED by `drop_all_fids` or a second exhaustion on a reused conn is silent (round F5). |
